# Build Log

A dated log of conventions, decisions, and gotchas established while building this codebase. This is reference material, not required reading — consult it when working in an area it covers (the entry dates and subject lines are there so you can scan for relevance), and append to it, not to `CLAUDE.md`, when you establish something a future session needs.

This file used to be CLAUDE.md §11. It moved out because it had grown too large to reread at the start of every session — CLAUDE.md now only points here.

---

**2026-08-06 — Slice 0021 Part G, the world reset, was run.** Reported by the operator in conversation. No code changed; this records a state change a future session cannot see from the repository.

- **Slice 0021 is now complete.** Parts A-F shipped 2026-08-04; Part G was always the operator's own step — a full `world`-scope reset from `/admin`, per that slice's §8 — because until it ran, no existing horse had a `head_profile` genotype or any of the ten new colour/pattern loci. Every horse in the game now carries all thirteen colour/pattern loci and the fifth conformation trait, and the children have new founding stock.
- **The reset is spent, and that changes the plan for `docs/fixes/quality-band-on-target.md`.** That document had proposed landing alongside this reset so one reset covered both; it must now be planned as a change landing into a live population. Its §9 is rewritten accordingly and recommends landing the fix and then granting every child a fresh founding batch — no second reset, nothing lost, and no new code, since the batch-grant mechanism already exists. **Grant those batches after the migration, not before**: `import_offers.polygenic_one_chance` is a snapshot column, so a batch granted early generates under the old rules.
- **2026-08-06, same day — the quality-band defect confirmed against the live population, plus two findings no simulation would have produced.** The operator exported all 107 horses. Recorded in `docs/fixes/quality-band-on-target.md` §3.1 and §7.1; the parts a future session needs:

- **The band's effect measured directly, without guessing anything.** The band raises a horse's allele count and does nothing else, so correlating allele count against the judge's score tests it cleanly. Quarter Horse **−0.30**, Paso Fino −0.07, German Warmblood **+0.77** — precisely tracking each breed's weighted-average target (50.1 / 52.0 / 64.6). One dial, three meanings, none of them the one on the label. This is the measurement to re-run after the fix; it should read near +1.0 for every breed.
- **Predictions held for unselected stock**: NPC breeding stables 2.15 traits right, consignment 2.21, against a predicted 2.1–2.2. And **slice 0019's specialist is working perfectly — 0 of 101 generated horses have zero traits right.**
- **Players hold better horses than the generator makes, and it is pure selection.** Player stables average 2.63 traits right against NPC stables' 2.15, while carrying an identical allele count (50.6 vs 50.4). The cause is the founding grant showing four mares and keeping two. **Anything measuring player-versus-NPC quality must model the claim step**, or it understates player stock by about half a trait — `docs/analysis/training-effect.mjs` does not, and says so.
- **The finding above is easy to state in a way that is wrong, and the operator was misled by it once already.** *"The band delivers 28% instead of 50%"* sounds like the children's horses are bad. They are not: measured on the operator's own sixteen Paso Finos, player horses read **2.58 of 5 on target (52%)** — essentially the intended 50% — and **1.89 Outstanding per horse** on the Conformation card, one mare showing 4 of 5. **"Outstanding" and "on target" are the same bar** (`traitScore >= 90` at falloff 2.0 is within 5 of target; one allele is worth 5); this document measures the genotype and the card measures the expressed value after birth noise, which is the whole difference. What is broken is that the band is not what gets a player there — the specialist contributes 1.0, the player's own choosing ~0.5, and the band only ~1.1, and it would contribute roughly that much at any setting. **The damage lands on the NPC field, which gets the specialist but no choosing, and hardest on the show barn's champions, who gain nothing from `high`.** Full reconciliation in the fix document's §3.2; read it before quoting §2 to anyone.
- **Home-bred foals start behind founding stock** — 1.83 traits right against 2.32. Expected rather than broken (the specialist is a gift to generated horses; a foal inherits genes, not the guarantee) but it means a child's first crop is worse than the horses they were given. Six foals proves nothing; **watch it as the population grows**, and note it argues for minting founding stock at the `low` band rather than `mid`, since a higher founding band widens the gap.

**Worth keeping in proportion.** The gap the fix opens between an existing horse and a new one is about four score points (Paso Fino: 72 today, 76 at the `low` band after), against a show-noise SD of 5. An existing horse is less than one bad day from the judge behind a new one. A future session should not describe this to the operator as their children's barns being obsoleted, because it is not that.

**2026-08-06 — Training reviewed and specified (`docs/slices/0027-training.md`). Nothing built.** A spec session, not a build one. Training had been referenced by ten documents, two engine signatures and a promised `show_entries` column without ever being designed; this collects the scattered commitments and resolves them. Four things a future session should know without reading the whole slice:

- **Training was missing from the build order entirely** (overview §13), between Market and Tack. Added there. It was in `docs/horse-game-schema.md` §4.6 and §6.4, in slice 0012 §2.4's "the column arrives with the training slice", in slice 0009's turn-cost list, in `ACTION_COSTS`' own comment, and in both scorers' signatures — but in no list of things to build. Worth a glance at the other §13 entries for the same failure.
- **`realization()`'s `trainingFactor`/`careFactor` are to be removed, not wired.** Slice 0006 carried them for the care and training stages to fill in; care decided against it (slice 0013 §3.4 — a modifier that moves displayed conformation numbers double-counts, since realization already feeds the raw score the modifier then multiplies again) and training now decides the same, for that reason plus a sharper one: `realization()` is shared by `conformationValues` **and** `abilityValues`, so wiring training there would raise the ability values that `judgeOneClass` bands into the permanent word in `horse_ability_words` — silently breaking the "training never touches an ability test" decision through a side door nothing in the ability-test code would show. Both stages have now declined; a defaulted parameter nobody sets is a trap for whoever finds it next.
- **The class → programme mapping is one function, `trainingProgrammeForClass`**, returning `null` for `ability_test`. That single return is the whole enforcement of the ability-test exclusion — the alternative is every scoring and display site remembering a filter, which is exactly the shape slice 0022 had to delete six copies of (`category !== 'acquired'`).
- **Slice 0020's workload proxy is finally replaced.** The 2026-08-04 entry below flagged show-entry count in a trailing window as a stand-in "due for replacement once a real training system exists"; §5.1 of the slice replaces it with show entries plus real training load, which is also what stops training being pure upside — a horse in hard work is a horse likelier to pull a suspensory.

**Measured before building, in `docs/analysis/training-effect.mjs`** (the third inhabitant of that directory, same standing rules as the other two — own PRNG, own copy of the constants, that copy can drift). Headline: one completed programme with the session bonus is worth +2.4 raw points, a 63% head-to-head win rate, and it takes a player horse in a field of eight from 8.3% wins to 12.4% — exactly the 12.5% a fair field gives, which is what justifies `npc_training_level = 2`. Breeding still dominates: one trait brought onto its breed's target is worth 3.9 points against the whole training ladder's 4.4, and a fully Polished founding horse still loses 81% of the time to a fully bred untrained one.

**That side finding turned out to be a real defect, now specified in `docs/fixes/quality-band-on-target.md`.** The operator's own statement of intent settled it: *"mid was supposed to mean that a horse was on target for a conformation trait 50% of the time."* It never has. Read that document before touching `generateCandidate`; the one-line version is that a Paso Fino needs 14 of its 20 neck alleles and 6 of its 20 back alleles, and `polygenicOneChance` is a single dial applied to both — so raising it to fix the neck (13% → 31% on target) destroys the back (13% → 3%). The visible symptom nobody had connected to it: the show barn mints champions at `high` and novices at `mid`, so its champion field has never actually been better than its novice field. Decided by the operator, 2026-08-06: keep slice 0019's guaranteed specialist, bands become 0.25/0.50/0.75, founding stock drops to `low` to preserve breeding headroom. The original observation is kept below because it is the reasoning the fix rests on.

**The observation, as first recorded: the founding quality band barely moves a conformation score, and for three of the eight breeds the *mid* band is the best one.** This falls straight out of slice 0006 §2.2 and nothing was broken — a conformation trait is a bidirectional measurement against an intermediate target, so a band raises a horse towards targets above 50 and away from targets below it. A band's mean genetic value is `100 × polygenic_one_chance`; the band that suits a breed is the one landing on that breed's *weight-averaged* target. Measured (scenario 6): Quarter Horse 50.1, Paso Fino 52.0 and Norwegian Fjord 48.6 are all best served by the mid band, Arabian (46.0) and Icelandic (40.4) by the low band, and Thoroughbred (62.5), German Warmblood (64.6) and Friesian (66.1) by the high one. **So "high quality band" is not a synonym for "better horse"** anywhere except the ability traits, which are `higher_better` with no target at all. This is why slice 0019's founding specialists exist and why they work by moving traits onto target rather than by raising the band. Before reading a band as a quality knob — the consignment dealer's mid band, the show barn's rank plan, `/admin/npc`'s mint control — check it against the breed's own weighted-average target. Nothing was changed in response; recorded because a future session will otherwise assume high means good.

Four decisions were taken by the operator before the document was written and are recorded in its §1: training counts in every class type except ability tests; it is a programme *with* optional sessions inside it; money and one turn to start, sessions free; NPC horses carry a fixed middling level from config. The session cap is one per tick per horse, deliberately — overview §6c asks that this game not be built to reward compulsive re-checking, and a cap tied to the tick means checking back sooner gains nothing at all.

**2026-08-06 — A show's own page gets class-type tabs, and its parallel classes group instead of interleaving.** Operator report: opening a judged show to read the results shows every class in one list in mint order, and since the 2026-08-06 walkback (migration `0175`, parallel classes minted once a stable hits its per-class cap) a household entering four-plus horses at a time produces two and three copies of the same class — so the copies scatter through the list and the class you actually care about is impossible to find. Asked for tabs by class type, or a dropdown picker.

- **The tabs already half-existed and were never drawn.** `showRoute` has called `resolveShowsFilter` since slice 0016 §5.2 and passes the filter into its class loading, so `/shows/:id?class=dressage` has always worked — the page just rendered no control to set it, and every link into the page (`/shows`' own "View and enter" and recent-results links) dropped the filter on the floor. This is the third "handler with no way to reach it" in this codebase (the `/treat` 404, the same-account buy form); the fix is one screen's worth of markup, not a mechanism.
- **`src/lib/showsFilter.ts`** is the write side of that filter — `showsFilterQuery`/`showsIndexUrl`/`showPageUrl`, the counterpart to `resolveShowsFilter`'s read side. It drops `breed` on any tab that doesn't read one (a discipline or young-horse tab), so a stale breed can't ride along invisibly and reappear when the player clicks back to Conformation. Deliberately a plain structural type rather than importing `ShowsFilterParams` from `db/shows`, so nothing in `lib/` depends on the database layer.
- **The POST redirect goes back to its own tab** (`showPageUrl(showId, filter, { entered: '1' })`) — slice 0026 §2.2's rule for the horse page's tabs, and it applies here for the identical reason: a redirect to the bare page lands the player on "All" with their "Entered." notice behind a tab they aren't looking at, which is indistinguishable from the entry having failed. The entry form's own `action` carries the filter too, so a refusal re-renders on the tab it was made from.
- **Tabs are built from the show's own classes, not the fixed list.** `buildShowPageTabs` (`src/render/shows.ts`) draws All plus one tab per class type actually present, counted (`Conformation (7)`), disciplines ordered by name — the operator's "tabs by each type part of it". The `/shows` index keeps its fixed bar (every enabled discipline, whether or not anything is scheduled in it), since that screen is also how a player finds out a discipline exists; both now render through one `filterControls`, each handing in its own link builder. The active tab is always in the bar even at count 0, so a stale `?class=` link reads as "Dressage (0)" rather than as an empty page under a bar with nothing selected.
- **`buildShowPageClassGroups`** files every copy of one class under a single heading, keyed on `(class_key, rank)` — the same definition of "the same class" `requestClassEntry` uses. Groups are ordered conformation → disciplines → young horse, then by name, then Novice → Open → Champion; **explicitly not mint order**, which is the thing that was asked to go. Copies render as "Section 1 of 3" inside one card, with a line saying the class was split and each section is judged on its own. `thinFieldNote` is suppressed inside a split class: a section is thin *because* it was split, and "a rank bracket that has not filled up yet" would be flatly untrue there.
- **A "Yours" badge on your own entries**, plus a per-section count, since the tables are mostly show-barn horses and "find the ones I care most about" is the actual complaint. Read off `entry.stable_id` against the account's own stables, which the route already loads for the entry form.
- **The /shows index groups the same way.** Its "Open for entries" cards and its recent-results lines listed a show's classes flat too, so three sections of one class were three near-identical lines *before* a player ever clicked into the show. `groupClassesByKeyAndRank` is the one ordering-and-grouping rule, generic over the view type because the two screens carry different payloads (`buildShowPageClassGroups` is now a two-line wrapper around it) - a show's classes cannot come out in one order on the index and another on the show page. A split class reads "Conformation- Quarter Horse (2 sections)" with the entry counts summed and both judges named, and in recent results "Conformation- Quarter Horse, 2 sections: HH Bluebell and HH Comet won." - judges dropped there, since three names on top of three winners is noise the show's own page already carries.
- **One route pass, not one per tab.** `buildShowPageView` loads the show's full class list once (the tab bar has to count classes the active tab is hiding) and filters in memory; the expensive per-class work — each account's own eligibility for the entry form — still happens only for the classes being drawn. The breed picker offers only breeds this show holds a class for, not every breed with an `ideal_vector`.

**2026-08-05 — Barn list stops showing dead/retired-away horses after the day they end.** Operator report: dead horses were cluttering the stable view. Not a bug — slice 0011 §8.1 deliberately kept an ended horse visible in the main barn list (marked Died/Retired away) for `barn_shows_ended_game_days` (180) after it ended, specifically so the moment wasn't lost the instant it happened. The operator wants the stable view clear of them instead.

- No code change. `barn_shows_ended_game_days` was already a live tunable read fresh by `listStableHorsesWithDead` (`src/db/horses.ts`), exactly built for this. `migrations/0164_barn_hides_ended_horses.sql` retunes it 180 → 0, `json_set`-ing the existing key the same way `0101` retuned `consignment_cadence_game_days` — a new migration, not an edit to `0060` (CLAUDE.md §8, forward-only). Numbered `0164` rather than the `0153` it was authored under, because main gained migrations `0153`-`0163` (slice 0025 stages 1-3) while this change was in review; renumbered on rebase, no other change.
- An ended horse still shows on the game day it actually dies or is retired away (the cutoff comparison is `ended_game_day >= gameDay - 0`), then drops out of the barn list starting the next day. It stays reachable with no cutoff from `/stables/:id/past` (`listPastHorses`), which the barn list already links to ("Past horses"), and its ending is still announced in the events feed — nothing about how a horse ends changed, only how long it lingers in the main view.
- This is a live tunable specifically so the operator can retune it again from `/admin/config` without a code change if 0 turns out to be too abrupt in practice.
- Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `npx vitest run` green (83 files, 783 tests, no test referenced the old default).

**2026-08-05 — Young-horse classes and ability tests.** Stage three of `docs/slices/0025-difficulty-foals-shows-and-evaluation.md`. Answers the fourth and fifth things the children said: they can't tell what a foal is worth because it can't show, and a single adult class per breed/discipline means their top horses just keep winning.

- **`show_classes` gets two more `class_type`s via the same rebuild `discipline` used.** SQLite can't `ALTER` a `CHECK` constraint, so migration `0161` is another create-copy-drop-rename (migration `0064`'s own mechanics, `show_entries` round-tripped through a holding table because its foreign key blocks a bare `DROP TABLE`). `young_conformation` and `ability_test` join `breed_conformation`/`discipline`, plus two new nullable columns, `ability_trait_code` and `age_band` — the combined `CHECK` enforces exactly which columns each of the four requires, the same pairing `discipline` already established for `ideal_vector` XOR `ability_weights`.
- **Neither new class type gets a new scoring formula.** `young_conformation` is scored exactly like `breed_conformation` — same `conformationValues`, same `scoreEntry`, same breed `ideal_vector`, same conformation-judge pool — and `ability_test` exactly like `discipline` — same `abilityValues`, same `scoreAbilityEntry` with a single-trait weight vector (`{v:1,traits:{speed:1}}`; a missing key already reads 0), same discipline-judge pool. `classUsesAbilityScoring(class_type)` is the one discriminant everywhere a caller used to check `=== 'discipline'`; CLAUDE.md §13's no-second-scoring-path rule held.
- **The breed-aptitude modifier stays scoped to a real discipline, on purpose.** `aptitudeFor` reads a breed's suitability for one `discipline_code` — an `ability_test` class has none (it measures raw ability, not fitness for a specific discipline), so `isRealDiscipline = cls.class_type === 'discipline'` gates that modifier separately from the ability-vs-conformation scoring branch. Getting this backwards would have applied a discipline's own aptitude number to a class it has nothing to do with.
- **The two age bands are two config keys and one derivation, not four.** `youngHorseAgeBands()` (`src/db/shows.ts`) computes yearling as `[young_horse_yearling_min_age_game_days, young_horse_two_year_old_min_age_game_days - 1]` and two-year-old as `[young_horse_two_year_old_min_age_game_days, show_conformation_min_age_game_days - 1]` — reusing the *existing* adult min age as the upper bound rather than a fourth key, so a horse ages out of "young" on exactly the day it becomes eligible for the class it graduates into. Seeded at 360/720 game days (migration `0163`) — one and two game years against the adult class's existing 1080 (three years, already the same number `min_breeding_age_game_days` uses).
- **Both new class types are minted every cycle regardless of demand**, the same as every existing breed_conformation/discipline class already is — one `young_conformation` class per breed with an `ideal_vector` per age band (16 today), one `ability_test` class per `ABILITY_TRAITS` trait per age band (10 today, always, independent of which disciplines happen to be enabled). Consistent with the existing convention, not a new one; flagged in the slice document as the thing to revisit if stage 4 multiplies it further (§7.6).
- **The permanent ability word lives in its own table, `horse_ability_words` (migration `0162`), upserted like `horse_show_summary`.** At judging time `judgeOneClass` bands the horse's own **true expressed value** for the one trait an `ability_test` class measures — before noise, care or age modifiers, which describe the judge's day and the horse's upkeep, not the horse's raw ability — into the same Poor/Weak/Acceptable/Good/Outstanding vocabulary the Conformation card already uses. `abilityLabelFor` (`src/engines/conformation/labels.ts`) reuses `bandForTraitScore` directly rather than a new formula: an ability trait has no breed target to measure distance from, so the raw expressed value **is** the band input.
- **The horse page's new Ability card reads the stored word back, and never recomputes it live.** This was a real decision, not the obvious default: conformation labels (slice 0022) are fully dynamic, recomputed from the horse's *current* expressed value on every page view. An ability word is not — "the result permanently records a word" (the slice's own phrase) means the word a horse earned in the class it was actually judged in, the same way a real placing is a fact about that day, not a live-updating estimate that would quietly improve as a young horse matures without ever being tested again. A later test on the same trait overwrites the word (the upsert, not an append), which is the sense in which "permanent" means durable rather than frozen at the first attempt.
- **Word only, no meter, ever.** Unlike the Conformation card's bar-plus-word, this card has no number and no bar — ability values have never been shown to a player anywhere in this game (`engines/conformation/model.ts`'s own long-standing comment), and this card does not start now. A trait with no row reads **Untested**.
- **One bug this surfaced, fixed alongside it rather than filed separately.** The horse page's "Enter in a show" card (`buildEnterShowInfos`) and its entry-refusal message both called `getOpenClasses(ctx.env, 10)` — a hardcoded limit that was already tight against the 14 classes one show could carry before this slice, and would have silently dropped every new `young_conformation`/`ability_test` class (created last, so carrying the highest `id`s, read back `ORDER BY id ASC LIMIT 10`) off the exact card a young horse's owner needs to see them on. Raised to 200, the margin `SHOW_RESULT_FETCH_LIMIT` already uses for the identical reason.
- **`horse_ability_words` carries real foreign keys into both `horses` and `show_entries`**, so it had to be added in two more places this codebase already checks for exactly this: `src/db/reset.ts`'s `RESET_TABLES` (before `show_entries`, not merely before `horses` — it references both) and `src/db/horseRemoval.ts`'s `buildDeleteHorseStatements` (a delete, not a keep-clause, same reasoning as `horse_evaluations` beside it — in practice always zero rows for a horse `deletableHorseSql` actually passes, since that predicate already requires no `show_entries` row exists, but listed anyway for the same "every table is in one list or the other" invariant the file exists to hold). `test/reset.test.ts`'s own `REFERENCES` map and `test/reset-d1.test.ts`'s live-database check both caught the omission before this shipped.
- New tests: `test/conformation/labels.test.ts` (`abilityLabelFor`), `test/showing/showsFilter.test.ts` (the `'young'` tab), `test/showing/showResultGroups.test.ts` (the two new group labels), `test/showing/youngHorseAgeBands.test.ts` (band contiguity), and `test/showing/young-and-ability-classes-d1.test.ts` — the first D1-backed test for show creation/judging in this codebase, covering class-count/shape at creation, a yearling judged in both a `young_conformation` and an `ability_test` class in one pass, the `horse_ability_words` write, and the age-band eligibility refusal (too_young/too_old) at the boundaries.
- Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `npx vitest run` green — 89 files / 841 tests. **Not** verified in a browser.

**2026-08-05 — The per-horse time warp, and foals stop costing board.** Stage two of `docs/slices/0025-difficulty-foals-shows-and-evaluation.md`. Prompted by the sharpest thing in the operator's feedback: a ten-year-old *"already talking about wanting a different game"* because a foal is useless for three game years.

- **The warp moves one horse's own timeline, not the world's.** `src/db/timeWarp.ts` subtracts the same number of days from every absolute game day anchored to *this horse's* birth: `born_game_day`, `natural_death_game_day`, and `horse_conditions.terminal_game_day` / `signs_game_day`. **`born_game_day` moves rather than an age offset being stored** — age is derived from it in roughly forty places (care start, conformation realisation, show eligibility, ageing bands, market appraisal, breeding maturity), and a second source of truth for "how old is this horse" would be wrong in some of them inside one slice. **If a future slice adds another column anchored to a horse's birth, it belongs in `buildTimeWarpStatements`** — nothing can enumerate that for you, which is why this is written here rather than left to be noticed.
- **The death date moves with it, on the operator's explicit choice.** Asked whether warped time should count against the horse's life or be compensated, the answer was "a": you bought the time, and time costs time. A warped horse really will die sooner.
- **A carried lethal still kills.** `terminal_game_day` landing in the past is the point, not a bug — `killDueLethalFoals` finds it on the next tick, so a GBED foal warped to a yearling is a GBED foal that died on the way. The operator named this exception themselves while ruling out simulating anything else: *"if they had a lethal genetic condition that would kill it and that isn't turned off, that should happen. dont roll all the chance/environmental incidents though."*
- **Everything else in the skipped months is genuinely skipped.** No board, no acquired incidents, no care progression. The care markers and `last_incident_check_game_day` are **reset to today** as part of the warp — left alone, a foal whose care timers had not started yet would wake past its own care-start day with no farrier visit on record and read as instantly overdue, charging the player in care penalty for exactly the months the warp did not simulate.
- **Capped at `min_breeding_age_game_days`, and the last step is short rather than refused.** `planTimeWarp` clamps the advance to land exactly on maturity, the button names the real number of days, and the card says the step was shortened. Full price for a short step is deliberate and stated, not hidden. The cap is what stops the warp being a way to run a mare out of her breeding years or a veteran into its own death date — `test/time-warp.test.ts` proves twenty repeat purchases can never cross it.
- **A turn as well as money, and deliberately no per-horse daily limit** (the operator declined one). The turn is the whole rate limit: a crop of six foals is six turns to warp once each.
- **Foals cost nothing to keep until one year old** (migration `0158`, one clause in `chargeUpkeep`'s counting query). The operator raised this unprompted: *"foals probably shouldn't cost upkeep. id throw them out in the pasture myself to avoid it since they cant be shown or bred anyways"* — board on a horse that can neither show nor breed is a chore with no decision in it. Excluded from **both** the barn and pasture counts, filtered in SQL rather than after the fact so the ledger's "Board for N horses" sentence and the actual charge can never disagree about who was billed. One year rather than three because stage three's yearling classes are what make one year the age a horse can do something. `upkeep_free_until_age_game_days = 0` restores the old behaviour exactly.
- **New ledger kind `time_warp`** (migration `0160`, the usual table rebuild). Its own kind because it is the one purchase in the game that buys nothing you can point at — no test result, no horse, no service — and a child reading the money page needs to see what that money was.
- Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `npx vitest run` green — 87 files / 823 tests. **Not** verified in a browser.

**2026-08-05 — Per-account difficulty, paid evaluations, conformation on studs, and an honest foal range.** Stage one of `docs/slices/0025-difficulty-foals-shows-and-evaluation.md`, commissioned from three days of real play. Four pieces, shipped together because they interlock; the other three stages of that brief (the time warp and free foal upkeep, young-horse classes and ability tests, two shows and a three-rank progression) are specified there and **not built**.

- **Difficulty is per account, and its scope is the decision worth keeping.** `accounts.difficulty` (migration `0153`) plus six flat decimal config keys (`0154`), read by `src/engines/incidents/difficulty.ts` and applied at exactly two call sites in `src/db/incidents.ts`. It scales incident onset rate and the bad half of an outcome table, **and nothing else** — not inherited disease, not lethal foals, not death from old age. The reasoning to keep if someone wants to widen it: an inherited disease is a fact about a genotype, so softening it per account would make two children's horses genuinely different animals, and a foal sold from one barn to another would change its own biology on the way across. Incident risk has no such problem — it is a property of how the world treats a horse, re-read from the owner's setting on every check.
- **Named levels with operator-editable numbers, as six flat decimals rather than a JSON blob.** `/admin/config` already edits flat numeric keys through a form the operator can use; a blob would need a JSON editor they have no way to validate. `normal` is 1.0/1.0 **by definition** — it is today's game, and moving it moves the baseline for everyone at once, NPC-facing balance included.
- **`scaleBadOutcomes` always returns a table summing to 1.** Slice 0020 shipped two outcome tables that didn't and only its own test caught them, so this one is proved for every level, both tables, and multipliers from 0 to 50. It scales the death + degenerative pair, gives what that frees back to resolved and manageable **in their existing ratio**, and clamps rather than going negative. The rate multiplier is applied **before** the ceiling clamp, so `incident_probability_ceiling_per_game_day` still means what it says at every level. An NPC stable (no `account_id`) always reads neutral.
- **`rollOutcome` gained a multiplier, not a second roll.** The seed and the draw are unchanged; difficulty reshapes the table the same draw is measured against. That is what keeps an outcome reproducible from the horse's own seed (CLAUDE.md §5.2) — a re-run of the same tick gives the same answer, which matters most when the answer was a death.
- **Evaluations are `horse_evaluations`, deliberately NOT `horse_knowledge`.** That table's unique index `(stable_id, horse_id, subject_code)` exists precisely to stop a permanent result being bought twice; an evaluation is repeatable at full price, because the answer genuinely improves with the horse's age. Its own table keeps that difference visible instead of forcing an exception into the one place the codebase guarantees a permanent result cannot be bought twice.
- **Three properties carry the evaluation and are worth restating before changing it.** (1) *It never lies* — the true label is always inside the range; a wide answer is vague, never wrong. (2) *It cannot be averaged away* — the hedging offset is derived from the horse's own `rng_seed`, not drawn per purchase, so five evaluations on the same foal on the same day return the same five answers. Independent per-purchase noise would let a player average it out and read the exact answer off a weanling for the price of a few tests. (3) *It sharpens smoothly* — the same offset scaled by a shrinking spread, so successive evaluations close in on the truth rather than jumping around it. The pages say (2) out loud so nobody spends money finding out.
- **A horse is evaluated at maturity, not at its current age.** A four-month-old's expressed conformation has barely realised; the question a child is actually asking is what the foal will become. An evaluation is **refused rather than sold** for a breed with no `ideal_vector` — an evaluator who cannot judge should not take the money.
- **The conformation labels were already "true" labels** and needed no change. `src/engines/conformation/labels.ts` has always scored against the breed ideal with the judge's weighting left out — its own comment says "a weight says how much a judge cares, not how good the horse is". The operator asked for true labels rather than judge-noised ones; that was already the case, and the only thing missing was that studs showed none at all.
- **`src/db/conformationLabels.ts` is the one shared builder** for a horse's conformation words, so an owner's words and a stranger's words cannot drift apart. Free and public on a stud listing, behind the same gate the owner's card uses (one show start, and a breed with a standard). **The constraint that makes this disclosure rather than cheating, in the operator's daughter's own words: "its not knowing the number or which side of the ideal it falls on."** A word only, and never which way the horse misses — do not add a direction hint to this render.
- **One consequence taken deliberately: `buildBreedPreview` now passes `stallionConformationKnown: true` for a stallion standing at stud.** It was false on the reasoning that his conformation appeared nowhere else in the game; that reasoning expired the moment his own stud page started printing the words in full, and leaving it would have made the preview read Unknown next to a page showing the answer.
- **`src/engines/genetics/foalPrediction.ts` is exact, not a simulation.** A foal's potential for a trait is a Poisson-binomial over twenty independent allele draws (ten per parent), convolved exactly — twenty terms over twenty-one buckets, so there is no reason to reach for a normal approximation. Noise is enumerated over a rounded grid rather than sampled. No RNG anywhere in the file. A test checks the distribution against ten thousand simulated foals drawn the way inheritance actually works; the simulation lives in the test, where RNG belongs, not in the engine.
- **A trait's label is not monotonic in its expressed value**, and the foal range would be wrong if you forgot it: too far above the breed's target is as bad as too far below, so the central 80% of values maps to a *set* of labels and the honest summary is the best and worst word in that set — not the labels at the two ends of the interval. A pairing whose range straddles the target has its best word in the middle.
- **The foal range reads "Unknown" when either parent's own label is unknown**, with a line saying to show or evaluate that parent. The operator chose this over showing a range computed from the real values: a prediction built from numbers the player has not earned is cheating dressed as helpfulness, and it would have leaked an outside stallion's conformation through the back door. A cross-breed pairing reads "No single standard" rather than quietly picking the dam's breed.
- **`horse_evaluations` joins `buildDeleteHorseStatements` as a delete and is deliberately NOT added to `deletableHorseSql`.** An evaluated horse must stay deletable — a child evaluating a whole crop of foals would otherwise quietly make every one of them un-removable, which is exactly the failure mode the 2026-08-04 pet-home fix was about. It also joins `src/db/reset.ts`'s two lists and `test/reset.test.ts`'s `REFERENCES` map.
- **New ledger kind `evaluation` (migration `0157`, the usual table rebuild).** Its own kind rather than reusing `vet`, because the ledger is a thing a child reads and it is the one deliberately repeatable purchase in the game.
- **A note on the standing concern.** The operator chose to fix the *expectation* rather than the genetics for "foals are too random". The honest range for a real pairing is often wide, so the preview may confirm the frustration rather than dissolve it. It is still the right thing to have built first: it is the instrument that will say whether the variance is genuinely too high before anybody touches inheritance.
- Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `npx vitest run` green apart from `test/genetics/foal-colours.test.ts`'s 50ms CPU guard, which **fails identically on the unmodified tree in this container** (~51ms under full-suite parallelism, passes in isolation) — a slow machine, not a regression. 86 files / 817 tests, up from 83 / 783. **Not** verified in a browser.

---

**2026-08-04 — Breeding eligibility display fix: stop offering breeding to horses that cannot breed.** `docs/fixes/breeding-eligibility-display.md`. Not a rules bug — `validateBooking` (`src/routes/horses.ts`) and `validateStudBooking` (`src/db/stud.ts`) already refused every one of these correctly. The bug was three screens and three pickers inviting a click that was always going to be refused.

- **Two new pure engines, `src/engines/breeding/`.** `maturity.ts`'s `isOldEnoughToBreed(bornGameDay, gameDay, minAgeGameDays)` answers the age question both refusal ladders already had inline. `eligibility.ts`'s `mareIneligibility(input)` factors out the mare-side, stallion-independent part of that same ladder (location, age, recovery, in-foal, booked) into one reason code — `validateBooking` and `validateStudBooking` both now call it and keep their own sentences; a picker calls it to decide what to list. Order matters and mirrors the ladders' own: location, then age, then recovery, then pregnancy, then an existing booking. Season and capacity stay **out** of it on purpose — both are barn-wide facts, not per-mare ones, and still live only in the two refusal ladders.
- **A `stallionIneligibility` sibling, not in the fix doc's own text but named in its own table** (the Breed page's "your stallion" picker was suffering the identical defect - a colt was offered same as a proven stallion). Deliberately narrower than the mare version: only `too_young`/`at_pasture`/`settling_in`, since "booked" and "recovering" are mare concepts (`coverings.mare_id`, the recovery timer) that don't apply to a stallion picked with no mare chosen yet.
- **Three screens fixed by the same age check.** `mareStatusLine` (a mare's own page) now checks `isOldEnoughToBreed` before anything else in its priority ladder and returns "Too young to breed. She can be bred from around {date}." for a filly - previously she read "In season now." or a "due back around" date for a heat she could not physically be having. The barn list's green "in season" badge gets the same guard. The horse page's "Offer at stud" card, for an immature colt, is replaced with a muted "Old enough to stand at stud from around {date}." rather than a form that would always fail on the first booking attempt.
- **Three pickers now only offer what they'd actually accept**, each with a line naming who was left off and why (the operator's own "show why, do not just hide" call, made for the 2026-08-04 mare-picker fix earlier the same day): the Breed page's mare picker and its own-stallion picker (`src/routes/horses.ts`'s new `mareEligibilityFor`/`stallionEligibilityFor`), and `/market/stud`'s mare picker (`src/routes/market.ts`'s rewritten `mareOptionsFor`, now returning `{ eligible, excluded }` instead of every mare unconditionally). The phrasing lives in `src/render/horses.ts` (`MareExclusion`, `mareExclusionPhrase`, `exclusionsLine`, `noEligibleMaresLine`) — a picker only returns a reason code, never a sentence — and `src/render/market.ts` imports the same phrase function so a mare reads identically on both screens, adding only the stable name its own picker (spanning every stable on the account) needs. When **no** mare is eligible, the whole booking form is replaced with one sentence ("None of your mares can be bred right now. Bella is in foal, ... and Pepper is recovering from foaling until around {date}.") rather than an empty `<select>` next to a live button.
- **`test/market/breed-and-stud-pickers-d1.test.ts`'s first describe block tested the old bug on purpose** (a stale doc comment called it "the whole of the fix" for a defect one layer up) — it asserted the in-foal mare's refusal text appeared on the stud page's default view. Rewritten to assert the new, correct behaviour instead: she's absent from the picker, named in the exclusion line, and the one remaining eligible mare already has a live book button with no click needed. A second case (every mare ineligible) and two new Breed-page cases (partial exclusion, and total exclusion collapsing the form) were added alongside it.
- New `test/breeding/maturity.test.ts` and `test/breeding/eligibility.test.ts` cover the two engines directly, boundary and priority order included. The regression the doc's own §Tests calls out (both refusal ladders refuse everything they refused before) is covered implicitly - the full existing suite, including `test/market/stud-d1.test.ts`'s season/capacity/balance/dead-stallion cases and the "is already in foal" assertion still in the second Breed-page test, passed unchanged against the refactored ladders.
- Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `npx vitest run` green (80 files, 742 tests, up from 78 files/727 tests). **Not** verified in a browser.

---

**2026-08-04 — Breed finally means something in a discipline class.** Operator report: German Warmbloods were outperforming Quarter Horses in barrel racing. Nothing was broken — **breed had never entered ability at any point in the game**, and this is the change that makes it.

- **What was actually true before this.** `potential()` → `geneticValue` → `expressedValue` → `scoreAbilityEntry` contains no reference to `breed_id` anywhere. The three places breed lives were all somewhere else: `founding_allele_pool` is Mendelian only (colour, gait, disease — migration `0010` says so), `ideal_vector` covers only conformation, and a discipline `show_class` carries `breed_id = NULL` by migration `0064`'s own `CHECK` with every discipline seeded `crosses_eligible = 1`. So a Warmblood's speed alleles were drawn from the identical distribution as a Quarter Horse's, and the class was decided by whichever horse happened to roll a slice 0019 **founding specialist** in `speed` or `agility` — a flat 2-in-5 chance for every founding and consignment horse of every breed, since `getSpecializableAbilityTraits()` returns all five ability traits now that all six disciplines are enabled. Slice 0012 §3.2 and `docs/breed-ideal-vectors.md` §6.3 both named this gap and left it open on purpose, because the aptitudes had not been decided.
- **Two mechanisms, both built, because the operator asked for both after being shown the trade.** `breeds.ability_bias` (migrations `0141`/`0142`) is an offset to the quality band's `polygenic_one_chance`, applied in `generateCandidate` — heritable, breeds true under selection, and reaches only horses generated after it landed. `breeds.discipline_aptitudes` (migrations `0143`/`0144`) is a multiplier folded into `scoreAbilityEntry` — reaches every existing horse immediately, but is a fact about the breed that no amount of good breeding answers. Neither substitutes for the other; see `docs/breed-ability-and-aptitude.md` §2 for the table.
- **Every `ability_bias` row sums to zero, and a test reads the migration to prove it.** This is the load-bearing constraint: a row that does not sum to zero does not make a breed *different*, it makes that breed *better*. `discipline_aptitudes` rows are deliberately **not** zero-sum (a breed may be good at two things, and a horse enters one class at a time), so the check that replaces it is that every row's *mean* sits within 0.02 of 1.00. Both tests read `migrations/0142` and `migrations/0144` off disk rather than hand-copying the numbers, the same way `test/router-paths.test.ts` reads `src/router.ts` — a retune that breaks either invariant fails there, named. Both join the `tsconfig.json` exclude list for `node:fs`, for the reason that list's own comment gives.
- **The size of the numbers is the whole decision, and it is written out in `docs/breed-ability-and-aptitude.md` §4.** An `ability_bias` offset of 0.06 is worth 1.2 potential = 6 geneticValue points. At the seeded ±0.05 aptitude an equally-bred Quarter Horse beats an equally-bred Warmblood in barrels by 10.1 points ≈ 3.4σ of that class's noise. **The property that fixed the number at ±0.05 rather than higher:** a specialist *overwrites* its trait's potential rather than adjusting it, so a breed's bias never applies to the one trait a horse was singled out for — which means a Warmblood carrying a speed specialist still scores 57.4 against an ordinary Quarter Horse's 56.9 and can take the class. Push past about ±0.06 and breed stops being a strong tendency and becomes a gate.
- **`src/engines/breeds/identity.ts` is a new engine directory** (`CLAUDE.md` §4's list grows by one). Both parsers live there rather than next to their consumers because they answer the same question from the same table, and splitting them would put half of breed balance under `engines/founding` and half under `engines/showing`. `parseAbilityBias` **filters to `ABILITY_TRAITS`** — a conformation key in the JSON is dropped, not honoured, because conformation already differs by breed through `ideal_vector` (which moves the target, not the draw) and robustness is drawn at its own fixed chance for a slice 0014 §2.8 reason a breed has no business overriding.
- **A crossbred horse always reads `NEUTRAL_APTITUDE`.** Not a fallback — a real decision. `horses.breed_id` on a cross names the registry it competes under (a Paint's `breed_id` *is* the Quarter Horse's, overview §4a, which `checkEligibility` leans on), so honouring it would hand a half-Warmblood cross the full Quarter Horse barrel bonus.
- **`scoreEntry` gets no counterpart and must not grow one.** A conformation class admits one breed and judges it against that breed's own `ideal_vector`, so an aptitude there would multiply every entry in the class by the identical number.
- **The aptitude is a live tunable and is NOT snapshotted onto the class** (`CLAUDE.md` §5.5), because it belongs to the horse's breed and a discipline class has no `breed_id` to snapshot it against. Retuning therefore changes a class already entered but not yet judged, the same way retuning care or upkeep does. To keep the record, a discipline entry's `score_breakdown` now carries an `aptitude` block with the exact multiplier, the breed id, and the cross flag — that row is the only surviving evidence of what a horse was actually judged with.
- **NPC-minted horses now get founding specialists, on the operator's explicit decision.** `mintFoundingHorses` (`src/db/npc.ts`) had passed neither `breedIdealVector` nor `eligibleAbilityTraits` since slice 0019, so specialists reached every founding, claimed and consigned horse in the game *except* the ones NPC stables mint — meaning player stock was systematically better than the field it competed against, in both conformation and ability, for reasons nothing had written down. This raises NPC quality, which `CLAUDE.md` §13 singles out as the failure mode most likely to kill the project. It is bounded rather than climbing (one trait at a fixed potential, and the `npc_policy` ceiling still governs everything these stables *breed*), but **watch `/admin/npc` over the next few game weeks.**
- **NPC valuation was deliberately left alone.** `engines/npc/selection.ts` and `db/npcMarket.ts` already score with `noise: 0` and no care, age or tack modifier; aptitude joins that list rather than breaking into it. An NPC stable is single-breed, so an aptitude would multiply every candidate it ranks by the same number and change no decision. This is not a second scoring path — it is the same function called with the show-time modifiers a valuation has never used.
- **Not touched, and flagged rather than fixed:** horses created by hand at `/admin/horses` get no bias, no aptitude in generation, and no founding specialist — that form uses `generateFounderPolygenic`'s flat 50/50 draw, which slice 0005 §6.6 chose so a hand-built test horse is a neutral control. Also still unbuilt: `eligible_class_types` (every discipline still admits every breed — an aptitude says a Warmblood is *bad* at barrels, not *barred* from them), `height_range`/`weight_range`, and the seven non-Quarter-Horse disease panels.

**2026-08-04 — "Call the vet" 404'd from the day it shipped; two mare-picker defects the operator reported.** Three unrelated fixes, written up together because they arrived together.

- **The router keeps the same fact in two places, and they had drifted.** `HORSE_ROUTE` (`src/router.ts`) is a regex whose second capture group lists every allowed sub-path; twenty lines below it sits a chain of `if (sub === '/x')` handlers. Slice 0020's `POST /horses/:id/treat` was added to the chain and never to the regex, so **every press of "Call the vet" on the Incidents card fell past the whole horse block to the router's closing `notFound()`** — a bare "Not found" page, from a button that had a handler, a test suite, and a database layer all working perfectly behind it. Nothing to do with the world reset the operator suspected. `/treat` added to the alternation; the regex now carries a comment saying the two lists must match.
- **`test/router-paths.test.ts` reads `src/router.ts` off disk rather than restating it.** It extracts each route regex literal *and* the `sub === '...'` literals from the handler block that regex opens, and asserts every handled sub-path matches. A hand-copied list in a test would have been written from the same reading of the code that produced the bug. Confirmed to fail with the fix reverted. It needs `node:fs`, so it joins the D1 tests in `tsconfig.json`'s `exclude` list, for the reason that list's own comment gives.
- **A stud booking refusal used to be the whole page.** `/market/stud/:id` rendered the mare `<select>` *inside* the booking form, and the booking form only rendered when `validateStudBooking` returned nothing — so an operator with four mares whose first one was in foal read "she is already in foal" with no control anywhere to say "not that one, this one". The picker is now its own **GET form back to the same page** (`?mare=<id>`, a route parameter that already existed and was only reachable by hand-typing a URL) and renders whenever there is more than one mare, refusal or not. A refused `POST /market/stud/:id/book` carries `&mare=` back with the error so the picker returns showing the mare it is talking about. No JavaScript, a visible "Choose this mare" button, same shape as the barn page's feed selector.
- **The Breed page has a third picker: every stallion standing at another ranch.** Cross-stable breeding has existed since slice 0017 Part D but only at `/market/stud`, which a child would have to already know about. The picker lists every active stud listing whose `stable_account_id` is not this account's (NPC ranches, whose column is `NULL`, are included by that test rather than by a special case), defaults to "none, use my own stallion above", and wins over the stallion picker when something is chosen. **The Book button posts to `/market/stud/:id/book` — the market's own endpoint, unchanged** — so there is still exactly one cross-stable breeding path in the game, not a second one grown on this page (CLAUDE.md §13). The refusal is computed and shown *in place of* the button rather than after pressing it, which is the same lesson the stud page just had to learn.
- **`buildBreedPreview` (`src/routes/horses.ts`) is now the one preview builder**, extracted from the `action === 'check'` block so the outside-stud path cannot drift from the same-stable one — the numbers a child compares two stallions on have to be the same numbers. Its two options are the whole of the difference, and both are knowledge-boundary decisions:
  - `stallionKnowledgeStableId` — whose `horse_knowledge` stands for what is known about him. For a stud listing this is **his owner's stable**, which reproduces exactly the disclosure the market already makes on his stud page (slice 0017 §2.3, health travels with the horse) and nothing wider: still knowledge rows, never a genotype, so an untested stallion still reads untested. `breedingHealthWarningsFor` (`src/db/health.ts`) grew a fifth parameter defaulting to the booking stable, so every pre-existing caller is untouched.
  - `stallionConformationKnown` — **false** for somebody else's stallion. His conformation is shown nowhere else in the game to anyone but his owner (not on `/world/horses/:id`, not on his stud page), so his column reads Unknown rather than this becoming the one screen that tells you. Colour is deliberately *not* treated the same way: colour test results are not part of any listing's disclosure, so an outside stallion's colour reads off his coat, as it does for anyone standing at the rail.
- **The stallion `<select>` lost its `required` attribute**, since a pairing can now legitimately be made without one. The route refuses a submission that names neither with a sentence naming both options.
- **The third picker's options read `Quarter Horse - CD Waverly at Suchon Acres. Fee 430.`** — breed first, no colour blurb. The first cut reused the market's own horse description (`A white grey tobiano stallion, born bay, 8 years old.`), which on the operator's phone made every option three lines tall, pushed the fee off the end, and left the one thing being scanned for — the breed — nowhere on the line at all. **Ordered by the mare's own breed first, then the remaining breeds alphabetically, then by stallion name.** The mare it sorts around is whichever one the mare picker is currently showing (the first in the list on a fresh page view, the chosen one after a Check pairing), so the order always describes the pairing the form would actually submit. Nothing is hidden and cross-breed pairings are as legal as they ever were — this is ordering, not filtering.
- `test/market/breed-and-stud-pickers-d1.test.ts` covers all of it against a real database: the refusal-with-picker, picking the other mare and getting a book button, the third picker appearing with its fee, an outside-stud preview posting to the market route, the in-foal mare's refusal replacing that button, and — the guard against silently revealing what should not be revealed — an exact count of `Unknown` conformation cells with both horses given show records, which fails the moment `stallionConformationKnown` is flipped.
- Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm test` green (75 files, 691 tests) apart from the pre-existing CPU-timing flake in `test/genetics/foal-colours.test.ts` noted in the two entries below, which fails under full-suite load on this container and is untouched by any of this. **Not** verified in a browser — the three screens have not been looked at, and the "Call the vet" fix in particular deserves one real press on the deployed site.
