**2026-08-04 — Discipline judges: answers slice 0012 §15.** `docs/slices/0024-discipline-judges.md`. A discipline class has judge variance now, the way a breed class always has, and a discipline class's result page no longer shows a conformation judge's blurb sitting over a score it had zero effect on.

- **`judges` gained `kind` and `ability_weights` via plain `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN` (migration `0139`) — no CHECK enforcing the trait_weights/ability_weights pairing.** Adding one would have forced the same rebuild migration `0064` had to do to `show_classes` — pulling `show_entries` out of the way first, since it has a live foreign key one hop further down the chain (`show_entries.class_id` → `show_classes.judge_id` → `judges.id`). Two nullable columns on reference data don't warrant that; `breeds.ideal_vector` (migration `0034`) is the existing precedent for a nullable pairing enforced only by comment and convention, not a CHECK.
- **Three new discipline judges (migration `0140`)**: `even_hand`/Osgood (every ability trait at 1.0), `need_for_speed`/Ferris (speed/stamina up, trainability down), `clean_and_correct`/Winslow (trainability/jump_scope up, speed down) — same spread pattern as the three existing conformation judges (Marchbank/Ellery/Halloway), a neutral read plus two pulling opposite directions so no single horse dominates every class. `trait_weights` is still populated (`{"v":1,"traits":{}}`) on all three even though it is never read for a discipline judge, because the column stays `NOT NULL` and an empty object is the honest "no opinion" value, not a fabricated one.
- **`getJudges(env, kind?)` (`src/db/judges.ts`) takes an optional pool filter now; `getJudgeById` stays kind-agnostic.** `createShowIfMissing` (`src/db/shows.ts`) fetches both pools up front and draws a breed class's judge from `conformationJudges`, a discipline class's from `disciplineJudges` — the `class_N`/`judge_N` seed derivation itself (CLAUDE.md §5.2) is completely unchanged, only which array the roll indexes into moved. A show created before this migration is unaffected.
- **`scoreAbilityEntry` (`src/engines/showing/abilityScore.ts`) gained an optional `judgeWeights` parameter, defaulting to `{}`.** Folds in exactly the way `scoreEntry`'s own `judgeWeights` folds into `ideal.weight`: `weight_t = discipline_weight_t * judge_weight_t`. A missing key in `judgeWeights` reads as **1.0** ("no opinion") — the opposite of the discipline's own `weights`, where a missing key reads as **0** ("does not care about this ability at all"). Every caller that predates this slice (NPC valuation in `npcMarket.ts`/`npcBreeding.ts`, `selection.ts`, and the pre-existing test file) keeps producing exactly today's number, since the default is a no-op multiplier.
- **`judgeOneClass` reuses the existing `parseAbilityWeights` for a judge's `ability_weights`** — no new parser needed, since the JSON shape (`{v, traits}` over the five ability trait codes) is identical to a discipline's own `ability_weights`.
- **Noise (`noise_sd`) was deliberately left untouched.** Judge variance and noise are two separate mechanics (overview §9); this only adds the one disciplines never had, onto values (slice 0012 §6.5) already tuned for the one they did.
- Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `npx vitest run` green (684 tests — 12 new in `test/showing/abilityScore.test.ts`'s `judgeWeights` block: the omitted-vs-`{}` equivalence, the missing-key-reads-1.0 convention, a hand-checked worked example against Ferris's weights, and a same-two-horses-different-winners case swapping the Show Jumping placing between Ferris and Winslow). Migrations `0139`/`0140` exercised by the existing D1-backed test suite (`test/reset-d1.test.ts` and friends apply every migration file to a real SQLite database) with no error. **Not** verified against a live `wrangler dev` or real D1, and **not** verified visually — no browser was available this session to look at a discipline class's result page and confirm the new judge's blurb reads sensibly next to a Barrel Racing or Show Jumping score.

---

**2026-08-04 — Picture labels: an operator-only caption per numbered library picture.** Small follow-up to slice 0007 (the image slot), requested directly by the operator ("a way to label my pics via the file title") rather than specified in a slice document.

- **Two ways to build this were on the table, and the operator picked the smaller one.** Slice 0007 §2.3 fixed the library forever as `<code>-NN.webp`, read only by count, because Cloudflare's static assets have no directory-listing API — the app can never discover what a file is named, only how many exist. Putting the label *in* the filename would have meant replacing `image_count` (one number) with a maintained list of filenames, a real architecture change for a small want. Instead: **a new table, `breed_image_labels` (migration `0138`, `breed_id`, `image_index`, `label`, one row per labelled picture, no row = no label)**, edited from `/admin/breeds` right next to the existing count inputs, in the same one-Save-button form. The filename convention, the upload workflow, and the picker's own contract are all untouched.
- **The label is a caption on the picture, never a claim about the horse wearing it.** `docs/slices/0007-image-slot.md` §4.2 requires alt text not describe the horse, because a picture is chosen freely and the label would be a lie the moment it's mismatched. A label like "flashy chestnut, jumping" describes what the operator actually put in the file — true regardless of which horse later picks it — so it doesn't reopen §2.1's no-colour-matching decision. It shows as a small caption under the thumbnail in `/horses/:id/image` (and is folded into that image's `alt` text) purely to help a child recognise a picture they remember, same spirit as the existing "also used by Dusty" note.
- **`ImageOption` gained an optional `label` field; `imageOptionsFor` gained an optional third `labels: Map<string,string>` argument**, keyed by `imageLabelKey(breedCode, index)` — backward compatible, every existing call site and test untouched. `buildImageLabelMap` joins the DB rows (keyed by `breed_id`, the foreign key) onto that breed-code key, skipping a row whose `breed_id` matches no known breed rather than throwing, the same discipline `imageOptionsFor` itself already uses for an unknown composition code.
- **Cached the same way `getBreeds`/`getLoci` already are** — a 60-second per-isolate module cache, cleared on write, with the same accepted staleness the admin page already states in plain English for counts. A blank label box deletes the row rather than storing `label = ''`, so "no label" stays one state, not two.
- Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `npx vitest run` green (672 tests, including two new `describe` blocks in `test/images.test.ts` covering label pass-through and the breed_id join). Not verified against live D1 or in a browser this session — the operator should apply migration `0138` from `/admin/migrations`, then try `/admin/breeds`.

---

**2026-08-04 — Bug fix: the reset button threw FOREIGN KEY the moment a queued allele had actually been used.** No slice; the operator reported `/admin/reset` doing nothing. Built on top of slice 0023, whose own `resetWorld` rework did not touch this.

- **The symptom the operator saw was a button that did nothing.** `/admin/reset` has no `catch`, so the throw fell through to `src/index.ts`'s handler and came back as a bare `Something went wrong.` 500 — no message, no clue, both scopes. Worth remembering as a shape: anything that throws inside an admin route reaches the operator with all of its diagnostic value stripped off.
- **The cause: `consignment_injections.applied_horse_id` (migration `0096`) is a real foreign key into `horses`, and `src/db/reset.ts` never dealt with it.** D1 enforces foreign keys, so `DELETE FROM horses` failed and rolled the whole batch back. It only started biting once the operator's first queued allele was genuinely consumed by a batch — `runConsignments` stamps `status = 'applied', applied_horse_id = ?` — because until then the column was always NULL. `/admin/consignment`'s "Mint a batch now" button makes that happen the same day an allele is queued.
- **This is the exact failure `src/db/horseRemoval.ts`'s header warns about, in the other file.** That file's fifteen-table rule ("every table with a `REFERENCES horses (id)` column is in one list or the other, or the delete starts failing inside a batch") applies just as hard to `reset.ts`, which had no equivalent note and had never been reconciled against it. `horseRemoval.ts` handles `consignment_injections` correctly and has since it was written; `reset.ts` was the one that drifted. **Both files now say so in prose.**
- **The two reset scopes deliberately treat the table differently, which is why it is handled inside `resetWorld` rather than added to `HORSE_TABLES`.** A horses-only reset deletes just the `applied` rows (`WHERE applied_horse_id IS NOT NULL` — exactly the set that breaks the key, since `queued` and `cancelled` rows always have it NULL): the clock keeps running in that scope, so a still-`queued` injection is a pending instruction that is just as valid tomorrow, and silently cancelling it would be a surprise. A full world reset empties the table, because `game_day` goes back to 0 and every `eligible_from_game_day` in there then describes a world that no longer exists — a queue deferred to day 420 of the old world would sit dormant for 420 days of the new one (`computeInjectionEligibleFromGameDay`, `src/db/consignment.ts`).
- **`test/reset.test.ts` could never have caught this, and that is the more useful lesson.** It checks the delete *order* among the tables already in `RESET_TABLES`; it has no way to notice a table left out of the lists entirely. `test/reset-d1.test.ts` gained a guard that asks the built schema instead of a hand-maintained map — walk `sqlite_master`, run `PRAGMA foreign_key_list` on each table, and require every table with a foreign key into `horses` to be in `RESET_TABLES` or in a short, named exception set. A future migration that adds one and forgets the reset now fails with the table named. **Prefer this shape over another hand-kept list whenever the database can be asked directly.**
- The existing `reset-d1` tests missed it for a plainer reason worth restating: **they reset a world with no horses in it**, so nothing could ever violate a foreign key into `horses`. The two new cases use the real path — queue an allele, mint the batch, let the tick stamp `applied_horse_id` — rather than hand-inserting the row, and both were confirmed to fail against the unfixed `reset.ts` before the fix went in.
- Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean and `npx vitest run` green at 668 tests, apart from the same pre-existing flaky CPU-timing test in `test/genetics/foal-colours.test.ts` earlier entries already record (fails only under full-suite parallel load, passes in isolation, untouched by this fix). **Not** verified against live D1 or in a browser — the button itself has not been pressed on the deployed site.
- **Not changed, and worth a look by whoever picks this up:** `/admin/reset`'s confirmation table still does not list `consignment_injections` among the things about to go. `RESET_TABLE_LABELS` is typed over `RESET_TABLES`, so showing it there means either widening that type or special-casing the row, and neither was worth doing inside a bug fix. The operator therefore gets no warning that a full reset clears the injection queue.

---

**2026-08-04 — NPC stables for Paso Fino and German Warmblood: built, plus an operator rename of the three existing Quarter Horse personalities.** `docs/slices/0023-npc-stables-paso-fino-and-german-warmblood.md` is the spec; migrations `0136`/`0137` are the whole build. Confirms the spec's own headline finding: since slice 0015 built every downstream mechanism (`runNpcBreedingDecisions`, the ceiling schedule, market listing/buying, NPC stud) to read any `npc_policy` row generically, adding two breeds' worth of NPC stables really was a pure-data change plus one reset.ts touch — no engine, tick, or route file changed.

- **The operator supplied nine stable names and asked for them distributed by breed fit, and confirmed volume breeders for both new breeds** (resolving §2.1's "recommended against" — the operator wanted full parity, three personalities per breed, not two). Final mapping: Quarter Horse keeps its existing personalities, renamed by migration `0136` — Fair Meadow (volume breeder) → **Apples and Oats Ranch**, Cedar Hollow (conformation specialist) → **Bronco Valley**, Willow Creek Barrels (discipline barn, Barrel Racing) → **Horseshoe Bay**. Migration `0137` adds Paso Fino's **Carrot Cove** (conformation specialist), **Honeycomb Hills** (discipline barn, Gaited Pleasure), **Reese Puff Horses** (volume breeder); and German Warmblood's **Queens Palace** (conformation specialist), **Springen Stables** (discipline barn), **Suchon Acres** (volume breeder).
- **German Warmblood's discipline barn targets Show Jumping, not Dressage** — the spec's §2.2 left this genuinely open (both fit the breed), and this session read the operator's own chosen name for the stable, "Springen Stables" ("springen" is German for "to jump"), as resolving it in Jumping's favour rather than asking a second question when the answer was already implied. Flagged to the operator in the same turn in case that reading is wrong — cheap to fix with `UPDATE npc_policy SET target_discipline_code = 'dressage' WHERE stable_id = (SELECT id FROM stables WHERE prefix = 'Springen Stables')` if so.
- **Every new personality's numbers are its Quarter Horse counterpart's, verbatim** (spec §2.4): conformation specialists copy Bronco Valley's row, discipline barns copy Horseshoe Bay's, volume breeders copy Apples and Oats Ranch's (capacity 200, vs. 40 for the other two personalities). No new numbers were invented for this session's two breeds.
- **A rename is a `stables` UPDATE plus a closed + reopened `stable_prefix_history` row, not a delete-and-reinsert** — preserves the stable's `id` (so every foreign key into it, and every existing horse's `breeder_prefix`, stays correct) and keeps `stable_prefix_history` an honest record of "Fair Meadow" having been claimed and then released, the same way the table would record a player renaming a stable. `npc_policy` needed no touch at all; it keys off `stable_id`, never the name.
- **`src/db/reset.ts`'s NPC re-seed block was refactored into one `personalityStableStatements` helper** called nine times, rather than copy-pasting the three-INSERT shape a ninth time — the file's own comment explains why nine copies stopped being the boring option once slice 0015 also had three. This is the one real code change beyond the two migrations.
- **Six existing `*-d1.test.ts` files hardcoded a player-test-stable id that assumed exactly four NPC stables already existed** (`test/health/acquired-d1.test.ts`, `test/market/sale-d1.test.ts`, `test/market/stud-d1.test.ts`, `test/market/sell-into-offer-d1.test.ts`, `test/npc/buying-d1.test.ts`, `test/npc/pet-home-d1.test.ts`) — each seeds its own player stable by relying on SQLite's `stables.id` autoincrement continuing on from whatever the migrations left behind, rather than an explicit id. Growing the roster from four NPC stables to ten shifted that number from 5 (and 6, for the two-stable tests) to 11 (and 12); all six were updated, plus `test/reset-d1.test.ts`'s own NPC-stable-count assertion (4 → 10). Found by running the full suite after the migrations landed, not by tracing every call site by hand — `npm test` (71 files, 658 tests) and `npx tsc --noEmit` both clean afterwards.
- **`src/db/npc.ts`'s exported prefix constants were renamed to match their new role names** (`CEDAR_HOLLOW_PREFIX`/`WILLOW_CREEK_BARRELS_PREFIX` → `QH_CONFORMATION_SPECIALIST_PREFIX`/`QH_DISCIPLINE_BARN_PREFIX`), plus six new ones for the Paso Fino/German Warmblood stables — `SHOW_BARN_PREFIX` keeps its identifier (it is still conceptually "the show barn" `stockShowBarn` mints breed-aware padding into) but its string value changed to `'Apples and Oats Ranch'`.
- **Not renamed, on purpose:** the show-venue name "Cedar Hollow" in `src/engines/showing/calendar.ts`'s venue list — a coincidental reuse of the same words for an unrelated concept (where a show physically happens, not who owns a stable), with no data-model link to the NPC stable of the same former name.
- Verified: `npm test` and `npx tsc --noEmit` both clean. Not verified against a live `wrangler dev`/D1 in this session.

**2026-08-04 — Acquired conditions (colic, laminitis, and the rest): specified, not built.** `docs/slices/0020-acquired-conditions.md` is the new document — a full slice specification, not a lighter data record like the two entries below it, because it needs new engine code, a schema change and two tick stages, not just new rows against existing machinery.

- **Two design calls were put to the operator directly before any of this was written**, the same way slice 0010's death-window question and slice 0017's stud-fee questions were: whether an ignored acute condition should carry real death risk (**yes, decided** — colic 40% untreated vs 5% treated; only 4 of the 12 conditions carry any death branch at all), and whether laminitis/navicular should be tied to the `foot_robustness`/`joint_robustness`/`ligament_robustness` substrate slice 0014 built ahead of use, or stay purely non-genetic this pass (**tied in, decided** — four of the twelve conditions read a robustness trait as an onset modifier).
- **One engine, two phases, twelve rows** — an `onsetProbability` function (care/workload/location/feed/robustness in, a per-game-day probability out) and a `rollOutcome` function (treated-or-not in, one of resolved/manageable/degenerative/death out), never one function per condition. Mirrors slice 0010's own founding choice not to write a function per single-gene condition.
- **A new `conditions.category` value (`acquired`) and a new `severity_class` value (`acute`)** — no migration needed for either, since neither column carries a `CHECK` constraint (confirmed against `migrations/0052`). `acute` is a genuinely new *shape* of consequence, not just a new value: every prior severity class was a fixed, foreknown outcome (GBED always dies on a snapshotted date); this one is a real, still-open event that resolves into one of the other three or clears entirely, which needed naming as its own thing rather than stretched to fit.
- **Onset probability is computed per game day, not per tick**, deliberately — `1 - (1-p_day)^daysSinceLastCheck` — so a world that ticks more often than once a game day never becomes riskier by accident, the same trap `computeUpkeep` already avoids by deriving its charge from `daysOwed`.
- **Robustness modifies onset only, not outcome severity** — a structurally weaker horse gets laminitis more often, not a worse case of it once it happens. Named explicitly in the document's own §12 as a real simplification worth revisiting, not a decision to forget was made.
- **Workload, for now, is show-entry count in a trailing 90-day window** — a deliberate, named departure from slice 0013 §3.4's own reasoning that workload "needs training to mean anything." The document argues a legible partial signal now beats none, and flags itself as due for replacement once a real training system exists.
- **Laminitis reads `feed_level` directly and gives Premium feed a real downside** — richer feed measurably raises laminitis onset risk. This is a direct answer to a risk slice 0013 §13.2 flagged about itself ("premium feed... may be worth it only for a stable that is already winning") rather than a coincidence.
- **No new heritable trait was added.** Colic, ulcers, tying-up, strangles, and three others have no robustness link at all — `TRAITS` is append-only and adding a trait changes every future horse's genotype forever, judged a bigger decision than this document needed to make.
- **`horse_conditions` gains an `outcome` column, distinct from `conditions.bars_showing`** — because whether a specific horse's incident became career-ending is a fact about that one incident, not a flat property of "laminitis" the condition. Show eligibility reads the per-incident column for the acquired set; the single-gene panel's flat `bars_showing` (HERDA) is untouched and still works the old way.
- **The document flags its own bug rather than shipping it silently**: §7.1 names a marker-column naming mismatch (`last_incident_check_tick_seq` needs to hold a game-day count, not a tick count, and should be renamed `last_incident_check_game_day` before build) and deliberately leaves the earlier wrong name in the spec text so a reviewer can see the discrepancy was already caught rather than freshly discovered.
- `CLAUDE.md` §10 gained a new row pointing at the document.

**2026-08-04 — The seven non-Quarter-Horse disease panels: specified, not built.** `docs/breed-disease-panels.md` is the new document, same "data record, not a built feature" status `docs/breed-ideal-vectors.md` already carries — nothing in `conditions`, `loci`, or any `founding_allele_pool` has actually changed.

- **Only single-gene conditions are specified**, because that is the only category with working machinery (slice 0010). Colour-linked conditions (melanoma via grey, MCOA via silver) and polygenic ones (laryngeal neuropathy, DSLD, insect bite hypersensitivity, osteochondrosis) are all listed and deferred with reasons in the document's §6, not built — they need `conditions.onset_model` (deliberately not built, slice 0010 §3.2) or, for MCOA, a Silver locus that does not exist yet.
- **Six new loci recommended: `SCID`, `CA`, `LFS` (Arabian), `WFFS` (German Warmblood), `DWARF`, `HYDRO` (Friesian)** — all real, named, single-gene equine conditions with identified genes, translated onto the existing `trigger` shape the same way HYPP/PSSM1/HERDA/GBED were. **Three of the seven breeds (Thoroughbred, Paso Fino, Icelandic) get no buildable single-gene condition at all**, and Nokota gets none deliberately (overview §4a's "None distinctive," the healthy landrace) — this isn't a gap in the research, it's what real equine veterinary genetics actually looks like for those breeds, and overview §3a predicted exactly this for the Thoroughbred by name.
- **After this is seeded, the game's lethal single-gene count becomes five** (GBED existing, plus SCID/LFS/WFFS/hydrocephalus) — landing exactly on overview §3b's own stated ceiling of "four or five across the whole game."
- **Unlike the ideal-vector document, this one has no show-barn blocker.** Disease panels create no show classes and touch no NPC stocking logic, so `docs/breed-disease-panels.md`'s contents are seedable independently, any time — the document's §8 lays out the (short) migration plan.
- **One question flagged for whoever builds it rather than decided here** (document §9): the game's lethal-foal death window (`lethal_foal_death_game_days`) is a single global config value, currently 30 game days, but SCID's real death timeline is months rather than days. The document's own lean is to reuse the shared window and disclose the compression honestly in the teaching text, rather than add a per-condition override column ahead of a second condition actually needing one — but says plainly this is a call for the implementing session, per `CLAUDE.md` §2.
- `CLAUDE.md` §10's "the other seven breeds" row updated to point at both documents.

**2026-08-04 — The consignment dealer now mints whatever breeds are in play, and its own separate breed allowlist is gone.** Migration `0109_config_remove_consignment_breed_codes.sql`. Follow-up to the same day's breed-ideal-vectors entry below, asked for directly once that entry's own build-log note ("the consignment dealer stocks Quarter Horses only... did not widen automatically") was read back.

- **The bug this closes**: `mintConsignmentBatch` (`src/db/consignment.ts`) picked eligible breeds as `getBreedsInPlay(env)` intersected with `config.values.consignment_breed_codes`, a second list seeded to `["QH"]` and never touched since. `breeds.enabled` (the `/admin/breeds` toggle) already meant "this breed is in play, admit new horses of it" everywhere else in the game — founding offers, admin horse creation, NPC breeding targets, new show classes, and (as of the same day) the NPC show barn. The dealer was the one place with a second, independent switch that had to be edited by hand to stay in sync, and nobody ever had a reason to until this session.
- **The fix removes the second switch rather than widening its default.** `eligibleBreeds` is now `getBreedsInPlay(env)` directly, no intersection. `consignment_breed_codes` is deleted from `Config['values']` (`src/lib/config-cache.ts`) and from the stored config JSON (`json_remove` in migration `0109`) rather than left as an unread key — CLAUDE.md §9's "don't leave a promise nobody kept" reasoning from slice 0010 applies just as well in reverse, to a control nothing reads anymore.
- **Consequence worth knowing, not acted on**: `consignment_batch_min`/`_max` (1-2 horses every `consignment_cadence_game_days` = 60, per migration `0101`'s slice-0019 retune) now spreads across up to eight breeds instead of one. A given breed's owners will see a consignment horse of their breed less often than a Quarter-Horse-only dealer produced them. Not tuned here — this change is "which breeds," not "how many, how often" — but worth watching the same way the show-classes-per-show balance note in the same day's other entry is.
- **Test coverage**: two new tests appended to `test/founding/consignment-d1.test.ts` (the existing `node:sqlite` harness, no new file needed) — thirty forced batches produce more than one distinct breed among the dealer's horses, and disabling a breed via `setBreedEnabled` stops the dealer minting it within twenty further batches. Both are regression guards for the exact drift this entry fixes, not just coverage for coverage's sake.
- **Docs touched**: `CLAUDE.md` §10's consignment-dealer row, `docs/horse-game-overview.md` §4a (the paragraph the same day's earlier entry already updated once), and `docs/slices/0017a-amendment-colour-testing-and-the-consignment-dealer.md` (§3 point 5, §5.8's config table, §6.2 and §6.5) marked superseded in place rather than rewritten, matching how slice 0012's own superseded sections were handled in the entry below.
- Verified: `npm test` (68 files, 577 tests) and `npx tsc --noEmit` both clean, including the new migration applying cleanly to a real SQLite database alongside all prior ones. **Not** verified against a live `wrangler dev` or real D1, the same standing constraint every prior entry in this log has noted. Before trusting this in play: apply migration `0109` at `/admin/migrations`, press "Mint a batch now" on `/admin/consignment` a few times, and confirm the minted horses' breeds vary rather than all reading Quarter Horse.

**2026-08-04 — The other seven breeds' ideal vectors seeded, five remaining disciplines seeded, and the NPC show barn made breed-aware.** Migrations `0107_seed_breed_ideal_vectors.sql` and `0108_seed_disciplines_remaining.sql`. Closes `docs/breed-ideal-vectors.md` §6.1's blocker and the last line of slice 0012's own status ("only Barrel Racing is seeded"). Together this is what the operator's "eight breeds" and "six disciplines" goals meant concretely.

- **`0107` is pure data**, the seven JSON blobs from `docs/breed-ideal-vectors.md` §3.3-§3.9 pasted verbatim into `UPDATE breeds SET ideal_vector = ... WHERE code = ...`, one statement per breed (AR, TB, GW, FR, PF, IC, NOK). `createShowIfMissing` (`src/db/shows.ts`) already creates one class per breed in play with a non-null `ideal_vector`, so this alone turns every show from one Quarter Horse class into eight breed classes — no code change there, exactly as that document's §6.1 said it would be.
- **`0108` is also pure data**: Flat Racing, Show Jumping, Endurance, Dressage and Gaited Pleasure, using slice 0012 §5.1's weights, §5.4's minimum ages, and §6.5's per-discipline `default_noise_sd` estimates. `show_discipline_classes_per_show` (migration `0066`) was already seeded at 6, which now exactly covers all six disciplines with none rotated out — no config change needed either.
- **The one real code change: `stockShowBarn` (`src/db/npc.ts`) stopped hardcoding Quarter Horses.** This was `docs/breed-ideal-vectors.md` §6.2 step 1's named blocker — without it, seven of the eight new breed classes would have had zero NPC padding, and a class with one player entry is a class of one, winning a national-sounding ribbon by being the only owner of a breed. It now loops every breed `getBreedsInPlay` returns with a non-null `ideal_vector` and tops each one up to `targetSize` independently, same idempotent top-up-the-shortfall shape as before, just per breed instead of once overall. New helper `countAliveHorsesByBreedForStable(env, stableId)` in `src/db/horses.ts`, the stable-scoped sibling of the existing game-wide `countAliveHorsesByBreed`.
- **No decision was made about empty classes** (`docs/breed-ideal-vectors.md` §6.2 step 2's "worth deciding rather than inheriting"). Left alone on purpose, following the precedent slice 0012 §5.5 already set for Gaited Pleasure: a class nobody entered is never topped up from the barn (`judgeOneClass`'s existing rule) and simply judges zero entries; a thin class is strictly better than no class at all, and gating creation on ownership was more machinery than this change needed.
- **`/admin/shows`'s barn panel changed shape**, not just its numbers: a single blended count against one target stopped meaning anything once the barn holds eight breeds' worth of horses instead of one. It's now a table, one row per breed in play with an ideal vector (`AdminBarnBreedCount`, `renderShowsAdminPage`'s new `barnByBreed` param, replacing the old `barnCount`), and the below-target warning names which breeds are short rather than a single number. `countAliveHorses` (the old single-stable-total helper) is no longer imported by `src/routes/admin.ts` at all — only the per-breed one is needed there now.
- **Cedar Hollow and Willow Creek Barrels (the two NPC personality stables) were left untouched.** They exist to model a *policy* (a conformation specialist, a discipline barn), not to pad every class's field — that job already belonged to Fair Meadow via `listNpcStableHorses`, which pools every `is_npc` stable's stock regardless of which one it came from. Making the show barn breed-aware was sufficient; nothing about slice 0015's personality stables needed to change.
- **Balance risk worth watching, not acted on**: slice 0012 §13.1/§13.2 already flagged that six discipline classes plus one breed class would strain the action budget and the prize faucet. This change adds seven *more* breed classes on top of that — a show can now carry up to fourteen classes total (eight breed + six discipline) against the same `actions_per_tick` and `show_prize_schedule` slice 0012 was already worried about at seven. Left alone deliberately (this change is data-and-one-function, not a rebalancing pass) but flagged here so the first real week of eight-breed, six-discipline play gets watched for exactly the symptom those two sections describe: `actions_per_tick`, `show_discipline_classes_per_show`, and `show_prize_schedule` are the three levers already named, all live config, no deploy needed.
- **What `docs/breed-ideal-vectors.md` §6.3 still lists as unbuilt is still unbuilt**: `eligible_class_types`/`discipline_aptitudes` (no breed gating on disciplines, unchanged from slice 0012 §2.1's deliberate choice), `height_range`/`weight_range`, and disease panels for the seven non-Quarter-Horse breeds. None of those blocked this change; none of them are touched by it.
- **Test coverage**: `test/genetics/consistency.test.ts`'s discipline-seed and ideal-vector blocks now scan both migrations of each pair (`0063`+`0108`, `0035`+`0107`) instead of just the first, so a future retune of either set is checked the same way the LOCI/TRAITS blocks above them already are. Added slice 0012 §5.2's full "every ability trait is the strict maximum weight in at least one discipline" property, which only holds for the complete six-discipline set and couldn't be checked while only Barrel Racing existed. Added a weak version of `docs/breed-ideal-vectors.md` §5's "keep targets off 50" rule (at least one of a breed's four targets is not exactly 50) so a future retune can't silently reintroduce the inbreeding-rewards-itself hole. New D1 harness file `test/npc/show-barn-d1.test.ts` (same `node:sqlite` pattern as `test/market/sale-d1.test.ts` — see that file's header — added to `tsconfig.json`'s exclude list for the same reason every prior file in this pattern is there) exercises `stockShowBarn` against a real migrated database: all eight breeds get topped to target, stocking twice is idempotent, and a partially-stocked breed only gets its shortfall minted.
- Verified: `npm test` (68 files, 575 tests) and `npx tsc --noEmit` both clean, including both new migrations applying cleanly to a real SQLite database alongside all prior ones (via `test/reset-d1.test.ts`'s full-migration-folder harness, which needed no changes to pick the new files up). **Not** verified against a live `wrangler dev` or real D1 — no Cloudflare account/login in this sandbox, the same standing constraint every prior entry in this log has noted. Before trusting this in play: apply the two new migrations at `/admin/migrations`, press "Stock the show barn" on `/admin/shows` and confirm all eight breeds appear in the new table, and watch the next show actually carry fourteen classes.

**2026-08-04 — Slice 0019, founding specialists: built, Parts A/B/C only, other seven breeds' ideal vectors left for a follow-up.** Every founding candidate (`generateCandidate`, `src/engines/founding/generate.ts`) now gets one conformation specialist trait (near its breed's `ideal_vector` target, ±1 allele) and one ability specialist trait (potential 15/20 ±1), each overwritten *after* the existing Mendelian/polygenic loops finish, from two brand-new RNG stream labels (`specialist_choice_conformation`/`specialist_alleles_conformation` and the `_ability` pair) — never resampled, so no existing draw sequence moves (CLAUDE.md §5.2, slice §7). `specialistTraits: { conformation, ability }` is returned alongside the genotype so the choice is directly testable rather than inferred.

Operator decisions (asked via the four open questions in §9 that mattered — Q1/Q2 tuning, Q3 one-per-side vs two-conformation, Q5 batch coverage, Q6 seed the other breeds now):
- **±1 allele slack, ability specialist at 15/20** — the slice's own measured defaults, used as-is.
- **One conformation + one ability specialist per horse** (§5's default), not two conformation specialists.
- **Batch coverage across a batch's 6 candidates left to chance** — not built, §9 Q5's "not modelled" guess left undecided rather than picked for the operator.
- **The other seven breeds' `ideal_vector`s (Q6, `docs/breed-ideal-vectors.md`) left for a separate follow-up** — Part A is a no-op for any breed without one (still seven of eight), by design (slice §3's "if the breed has no ideal_vector, skip Part A entirely").

**Part B's eligible-trait set caught a real inconsistency in the slice document.** §4.2 defines the rule as "nonzero weight in at least one enabled discipline," then two sentences later says "today that is agility, speed and trainability" — but Barrel Racing's own seeded weights (migration 0063) give `stamina` a weight of 0.2, which *is* nonzero. The prose undercounts by one trait. `docs/analysis/stable-timeline.mjs` — named in the slice itself as the acceptance test — settles it: its `AT_LIVE` filter is `ABIL[t] > 0`, which includes stamina. Built to the literal rule and the measured script, not the prose: `getSpecializableAbilityTraits` (`src/db/disciplines.ts`, new) returns `[stamina, speed, trainability, agility]` today, not just the three the prose names. `jump_scope` (weight 0.0) is the only one actually excluded.

**New pieces:**
- `src/db/disciplines.ts`'s `getSpecializableAbilityTraits(env)` — `ABILITY_TRAITS` filtered to a nonzero weight in ≥1 *enabled* discipline's `ability_weights`, following `getBreedsInPlay`'s own shape (one helper, every call site reads it, widens by itself the day a second discipline is seeded).
- `config.founding_ability_specialist_potential` (15) and `consignment_cadence_game_days` dropped 90 → 60 (Part C, §6) — `migrations/0101_config_founding_specialists.sql`. Both stay live tunables (CLAUDE.md §5.5), editable at `/admin/config` afterwards.
- `src/db/founding.ts`'s `chooseBreedForOffer` and `src/db/consignment.ts`'s `mintConsignmentBatch`/`mintConsignmentCandidate` both now parse the breed's `ideal_vector` (`parseIdealVector`, `src/engines/showing/score.ts`) and call `getSpecializableAbilityTraits` once per offer/batch, threading both into every `generateCandidate` call. The consignment dealer needed no new specialist logic at all — it inherits Parts A/B for free, exactly as §6 says, only a widened inline type on `eligibleBreeds` to carry `ideal_vector` through.

**Re-ran `docs/analysis/stable-timeline.mjs` with the chosen numbers** (its defaults — `SPEC_SLACK=1`, `ABIL_TARGET=15` — already matched the operator's choices, so no constants changed):

| milestone | as built | +1 conformation specialist | + Part C (cadence 60) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 6 good horses (conformation, ≥90) | 1.2y, 133/200 | 353d, 137/200 | **199d, 194/200** |
| 6 good horses (barrels, dominate ≥70) | 1.8y, 124/200 | — | **221d, 200/200** |
| 6 good horses (barrels, elite ≥78 — long game) | — | — | 1.7y, 139/200 |

Matches §2's table exactly — both lines land at 6½–7½ months, inside the operator's 6–8 month brief, and the elite bar stays a long way off on purpose.

New tests: `test/founding/generate.test.ts` gained a regression test that both specialists active leaves every *other* trait's bits identical to the slice-0014 golden values at seed 777777 (the direct test for §7's failure mode), determinism tests for each Part, the "no ideal vector skips Part A" / "empty eligible list skips Part B" identity checks, a jump_scope-never-chosen sweep, and a throw test for a missing `abilitySpecialistPotential`. New `test/db/disciplines.test.ts` covers `getSpecializableAbilityTraits` itself with a minimal fake `Env` — note its cache in `disciplines.ts` is module-scoped, so each test there calls `vi.resetModules()` and re-imports fresh rather than relying on a shared instance across `it()` blocks.
- Verified: `npm test` (65 files, 531 tests) and `npx tsc --noEmit` both clean. **Not** verified against a live `wrangler dev` or real D1 — no Cloudflare account/login in this sandbox, the same standing constraint every entry in this log has noted. The operator confirmed via a screenshot mid-session that `/admin/migrations` had `0100_config_npc_buying.sql` as its last applied migration, confirming `0101` was the correct next number.

**2026-08-03 — Bug fix: a full world reset deleted the Consignment Yard and never brought it back.** `migrations/0097_consignment_dealer_stable.sql` inserts the dealer's `stables` row once, on deploy, the same way `0040`/`0085` insert Fair Meadow/Cedar Hollow/Willow Creek Barrels — but unlike those three, `src/db/reset.ts`'s full-world-reset branch never re-inserted it after its own blanket `DELETE FROM stables`. `runConsignments` treats a missing dealer as "migration not yet applied" and quietly returns, so after any `/admin/reset` (world scope) the dealer was gone for good and no batch ever minted again, with no error anywhere to notice by. Fixed by adding the same two-statement pattern (`stables` + `stable_prefix_history`, prefix from `CONSIGNMENT_DEALER_PREFIX` in `src/db/consignment.ts`) the other three NPC stables already get, **deliberately with no `npc_policy` row**, matching `0097`'s own comment that the absence is what keeps the dealer out of `runNpcBreedingDecisions`/`runNpcMarketListings`. New test file `test/reset-d1.test.ts` (fourth use of the `node:sqlite` real-database harness — see `test/market/sale-d1.test.ts`'s header — added to `tsconfig.json`'s exclude list for the same reason the other three are there) asserts the dealer stable exists after a reset, has no policy row, and that `runConsignments` successfully mints a batch on the very next call.
- Verified: `npm test` (61 files, 495 tests — 2 new in `test/reset-d1.test.ts`) and `npx tsc --noEmit` both clean. **Not** verified against a live `wrangler dev` or real D1 — no Cloudflare account/login in this sandbox, the same standing constraint every session in this log has noted.

**2026-08-02 — No JavaScript anywhere in this codebase, until a slice names a specific case.** Every page is server-rendered HTML from the Worker (`src/render/`, built with the `html` tagged-template helper in `src/lib/html.ts`). Interactivity that would normally reach for `onclick`/`confirm()` or similar is done with plain HTML instead — e.g. the manual tick-advance control (`src/render/admin.ts`) uses a `required` checkbox the operator must tick, not a JS confirm dialog, and the server re-checks that checkbox's value before acting. If a future slice genuinely needs client-side JS (a live-updating widget, a richer form), add it deliberately and say so in that slice's summary and here — don't let it creep in one `onclick` at a time.

**2026-08-02 — `src/lib/rng.ts`: seeded randomness.** `makeRng(seed): Rng` gives `{ next(), int(maxExclusive), pick(items), shuffle(items), normal(mean, sd) }`. `deriveSeed(parentSeed, label)` makes a deterministic sub-seed — call this, never `makeRng` twice from the same stored seed. `randomSeed()` is the *only* place `crypto.getRandomValues` may be called, and only to mint a brand-new seed to store on a new row (horses, pregnancies, shows, …) — never to make a random decision directly. There is a golden-value test in `test/rng.test.ts` asserting exact output for `makeRng(12345)` and `deriveSeed(12345, "genotype")`. If you ever have a reason to touch the RNG algorithm itself, that test will fail — treat that failure as the whole game's stored history becoming unreproducible, not as a test to update.

**2026-08-02 — `src/lib/time.ts`: the wall clock.** `localParts(utcSeconds, timeZone)` returns `{ year, month, day, hour, minute, dateKey, minutesOfDay }` via `Intl.DateTimeFormat`. `formatLocal` for display, `parseSlot("HH:MM")` for minutes-since-midnight, `nowUtcSeconds()` for the current instant. Never compute offsets by hand. `src/tick/slot.ts`'s `decideNextSlot` is the pure function that decides which tick slot (if any) is due — it takes `SlotState` + `CurrentLocal` and returns a decision; it does not touch the database, which is what makes `test/tick-slots.test.ts` possible without one.

**2026-08-02 — `src/lib/html.ts`: the templating helper.** `` html`...` `` escapes every interpolated value by default; wrap already-safe HTML in `raw(...)` to inline it unescaped (used for nesting one `html` result inside another, and only for that). Every route in `src/render/` builds its page through `pageShell()` in `src/render/layout.ts`, which renders the game-day header, the nav, and the paused banner.

**2026-08-02 — signed-cookie sessions (`src/lib/session.ts`, `src/lib/cookies.ts`).** No sessions table, per CLAUDE.md §13. `hh_session` is `<accountId>.<issuedAt>.<hmac>`, HMAC-SHA256 over `env.SESSION_SECRET`, `HttpOnly; Secure; SameSite=Lax`, re-issued once it's a day old. `hh_stable` is the same pattern holding the selected stable id — it is a UI convenience only; every stable-scoped route (`src/routes/stables.ts`) re-reads the stable from the account_id on the row and 404s if it isn't owned by the logged-in account, never trusting the cookie alone.

**2026-08-02 — the prefix registry (`stable_prefix_history`).** Every prefix ever claimed, current or retired, is a row here, with the unique index living on `prefix`, not `stable_id`. Creating a stable and renaming one both write to this table and `stables.prefix` atomically via a single `env.DB.batch([...])` (D1 batches are one implicit transaction — any statement failing rolls the whole thing back). Creation uses SQLite's `last_insert_rowid()` inside the batch to link the new `stable_prefix_history` row to the just-inserted `stables` row, which is why `stable_id` could stay `NOT NULL` as the schema in the slice document specifies, rather than the insert-history-row-first-with-a-null-stable_id flow the document narrates — same guarantee (a taken prefix, live or retired, rejects the whole operation), true atomicity, no relaxed constraint. See `src/db/stables.ts`.

**2026-08-02 — The operator has no terminal, at all.** Confirmed directly by the person running the project: they cannot run `npm`, `wrangler`, or any other CLI command — console/dashboard/browser only (Cloudflare dashboard, GitHub's web file editor, the deployed site itself). Recorded in CLAUDE.md §1. This makes the migration workflow in `README.md` §6 (`npx wrangler login`, `npm run migrate:remote`) something they cannot personally do — flagged there and in §1 as unresolved. If you're building a slice that would hand the operator a CLI step, stop and find a dashboard/browser equivalent instead, or say plainly in your summary that someone else with terminal access will need to do it.

**2026-08-02 — `/admin/migrations`: applying migrations from the browser, no terminal.** Built directly in response to the operator hitting exactly the failure mode this exists to fix: a brand-new D1 database with no tables yet, so `/setup` (and every other page) 500'd with "Something went wrong." `src/db/migrations.ts` bundles every file in `/migrations` into the Worker as text — see the `[[rules]]` entry in `wrangler.toml`, which tells the bundler to treat `*.sql` imports as raw strings. **The glob had to be `**/*.sql`, not `migrations/*.sql`** — wrangler's rule matcher (`glob-to-regexp`, no `globstar` option) tests the *literal import specifier text* (`../../migrations/0001_world.sql`), not a resolved path, and without globstar even a single `*` compiles to `.*` — so the glob just needs `*` somewhere before `migrations/`, and `**/*.sql` is the clearest way to write that. Confirmed by actually running `wrangler dev --local` end to end (fresh DB → apply migrations → `/setup` succeeds → the route locks down to admin-only once an account exists), not just by reading the bundler source.

Each migration's SQL is split into individual statements by `src/lib/sql.ts`'s `splitSqlStatements` (strip `--` line comments, split on `;`) rather than passed to D1's `exec()`, which requires newline-separated statements and breaks on our pretty-printed multi-line `CREATE TABLE`s (see the comment in `sql.ts` and the workers-sdk issue it links). Each migration's statements plus its own tracking-row insert run through `env.DB.batch()` — the same atomic-batch pattern `createStableWithPrefix` already uses — so a migration lands completely or not at all.

The tracking table is literally `d1_migrations`, with the exact column schema and bare-filename `name` values that `wrangler d1 migrations apply` itself uses (confirmed by reading `wrangler`'s own source, since the Cloudflare docs site 403's this environment's fetch tool). This was deliberate, not incidental: it means the browser path and the CLI path (`npm run migrate:remote`) share one history and can be freely mixed, in either order, without redoing or fighting each other.

The route (`src/routes/migrations.ts`) bypasses `buildContext` entirely, the same way `/health` does, because `buildContext` loads the `world` and `config` rows and throws if those tables don't exist yet — which is exactly the state this route has to work in. Its own auth check treats "the `accounts` table doesn't exist, or has zero rows" as open-to-anyone, the same trust model `/setup` already uses (whoever gets there first is trusted, because nobody could possibly be logged in yet either way); once a first account exists, it requires a valid admin session like every other `/admin` route.

**2026-08-02 — PBKDF2 iteration count: 100,000, not yet measured on a live deploy.** `src/lib/password.ts` uses 100,000 PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 iterations, per the slice document's starting point. This session built and tested the app locally (`wrangler dev` / Miniflare) but has no live Cloudflare account to deploy to, so the CPU-time measurement the slice document asks for (§6.1: log in on the deployed Worker, check the CPU time in the dashboard) has **not** been done. Whoever does the first real deploy: log in once, check the Worker's CPU time in the dashboard, and if it's close to or over the free tier's 10ms ceiling, drop `ITERATIONS` in `src/lib/password.ts` to 50,000 — then update this line with what you measured and what you did.

**2026-08-02 — UI display conventions: admin as a separate mode, nested nav, a real token system.** Built directly in response to operator feedback ahead of phase 2: the admin link sat in the same nav row as gameplay links with no visual distinction, there was no pattern for anything beyond a flat top nav before the site inevitably outgrows one, and the styling was two flat colors with no hierarchy. All in `src/render/layout.ts` and `public/style.css`, no JS added.

- `pageShell()` takes a `section?: 'player' | 'admin'` param. `'admin'` swaps the header to a visually distinct treatment (`--color-admin`, a deep indigo, versus `--color-primary` green) with its own label ("Admin panel") and a "← Back to your stables" link in place of the ordinary nav, so an admin page can't be mistaken for a gameplay page at a glance. On player pages where `isAdmin` is true, `/admin` is no longer inline with `Stables` — it's a separate pill (`.admin-chip`) pushed to the far right, visually set apart rather than sitting as a peer option. See `src/render/admin.ts`'s `shell()` for the pattern every admin route follows.
- `pageShell()` also takes an optional `subnav: NavLink[]` — a second menu bar under the header, section-local (admin's Accounts/Config/World clock/Migrations; a stable's Overview/Change prefix), active page highlighted. This is the pattern for growing a site section instead of cramming everything into the primary nav or one page. Build an `xSubnav(active)` helper next to a render module's page functions, the way `adminSubnav()` and `stableSubnav()` already do.
- Dense, secondary content collapses behind native `<details><summary>` — zero JS, per the no-JS convention above. `.section-collapse` for a page-level block (e.g. "Create an account", "Recent ticks" — default-closed unless there's a reason to default-open, like a validation error that needs to stay visible), `.row-actions` for a table row's per-row actions tucked behind a "Manage" toggle instead of inline forms crowding the row. `<td>`/`<th>` are `vertical-align: top` specifically so an opened `.row-actions` panel doesn't drag a row's other cells to its vertical center.
- `public/style.css` now opens with a `:root` token block (surface/text/brand/semantic colors, radii, a card shadow) and the rest of the file is organized into labeled sections (tokens → base → header/nav → subnav → layout → forms → buttons → tables → cards → badges → collapsibles). Reuse these custom properties rather than hardcoding hex — that's the point of writing them down, since sessions building later slices won't remember this one. New: `.badge`/`.badge-success`/`.badge-warning`/`.badge-danger` for status pills — slice 0002's genetics UI will want these for carrier/clear/affected states rather than inventing its own.
- Deliberately did not add a second typeface or any image asset — visual hierarchy comes from the color tokens, card elevation, and type weight/spacing instead. If a future slice wants a real display font for headings, it should be a self-hosted static file in `/public` (never a Google Fonts / CDN `<link>` — that's a hosted third-party dependency, ruled out by CLAUDE.md §3), added deliberately and noted here.
- Still one stylesheet, per the file's own long-standing comment.
- Testing note for whoever runs `wrangler dev --local` next: it needs a `.dev.vars` file with `SESSION_SECRET=<anything>` (gitignored, not committed) or every login/setup POST 500s trying to sign a cookie with an empty key. Also, `serializeCookie` in `src/lib/cookies.ts` marks cookies `Secure`, which Chromium will not persist over plain `http://localhost` across a form-submit navigation (fine on the real HTTPS deploy) — if you're driving the app with a browser automation tool locally, log in via curl and inject the resulting `hh_session` cookie into the browser context rather than trusting the form login to stick.

**2026-08-02 — Genetics core (slice 0002): the genotype blob, the missing-locus rule, and one deviation from a literal reading of it.** `horses.genotype` is JSON, shape `{ v: 1, mendelian: { <locus code>: [allele, allele] }, polygenic: { <trait code>: "<20 chars of 0/1>" } }` — see `src/engines/genetics/genotype.ts`. `v` is the blob's own schema version; bump it and handle both shapes if the shape ever changes, rather than migrating every horse's JSON. A **missing** locus or trait key is legal (a horse born before that gene existed) and is read via `getMendelianPair`/`getPolygenicString`, never `genotype.mendelian[code]` directly. **Every iteration over loci goes through `LOCI` in `src/engines/genetics/loci.ts`, in that array's order — never `Object.keys(...)`** — this is load-bearing for reproducibility (CLAUDE.md §5.2): the RNG draws one allele per locus in sequence. `LOCI`'s `alleles` tuple is each locus's canonical storage order (a stored pair is always sorted into it via `sortAllelePair`); its separate `wildType` field is the missing-locus default. **Those two are not always the same allele** — `§4.2` of the slice document describes the default as "the last allele in canonical order," which holds for four of the five loci, but DMRT3's canonical order is fixed by real-world convention as `["C","A"]` even though `A` is the rare (2%) gait mutation and `C` is the common wild type. Defaulting a legacy horse to `alleles[1]` would have silently made it gaited; `wildType` is spelled out per-locus instead. Flagging this because it's a literal disagreement with one sentence of the slice document, resolved in the document's own favor of intent (a missing locus must never grant a trait) rather than its literal mechanism.

Sub-seed labels in use, all via `deriveSeed` (never a second `makeRng` from a stored seed): `mendelian_sire`, `mendelian_dam`, `polygenic_sire`, `polygenic_dam`, `sex` (all derived from a foal's own `rng_seed` in `combine`/`breedNow`), and `founder_polygenic` (derived from a founding horse's own `rng_seed` in `createFoundingHorse`). `birth_noise` is reserved — not drawn anywhere yet — for whichever slice first expresses polygenic potential; see the comment in `src/engines/genetics/polygenic.ts`.

**COI uses the tabular kinship recursion, not Wright's path method** (`src/engines/genetics/pedigree.ts`'s `kinship`/`coefficientOfInbreeding`) — exact, only needs parent pointers and each ancestor's own stored `coi`. `horse_ancestors.path_count` **does not feed the COI at all**; it exists only so "this ancestor appears N times in the pedigree" can be displayed. `db/horses.ts`'s `loadPedigreeContext(env, sireId, damId)` is the two-query load behind both the breeding preview and the actual birth — one query for every ancestor id either parent has, one for those horses' `(id, sire_id, dam_id, coi)` rows — which is what guarantees the number shown before confirming a pairing is the number the foal is actually born with. Reuse it rather than writing a third way to load the same subgraph.

**Referencing a just-inserted row from several dependent inserts in the same D1 batch:** `last_insert_rowid()` (used by `createStableWithPrefix`) only stays correct for a *single* statement immediately after the insert it refers to — it goes stale the moment another insert runs. Inserting a foal needs to write a variable number of `horse_ancestors` rows all pointing at the just-inserted foal, so each one uses `(SELECT id FROM horses ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT 1)` instead — safe because a D1 batch is one transaction and nothing else can insert into `horses` in the middle of it. Reach for this pattern, not `last_insert_rowid()`, whenever more than one dependent statement needs the same freshly-minted id. (Slice 0003 update: this logic now lives in `buildFoalInsertStatements` in `src/db/horses.ts`, not `breedNow` — see the 2026-08-03 entry below. `breedNow` itself is deleted.)

**2026-08-02 — Breeds: the list is now eight, a breed code is effectively permanent, and "Paint" is a display alias rather than a breed.** All three decided in conversation and written up in `docs/horse-game-overview.md` §4/§4c — read those before building any breed work. The build-relevant parts:

- **A breed's `code` is written into every horse's `composition` blob at birth** (`{"QH": 1}` — see `createFoundingHorse` and `breedNow` in `src/db/horses.ts`). Changing a code after horses exist means a migration rewriting every horse's `composition`. Treat a code as permanent once seeded, and get it confirmed rather than inventing one: `QH`, plus `FR` (Friesian) and `NOK` (Nokota) as agreed. There is no Paint code.
- **Nothing in this codebase branches on breed identity, and it should stay that way.** Every breed-aware screen loops over `getBreeds()` rows. When a breed needs behaviour — like Paint's display alias, or an ideal vector — that behaviour is a **column on the `breeds` row**, not a branch in code. If you find yourself typing `if (breed.code === '...')`, the thing you want is a field on the row instead. Design record §12's rule 6 ("breeds, loci, conditions … are rows") is the same point from the other side.
- **Adding a locus later is designed-for and safe.** The missing-locus rule (`getMendelianPair`) means horses born before a gene existed need no backfill, and `inheritance.ts`'s per-parent-per-system RNG streams mean adding a locus does not shift an unrelated system's draws. The five loci that exist are a slicing artifact, not the intended total — the design expects roughly sixteen. So a new breed's colour genetics are usually *already on the roadmap*; the only genes that genuinely scale with breed count are the per-breed disease panels.

**`vitest.config.ts` (new file):** Vitest runs migration files through Vite's module pipeline, which doesn't understand `.sql` imports the way wrangler's own `[[rules]]` bundler does. A small inline plugin exports each `.sql` file's raw text as its default export, test-only — it has no effect on the real Worker build. Needed once `src/db/migrations.ts` (which every session's migration list lives in) became reachable from a test, in `test/genetics/consistency.test.ts`.

**2026-08-03 — Slice 0003 (pregnancy, heat and fertility) built. `breedNow` is deleted; coverings and pregnancies are real tables; two new tick stages.** Followed `docs/slices/0003-breeding-and-fertility.md` closely; nothing in it was disagreed with. What a future session needs to know:

- **`src/engines/breeding/` is new** (`fertility.ts`, `cycle.ts`, `season.ts`, `twins.ts`) — pure functions, no DB access, same pattern as `src/engines/genetics/`. `fertility.ts`'s `fertilityPotential(horse, geneMin, geneMax)` is the one function in the whole codebase allowed to read a horse's genotype for a purpose an NPC or a viewer must never see (slice 0003 §5) — it is called from exactly one place, `src/db/coverings.ts`'s `resolveOneCovering`, and nowhere else. If you ever find yourself wanting to call it from a route or a render function, stop — that's the truth/knowledge line (CLAUDE.md §12) and you're about to cross it.
- **The fertility trait's missing-value rule is its own function, not the shared one.** `getPolygenicString` (slice 0002) reads a missing trait as all zeros, which is correct for a conformation trait and would read every horse alive before this slice as almost sterile. `fertilityPotential` checks for the key itself and falls back to `deriveSeed(horse.rng_seed, 'fertility_legacy')` instead — a stable, well-distributed stand-in per horse, never a guess. Do not call `getPolygenicString`/`potential(...)` for `'fertility'` directly anywhere; always go through `fertilityPotential`.
- **The oestrous cycle is measured in ticks (`horses.cycle_anchor_tick_seq`), never game days** — slice 0003 §2 explains why (a real 21-day cycle aliases against a 10-game-day tick). `src/engines/breeding/cycle.ts`'s `cyclePhase`/`isInSeason`/`ticksUntilNextEstrus` are the only place that arithmetic happens. Rolled once per mare, from her own seed via `deriveSeed(seed, 'cycle_slot')`, at creation (`createFoundingHorse`) or at foaling (`db/pregnancies.ts`'s `foalOnePregnancy`) — never re-rolled except at foal heat, when the tick resets a dam's own anchor to `tickSeq + 1` after she foals.
- **A covering (the mating event) and a pregnancy (one row per foal) are different tables**, per slice 0003 §6 — a covering can produce zero, one or two pregnancies (twins). Booking (`db/coverings.ts`'s `bookCovering`) is a plain insert from the breeding route. Resolution is the tick's first breeding stage, `resolveDueCoverings`: finds every booked covering whose mare is in season this tick (done in SQL — `cycle_anchor_tick_seq` is always ≤ `tickSeq`, so a plain `%` is safe), rolls conception, and on success rolls twins and writes one or two pregnancies — covering-status-update and pregnancy-inserts land in one `env.DB.batch()` together, which matters: without that atomicity, a crash between the two either strands a conceived covering with no pregnancy, or (on retry) double-creates the pregnancy, because the covering's `status` column is what makes the whole stage idempotent (CLAUDE.md §5.4).
- **Genetics are rolled at conception, stored on the pregnancy (`rolled_genotype`, `rolled_coi`), and only written into a `horses` row at foaling** (slice 0003 §3.9-§3.10) — `foal_rng_seed` is minted at conception and becomes `horses.rng_seed` unchanged. `src/db/horses.ts`'s `buildFoalInsertStatements` is what `breedNow` used to do inline; it now just writes down genetics that were already decided, and is shared by the tick's foaling stage (`db/pregnancies.ts`'s `foalDuePregnancies`). If a later slice adds a second way to create a foal (e.g. slice 0004's frozen semen), reuse this function rather than inlining another `INSERT INTO horses`.
- **Twins are two independent rolls off the covering's own seed** (`double_ovulation`, `twin_continue` — `src/engines/breeding/twins.ts`'s `rollTwins`), and when they happen, each twin gets its own freshly-minted `foal_rng_seed` and its own independent genotype and gestation-length draw — not identical twins, and not necessarily born on the same day (verified in local testing: one twin foaled 11 game days after the other). The admin "force next twins" control (`/admin/breeding`) is a one-shot `config.flags.force_next_twins` boolean, consumed by whichever covering is the *first to actually conceive* after it's set (not the first to resolve) — if conception fails, the flag stays armed for the next attempt.
- **New sub-seed labels**, all via `deriveSeed`: from `horses.rng_seed` → `cycle_slot`, `fertility_legacy`; from `coverings.rng_seed` → `conception`, `double_ovulation`, `twin_continue`; from `pregnancies.rng_seed` → `gestation`; from `pregnancies.foal_rng_seed` → the slice 0002 labels, unchanged, plus `cycle_slot` (reused for a mare foal's own cycle roll at foaling).
- **The conception estimate shown to a player is not the number rolled**, deliberately (slice 0003 §5). `db/coverings.ts`'s `estimateConceptionChance` calls the same `conceptionChance` engine function the real roll uses, but with both fertility factors fixed at `1.0` — a subfertile mare's owner finds out by missing, not by reading her genotype. Never wire the breeding screen up to `fertilityPotential` directly.
- **Migrations 0017-0021.** 0021's backfill (`cycle_anchor_tick_seq` for mares that predate this slice) uses `rng_seed % estrous_cycle_ticks` rather than the real `deriveSeed('cycle_slot')` algorithm, because a plain-SQL migration has no access to the JS RNG. Deliberate simplification, documented in the migration itself — still deterministic and evenly spread, which is all a one-time backfill needs.
- **`/admin/breeding`** (new admin subpage, in the existing admin subnav pattern) is read-only display of the live tunables plus the force-twins control — no editing form, per CLAUDE.md §13 ("no polished admin UI"). If a later slice wants these tunables editable from the browser, extend `/admin/config` rather than inventing a second config-editing UI.
- Verified end-to-end against a local `wrangler dev` + `d1 migrations apply --local`: booking, both refusal paths (out of season, recovering), conception, forced twins (two independently-gened foals, different due days), foaling, pedigree/COI/prefix on the resulting foal, the in-season barn badge, and the admin breeding page.

**2026-08-02 — Slice 0005 (founding stock, eight breeds) built for §3-§6 and §8-§11 of `docs/slices/0005-founding-stock.md`; §7 (the parent's PIN) deferred to a follow-up slice, per that document's own §13 split.** This is the state as of the `154c08a` commit — this entry documents it because that commit didn't update this file itself.

- **What exists now.** `src/engines/founding/` (`pool.ts`, `generate.ts`, `names.ts`) is the pure generator — Hardy-Weinberg draws per locus from a breed's `founding_allele_pool`, band-weighted polygenic draws (`pool_polygenic`, deliberately a different sub-seed label from the admin form's `founder_polygenic`), and the origin-prefix/name lists. `src/db/founding.ts` holds `mintOffer`, `chooseBreedForOffer` and `claimOffer`, each landing its writes in one `env.DB.batch()` (mint is a plain insert; breed choice writes the candidates + `breed_id` together so it can't be rerolled; claim writes the horse inserts, the candidate `chosen`/`horse_id` updates, and `status = 'claimed'` together). Seven breeds beyond `QH` are seeded (`AR`, `TB`, `PF`, `IC`, `GW`, `FR`, `NOK` — codes exactly as listed in the slice doc §5.2, now permanent per that section's own warning) with colour/gait pools only.
- **How a batch reaches a player.** An admin mints one at `/admin/founding` (pick a stable, pick a quality band — `mid` is the founding default). The stable then shows a "New horses" entry in its subnav and a callout on its home page (`src/render/stables.ts`'s `stableSubnav`/`renderStableHomePage`, gated on `hasWaitingFoundingOffer`) linking to `/stables/:id/founding` (`src/render/founding.ts`), which walks the four states from the slice doc §11: no offer, pending (breed picker with a required confirmation checkbox — the choice is final), open (candidate list, tick 2 mares + 1 stallion of 6), claimed (link to the barn). **There is no in-stable way to request or roll a batch** — a stable only ever displays and acts on an offer an admin already minted.
- **`createFoundingHorse` (in `db/horses.ts`) changed shape**, per slice doc §6.6: it now takes a full pre-built `Genotype` and an explicit `rngSeed` rather than generating polygenic values itself. The admin founder form (`/admin/horses/new`) moved its `generateFounderPolygenic` call out to the route, which is where CLAUDE.md §5.1's pure-engine split says it belonged anyway. `buildFoundingHorseInsertStatement` is the shared insert both the admin form and a founding claim now go through.
- **What's deferred: all of slice doc §7, the parent's PIN.** No `accounts.pin_hash` column, no `pin_attempts` table, no `decidePinAttempt` lockout function, and no in-session "a grown-up can add horses here" `<details>` block on the stable page or founding page. The chore-reward loop the PIN exists for still works today, just via an admin login at `/admin/founding` rather than a PIN typed on a child's own phone — the slice doc §13 explicitly sanctions this split ("the admin path... already mints batches, so the chore-reward loop works from the first day"). **Migrations only go up to `0025_config_founding.sql`** — the slice doc's proposed `0025_accounts_pin.sql` / `0026_pin_attempts.sql` / `0027_config_founding.sql` got renumbered in the build (config landed as `0025` since the PIN migrations were skipped), so whoever builds the PIN follow-up starts numbering at `0026`, not `0025`. **That number has since been wrong twice** — see the 2026-08-03 numbering entry below. Read `migrations/` and take the next free number; do not trust any number written in a slice document, including this one.
- Verified end-to-end against a local `wrangler dev` + migrations apply: mint → choose breed → claim → breed two founders → a real foal, including the first-ever cross-breed foal (QH × Arabian correctly reading `Cross`, not `Quarter Horse`). Wrong claim counts and over-capacity claims both refuse with a sentence naming which count or stable is wrong.

**2026-08-03 — Slice numbers are document order; migration numbers are claimed at build time; §10 is the build order.** Three numbering facts that have now caused confusion twice, written down so they stop.

- **A slice document's number records when it was written, not when it is built.** Slice 0005 was built before slice 0004 was started. Slice 0006 is specified but the (as yet unwritten) image slot slice is built before it. A number identifies a document; it does not schedule it.
- **A slice document proposing migrations `00NN`-`00NN+3` is estimating how many it needs, not reserving those numbers.** Whichever slice is built first takes them. **Always read `migrations/` and take the next free number**, then correct the slice document as you build. Slice 0005's own note about this has been wrong twice for precisely this reason.
- **The authoritative build order is CLAUDE.md §10**, which is live state. `docs/horse-game-overview.md` §13 is the design-record ordering and stays broadly true, but where the two disagree, §10 is what is actually happening. §13 now carries the same three rules at its end, plus the two specified-but-unbuilt pieces (semen storage, the parent's PIN) that sit outside the sequence entirely.

**2026-08-03 — Image library: matched on breed only, a numbered set, and the count is a column.** Decided in conversation; recorded in `docs/horse-game-overview.md` §5b and §14 (which closes that open question) and in the schema doc's `breeds` row. The build-relevant parts:

- **Files live in `public/horses/`, named `<breed code>-NN.webp`** — lowercase code (`qh`, `ar`, `tb`, `pf`, `ic`, `gw`, `fr`, `nok`), zero-padded from `01`. `public/` is the static-assets directory (`wrangler.toml`'s `[assets]`), served at the site root, so `public/horses/fr-03.webp` is `/horses/fr-03.webp`.
- **The picker derives the list from `breeds.image_count`, never from a directory listing or a manifest.** Cloudflare's static assets have no listing, and a manifest is a file the operator would have to hand-edit in a browser. Deriving means adding images is an upload plus one number — no code change, no session needed. **The cost: files must never be renumbered or deleted, only replaced in place**, because a gap renders as a broken image rather than being skipped. Say that on whatever admin screen edits the count.
- **No colour matching, deliberately** — the engine produces twelve visible colours today and the design plans ~16 loci, so the colour space is combinatorial and a colour-matched library would quietly stop being able to match. The player chooses freely within their breed's set; the picker shows the horse's real colour in text beside the grid.
- **The operator uploads through GitHub's web file editor** (repo → `public/horses` → Add file → Upload files), which is the only path they have — no terminal, per CLAUDE.md §1. Keep it that way: never design a library workflow that needs a build step, an image pipeline, or a CLI.

**2026-08-02 — Slice 0007 (the image slot) built, per `docs/slices/0007-image-slot.md` in full — nothing deferred, nothing disagreed with.** Migrations `0026_breeds_image_count.sql` and `0027_horses_image.sql` (the next free numbers, per the 2026-08-03 numbering entry above). What a future session needs to know:

- **`src/lib/images.ts` is new** — pure, no database access, in `src/lib` rather than `src/engines` because this is presentation plumbing, not simulation (slice 0007 §4.2). `libraryImagePath(breedCode, n)` builds a path; `imageOptionsFor(composition, breeds)` is the one function that turns a horse's `composition` blob into its ordered, deduplicated image set (slice 0007 §2.2 — union of every breed code's set, larger fraction first, breed code as the tiebreak); `isAllowedImagePath(path, options)` is server-side membership-checking validation, not a path-format check — a submitted value is safe exactly because it's re-derived and checked against the *live* set, never trusted from the form (slice 0007 §2.6, same shape as the `hh_stable` cookie rule elsewhere in this log); `parseImageCount` validates an admin's typed count to `0..99`. All six of its test groups (§8 of the slice doc) are in `test/images.test.ts`.
- **`horses.image_url`/`image_source` and `breeds.image_count`** are the only schema change. `db/horses.ts`'s `setHorseImage(env, horseId, imageUrl | null)` writes both image columns together and does *not* validate — the caller (the route) must call `isAllowedImagePath` first. `db/breeds.ts`'s `updateBreedImageCounts` writes every changed count in one `env.DB.batch()` and clears the module-scope breeds cache (slice 0007 §5.3) — that file's cache was previously safe to leave stale forever because nothing wrote to `breeds`; this is the first thing that does.
- **`/horses/:id/image`** (`src/routes/horses.ts`'s `horseImageRoute`, `src/render/horses.ts`'s `renderImagePickerPage`) is owner-only on both GET and POST, same `notFound()`-for-a-non-owner shape as every other stable-scoped route — an admin viewing someone else's horse gets no exception, confirmed by hand against a live `wrangler dev`. Radios are grouped by breed with a heading only when a horse's set spans more than one breed (a purebred never shows a heading); a **No picture** tile always ends the grid and is pre-checked when the horse has no picture yet, so the form always has a valid default. The *"also used by &lt;name&gt;"* courtesy label (§6.2) reuses `listStableHorses`, already loaded for the picker — no extra query beyond what was already needed.
- **No auto-assignment at birth, confirmed working as designed**: a freshly-foaled horse's `image_url` is null (nothing in `buildFoalInsertStatements` or `buildFoundingHorseInsertStatement` touches it), and its horse page renders the placeholder card — verified by breeding two founders through a live tick to a real foal and checking its page. The `image_pick` seed label stays reserved and unused, per slice 0007 §7 (no RNG anywhere in this slice; `test/rng.test.ts`'s golden values are untouched).
- **`renderHorsePage` gained a required `visibleColour: string` param** (the route already had `phenotype.visibleColour` on hand from the existing description call) — the placeholder card and the picker's colour line both read from it, never from a separate lookup, so the "stated in text" mitigation for breed-only matching (slice 0007 §2.1) can't drift out of sync with what `describeHorse` already says.
- **CSS**: new "Images" section in `public/style.css` (`.horse-row`, `.horse-thumb`/`--placeholder`, `.horse-portrait`/`--placeholder`, `.image-grid`, `.image-option`/`--none`, `.image-placeholder-tile`). The selected-tile highlight is `input:checked + img` — the radio itself is visually hidden with `position: absolute; opacity: 0` rather than `display: none`, so it stays keyboard-focusable (no JavaScript exists anywhere in this codebase, per the no-JS entry above, and this slice didn't add any).
- Verified end-to-end against a local `wrangler dev --local` with all 27 migrations applied through `/admin/migrations`: setting a breed's count and seeing the next filename update immediately; a purebred's picker (3 tiles + No picture); an empty-breed picker (one sentence, nothing to press, no broken image); picking, changing, and clearing a picture; the barn-list thumbnail and placeholder tile; a real cross-bred foal bred and foaled through the tick, showing a two-breed grouped picker with the courtesy label firing correctly; an admin viewing another account's horse (picture shows, no link, and a direct POST/GET to the picker 404s); a tampered POST naming a path outside the live set (rejected, nothing written); an invalid admin count (`abc`, rejected with the breed named in the error) — all as `docs/slices/0007-image-slot.md` §1's eleven acceptance steps describe.

**2026-08-02 — Slice 0006 (conformation) built, per `docs/slices/0006-conformation.md` in full.** Migrations `0028_quantitative_traits.sql`, `0029_seed_quantitative_traits.sql`, `0030_horses_environmental_noise.sql`, `0031_config_conformation.sql` (the next free numbers). What a future session needs to know:

- **`src/engines/conformation/` is new**, same pure-function pattern as `src/engines/genetics/` and `src/engines/breeding/`. `traits.ts` holds `TRAIT_CATEGORY`/`TRAIT_DIRECTION` (mirrors of the seed migration) and `anchorFor(trait)` — 50 for a bidirectional (conformation) trait, 0 for a unidirectional (ability) one, per the slice's §2.2 correction that a shape measurement has no good direction. `model.ts` holds the actual pipeline: `rollEnvironmentalNoise`/`legacyEnvironmentalNoise`/`noiseFor` (the noise roll and its §2.5 legacy fallback), `geneticValue` (potential×5+noise, clamped 1..99), `realization` (age × COI × config, trainingFactor/careFactor pinned at 1.0 for now), `expressedValue`, and `conformationValues`/`conformationDisplayRows` which zip the four conformation traits' computed numbers with their DB display metadata (name, low/high labels).
- **`horses.environmental_noise`** is written at exactly the two existing creation points (`buildFoundingHorseInsertStatement`, `buildFoalInsertStatements` in `src/db/horses.ts`), both of which now take a `conformationNoiseSd` parameter threaded from `ctx.config.values.conformation_noise_sd` at every call site (`routes/admin.ts`'s founder form, `db/founding.ts`'s `claimOffer`, `db/pregnancies.ts`'s foaling stage). Null on every horse that predates this slice — read via `noiseFor`'s legacy branch, which derives from the horse's own `rng_seed` against a **frozen module constant**, never the live config, so retuning `conformation_noise_sd` later can't move a horse already alive.
- **A founding candidate's conformation is computed fresh at read time, not stored** — slice 0006 §2.6 explicitly forbids a noise column on `import_candidates`, since the candidate's own `rng_seed` already determines it, and that same `rng_seed` becomes the claimed horse's unchanged. `routes/founding.ts` calls `rollEnvironmentalNoise` directly on the candidate's seed rather than going through `noiseFor` (a candidate row has no stored noise to be null-checked).
