- **New table `quantitative_traits` (`src/db/quantitativeTraits.ts`)** is display metadata only, exactly as `loci` is for Mendelian genes — `TRAITS` in `polygenic.ts` stays the sole iteration/RNG order, never re-derived from this table. `getConformationTraits()` filters to the four displayed rows. `display_unit` from the schema doc's original sketch is dropped (these are unitless positions between two labels); `direction`, `low_label`/`high_label` and `teaching_text` are added, and the schema doc's §3.4 is updated to say so.
- **A disagreement with the slice document's own worked example, found and fixed rather than silently matched:** §4.4's prose claims a foal with geneticValue 68 "reads 66 at maturity" at COI 0.25 with `inbreeding_depression_factor = 1.0`. Working the formula in §4.3 exactly as written gives `round(50 + 18 × (1 − 0.25)) = round(63.5) = 64`, not 66 — and the same formula reproduces the worked example's other three rows (60/63/68) exactly, so the formula is right and the "66" is an arithmetic slip in the document's prose. Implemented the formula as specified (64), not the prose's stated result; `test/conformation/model.test.ts` asserts 64 and says why in a comment, so a future session re-reading the slice document doesn't silently "fix" a working implementation to match a design-doc typo. Flagged here per CLAUDE.md §2 rather than building around it quietly.
- **A deviation §5.2 asked to be noted:** no `rolled_noise` column on `pregnancies` (unlike the schema doc's original sketch) — environmental noise is rolled at foaling, from the foal's own already-fixed seed, because nothing displays a pregnancy's conformation before birth and rolling it at foaling is simpler than also carrying it through conception.
- **Where it shows**: the horse page's new Conformation card (`src/render/horses.ts`'s `conformationCard`/`conformationRow` — a CSS-only meter bar, no JS, per the no-JS entry above), a compact one-line comparison in the barn list (`conformationCompactLine`, first word of each trait's name), and a compact line per candidate on the founding screen. The old "conformation is real and already decided, it just isn't visible on this screen yet" sentence on the founding page is removed now that it's false. The inbreeding note sits next to the existing COI line on the horse page, gated on the existing `coi_warn_threshold`, per §6.1.
- **Nothing here shows ability traits (stamina/jump scope/speed/trainability) or fertility** — `conformationValues` only ever returns the four conformation-category traits (`CONFORMATION_TRAITS` in `traits.ts`), and no route reads the others for display. `test/conformation/model.test.ts` asserts this directly.
- `/admin/config` gained the four new tunables (`conformation_noise_sd`, `conformation_maturity_years` as whole numbers; `conformation_realization_at_birth`, `inbreeding_depression_factor` as genuine fractions, needing a second `DECIMAL_CONFIG_KEYS` validation list in `routes/admin.ts` since the existing numeric form only ever accepted whole numbers) plus the §2.4 warning that changing `inbreeding_depression_factor` re-scores every already-inbred horse in the game immediately, since nothing about conformation is cached.
- Verified with `npm test` (all 163 tests, including the new `test/conformation/model.test.ts` and the extended `test/genetics/consistency.test.ts` checking `TRAITS` against the seed migration) and `npx tsc --noEmit`, both clean. **Not** verified against a live `wrangler dev` browser session in this build — whoever next touches this area should still give the ten acceptance steps in the slice document's §1 a real look in a browser at least once.

**2026-08-02 — `/admin/reset`: emptying the world from the browser, for play-testing.** Not a slice — asked for directly so the children can test playing from the start more than once. No migration; nothing was added to the schema.

- **`src/db/reset.ts` owns the delete order, and that order is the whole point of the file.** `RESET_TABLES` lists every table a reset empties, children before parents, and the comment above it names which foreign key forces each position. **If a later slice adds a table with a foreign key into any of these, add it to that list in the right place** — and to the `REFERENCES` map in `test/reset.test.ts`, which asserts the order against the foreign-key graph and fails loudly if a table is emptied before something that still points at it. A wrong order surfaces as a failed reset in front of the children, which is precisely when nobody can debug it.
- **Two scopes.** `'horses'` empties `import_candidates`, `import_offers`, `pregnancies`, `coverings`, `horse_ancestors`, `horses` — stables keep their name, prefix, money and capacity, and the clock keeps running. `'world'` adds `stable_prefix_history`, `stables`, `tick_run`, nulls every account's `last_active_stable_id`, and puts `world` back to day 0 / tick 0 / season 0 with the `last_tick_*` columns cleared and `started_real_ts` set to now.
- **`world.paused` is deliberately left alone by both scopes**, even though a fresh world seeds unpaused. A reset is about world content; silently unpausing would let the next cron tick start moving a world the operator had stopped on purpose. Said on the page in those words.
- **Never touched, by design, and the page says so:** `accounts` (deleting these locks everyone out, including whoever pressed the button), `config` (tuning, not world content), `config_audit` (append-only per CLAUDE.md §7, and a record of admin tuning rather than of the world), and the reference tables `breeds` / `loci` / `quantitative_traits` / `d1_migrations` (created by migrations — clearing them breaks the game with no browser path back). If you ever add a reset scope that touches accounts, it needs a conversation first, not a checkbox.
- **Two guards, not one.** The `required`-checkbox pattern from `/admin/world` *plus* the word `reset` typed by hand, both re-checked on the server (trimmed, case-insensitive) rather than trusted from the form. Still no JavaScript. The full-world reset also expires the `hh_stable` cookie, since the stable it names no longer exists.
- Every table name interpolated into SQL in that file comes from the module's own `RESET_TABLES` constant and can never come from a request — keep it that way if you extend it.
- Verified end-to-end against a live `wrangler dev --local`: both scopes with real rows in all nine tables, all three refusal paths, accounts/config/breeds/traits surviving, `paused` surviving, the clock resetting, an old prefix becoming claimable again, and the stable/barn/breeding pages all working afterwards.

**2026-08-02 — Slice 0008 (one show class) built in full, both halves of the document's own split (§11) in one session.** Migrations `0032`-`0041` (the next free numbers). `src/engines/showing/` is new (`score.ts`, `eligibility.ts`, `placing.ts`, `noise.ts`, `calendar.ts`), same pure-function pattern as `src/engines/breeding/` and `src/engines/conformation/` - `scoreEntry` never re-implements slice 0006's conformation pipeline, it only reads the `expressed` values that pipeline already produces. What a future session needs to know:

- **A disagreement with the slice document, found before building rather than after:** §5.3 says a show's venue is "drawn deterministically... by the show's month index, so the same venue comes round each year," but §7.2 lists `venue` as a sub-seed label derived from `shows.rng_seed` - a fresh `randomSeed()` per show (§7.1). Those two can't both hold: deriving venue from a per-show random seed would make the venue *different* every year at the same calendar position, not the same. Resolved in §5.3's favour (CLAUDE.md §2): `src/engines/showing/calendar.ts`'s `calendarEntryFor` keys venue selection off `scheduledGameDay`/`intervalGameDays` alone, draws no RNG at all, and `venue` is not among the sub-seed labels `db/shows.ts` derives. Verified live: the same venue (Cedar Hollow) recurs at the same calendar position every 12 shows.
- **Shows are created and judged in the same tick stage pass, back to back** (`createDueShows` then `judgeDueShowClasses` in `db/tick.ts`, after the breeding stages) - because the default `show_entry_window_game_days` equals `show_interval_game_days` (30 = 30), a show is created several ticks *before* its date and judged in the very tick that reaches its date, all in one `executeTick` call. Confirmed live: entering was possible for two ticks' worth of game days, then the third tick both judged the show *and* created the next one 30 days out, all in one page-load's worth of "advance".
- **The NPC show barn (`src/db/npc.ts`) is a real stable, prefix `Fair Meadow`** (migration `0040_npc_show_barn.sql`, `SHOW_BARN_PREFIX` constant). Its horses go through `generateCandidate`/`buildFoundingHorseInsertStatement` exactly as founding stock does - same seeds, same pipeline, same render path, no parallel scoring code. **A full `/admin/reset` world reset deletes it along with every other stable** (a blunt `DELETE FROM stables`), and since `migrations/0040` only ever runs once, nothing would recreate it - `src/db/reset.ts`'s `resetWorld` now re-inserts the barn (empty) after a world-scope reset, in the same shape the migration leaves it in. This is a deliberate addition to reset.ts's job, not asked for by the slice document, made because the alternative was a full reset silently and permanently breaking the show feature. Verified live: world reset, barn survives (0 horses), re-stocked from `/admin/shows` successfully afterwards.
- **`show_entries`, `horse_show_summary`, `show_classes` and `shows` were added to `src/db/reset.ts`'s `HORSE_TABLES`** (CLAUDE.md's own reset entry above asks for this) and to `test/reset.test.ts`'s `REFERENCES` map - a horses-only reset now also clears show history, since `show_entries`/`horse_show_summary` point at `horses` and would otherwise dangle once the show barn's own horses are wiped. `judges` joins `breeds`/`loci`/`quantitative_traits` as reference data, never cleared. Verified live: a horses-only reset with real show data in every new table completed with no FK error.
- **Judging is one `env.DB.batch()`**: the NPC field's entry inserts, every entry's score/placing update, every shown horse's `horse_show_summary` upsert, then the class's `status = 'judged'` and finally the show's - exactly the order §6.2 specifies, and in that order deliberately (the show's own "have all my classes finished" check reads the class row a statement after that row was itself flipped, relying on the same within-batch sequential-visibility guarantee `buildFoalInsertStatements`' ancestor-row subqueries already depend on). Verified live by pressing "advance" three more times after a show was judged: start counts did not move, no duplicate entries appeared.
- **A second, post-push correction: a class with zero player entries is never topped up with show-barn horses.** The first push topped up to `target_field_size` unconditionally, per a literal reading of §6.2 ("if entries are fewer than target_field_size... pick eligible NPC horses" has no floor on the *existing* count) - so a show nobody entered would still get judged, hand out ribbons, and write a "win" into a show-barn horse's permanent record against nobody. Caught by the person running the project asking directly whether this was built. `judgeOneClass` in `db/shows.ts` now sets `shortfall = existingEntries.length > 0 ? ... : 0`, so a class nobody entered goes straight to the zero-entry path (still marked `judged`, so it doesn't sit `scheduled` forever, but touches no horse's `horse_show_summary`). Flagged per CLAUDE.md §2 as a deliberate departure from §6.2's literal wording, not a bug fix to that wording.
- **Eligibility returns a reason code, never a sentence** (`EligibilityReason` in `eligibility.ts`) - the wording (`eligibilityMessage` in `render/shows.ts`) lives at the edges, with the horse's own name prepended by the route, so "Zahra isn't a Quarter Horse, and this class is for Quarter Horses only" and "Foaly isn't old enough yet..." are assembled from the same small set of fragments rather than invented per call site. Confirmed live against a real Arabian and a real newborn foal.
- **Entering a horse has two doors that both call the same `db/shows.ts` function (`enterHorseInClass`), never two implementations of the rules:** the horse page's "Enter in a show" button (`POST /horses/:id/enter-show`, new route in `routes/horses.ts`) and `/shows/:id`'s own entry form (`POST /shows/:id` with `action=enter`, `routes/shows.ts`). Both re-check eligibility server-side rather than trusting that the button was only shown because it passed.
- **A deliberate scope decision the slice document left open:** §8.1 describes `/shows/:id`'s entry form as being for "the current stable" - this codebase already has an `hh_stable` cookie convention for that, but `readStableCookie` had never actually been read anywhere yet. Built it instead as "every eligible horse across every stable the logged-in account owns," which needed no cookie plumbing, works identically for an account with one stable or three, and avoids a child ever being confused about why a horse they can see in the barn doesn't show up in the entry list because the wrong stable happened to be selected.
- **New sub-seed labels**, all via `deriveSeed`, none of them `venue` (see the disagreement above): from `shows.rng_seed` → `class_<n>`, `judge_<n>`; from `show_classes.rng_seed` → `npc_field`, `noise_<horseId>`. `test/rng.test.ts`'s golden values are untouched, confirmed by `npm test`.
- Verified end-to-end against a live `wrangler dev --local` with all 41 migrations applied through `/admin/migrations` (this is also where a real bug was caught and fixed - see below): stocking the barn, three ticks of the circuit (create → two open windows → judged-and-recreated-in-one-tick), entering a Quarter Horse and watching her place 3rd against five show-barn horses topped up to a field of 8, the entry breakdown page's arithmetic (79.69 weighted average − 3.66 noise = 76.03 final, matching `scoreEntry` exactly), the horse page's Show record card and re-entry button for the next show, the barn list's ribbon badge, refusing an Arabian and a newborn foal by name and rule, a second show 30 days later under a different judge (Ellery, not Halloway), re-pressing "advance" three times after judging with no double-counting, and both reset scopes with real show data in every new table.
- **A real bug caught by the live pass, not by `npm test`:** `migrations/0033_seed_judges.sql`'s first draft had a semicolon inside a judge's `blurb` string ("Likes a horse that moves; weights the shoulder..."). `src/lib/sql.ts`'s `splitSqlStatements` splits on every `;` with no awareness of string literals - documented as a known limitation in that file's own comment - so the semicolon silently split one `INSERT` into two invalid fragments and `/admin/migrations` failed with `unrecognized token`. Fixed by rewording the blurb to use a dash instead, per that file's existing warning ("apply it with the CLI instead, or extend this splitter first") - extending the splitter was out of scope for this slice. **Worth a general note for future sessions:** any seed migration's string literals must avoid bare `;` (and `--`) if it's ever going to be applied through `/admin/migrations` rather than the CLI.

**2026-08-02 — Slice 0009, Part A (money moves) built, per that document's own §11 split.** (Part B — turns and the events log — followed in a later session the same day; see its own dated entry further down.) Migrations `0042`-`0046` (the next free numbers). What a future session needs to know:

- **`src/db/ledger.ts`'s `buildLedgerStatements` is the one function in this codebase allowed to write `stables.balance`, and it is load-bearing the way slice 0009 §4.2 says it is.** It always pairs a ledger `INSERT` with a *relative* balance `UPDATE` (`balance = balance + ?`) - never an absolute write. `src/db/stables.ts`'s `createStableWithPrefix` is the one deliberate exception to routing *through* it: a brand-new stable's opening balance is set directly on its own founding `INSERT` (as it always was), and the matching `kind = 'opening'` ledger row is written alongside it in the same batch **without** a paired balance update - going through `buildLedgerStatements` there would double the starting balance. The comment on that function explains why; read it before "fixing" it.
- **Referencing a same-batch, not-yet-existing row's id without `last_insert_rowid()` going stale**: this slice hit the "which id do I write into `reference_id`" problem twice, in two different files, with two different solutions, both documented at their call sites:
  - `createStableWithPrefix` needs the ledger row's `stable_id` to point at the stable just inserted two statements earlier in the same batch - `last_insert_rowid()` is already stale by then (the prefix-history insert in between clobbers it), so the ledger insert uses `(SELECT id FROM stables ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT 1)` instead, the same subquery pattern `buildFoalInsertStatements`' ancestor rows already use.
  - `judgeOneClass` in `src/db/shows.ts` needs to write a `prize` ledger row's `reference_id` pointing at a show-barn entry's `show_entries.id` - but that row doesn't exist until its own `INSERT` runs, later in the *same* batch that also needs to carry the prize money atomically (slice 0009 §4.4: "this must not become a second write that can happen without it"). Neither `last_insert_rowid()` nor the subquery trick works cleanly here (several NPC rows are inserted in the same batch, and the subquery would only ever see the most recent one). Solved instead by reading `SELECT COALESCE(MAX(id), 0) FROM show_entries` once before building the batch, then assigning each new NPC entry an explicit, pre-allocated `id` at `INSERT` time - the same single-invocation-no-concurrent-insert assumption the ancestor-subquery pattern already relies on elsewhere in this codebase, just applied to an explicit primary key instead of a subquery.
- **Upkeep is its own tick-stage file, `src/db/upkeep.ts`, not inlined into `src/db/tick.ts`** - matching the existing pattern where `coverings.ts`, `pregnancies.ts` and `shows.ts` each own their tick stage and `tick.ts` only orchestrates. The pure day-arithmetic (`computeUpkeep`) lives in `src/lib/upkeep.ts` and returns `{ amount, advanceMarker }` rather than just an amount - a stable with no horses still needs its `last_upkeep_game_day` marker moved forward (so it isn't retroactively billed the day it buys its first horse), which a bare "amount" return can't express. Watch for `-0`: `-(0 * days * rate)` is `-0` in JS, `Object.is(-0, 0)` is `false`, and `test/upkeep.test.ts` caught it - `computeUpkeep` normalizes it back to `0` before returning.
- **The debt rule (`canTakeOnCost` in `src/lib/money.ts`) is called from exactly one place, `validateBooking` in `src/routes/horses.ts`, and deliberately not from `enterHorseInClass`** in `src/db/shows.ts` - slice 0009 §2.4/§4.6's correction to the original "can't breed, enter shows or buy anything" rule: a stable in the red must always be able to show its way out, since shows are the only income that exists. A comment at `enterHorseInClass` says so, per the slice document's own instruction to leave a comment "at the call site that does not exist" for whoever next goes looking for a missing check.
- **Prize money is paid inside `judgeOneClass`'s existing single batch**, never a second write - one `kind = 'prize'` ledger row per paid placing, `reference_type = 'show_entry'`, description naming the ordinal placing and the show by name (e.g. "6th place, Blue Ridge Winter Show, Year 1."). `show_classes.prize_schedule` is snapshotted from `config.values.show_prize_schedule` at class-creation time (`createDueShows`/`createShowIfMissing`), never re-read from config at judging - the same treatment `ideal_vector`/`noise_sd`/`ideal_falloff` already get. Pays to `show_entries.entered_by_stable_id`, so the NPC show barn is paid like any other stable (nothing reads its balance, but nothing special-cases it either).
- **`src/db/reset.ts`: `ledger` is added to `WORLD_ONLY_TABLES`, not `HORSE_TABLES`.** A horses-only reset keeps a stable's balance (its own header comment already promises this), so it must also keep the ledger rows that balance is the sum of - clearing `ledger` there would silently break the balance-equals-sum-of-ledger invariant the very next time the Money page rendered. Only a full world reset (which also deletes the stables themselves) clears it, positioned before `stables` in the delete order since it has a real foreign key there. `test/reset.test.ts`'s `REFERENCES` map updated to match.
- **`/stables/:id/money`** (`src/render/money.ts`, `stableMoneyRoute` in `src/routes/stables.ts`) fetches newest-first (capped at 100, matching the query the index on `ledger(stable_id, id DESC)` supports), reverses to oldest-first to compute a running balance via `withRunningBalance` (pure, `src/db/ledger.ts`, tested in `test/ledger.test.ts` without D1), then reverses back for display. This only reproduces the *true* current balance in the bottom-to-top sense described by the acceptance criteria ("the oldest row is the starting balance") while the full history fits under the 100-row cap - fine at the game's current scale, worth revisiting if a very old, very active stable's history ever gets trimmed.
- **The stable home page** now shows the balance (in red, with a sentence, when negative), a plain-English per-day upkeep cost line, and a link to the Money page; its subnav gained a **Money** entry between Breed and Change prefix. **`/admin/money`** (new admin subpage, same `required`-checkbox guard pattern as `/admin/world`'s advance control) lets an admin add or remove money from any stable by hand, with a required reason that becomes the ledger row's description - the relief valve slice 0009 §2.4 promises for a stable that has gone into the red with no other way out yet.
- Verified end-to-end against a live `wrangler dev --local` with all 46 migrations applied through the CLI (`wrangler d1 migrations apply --local`, confirmed at the prompt): a fresh stable's opening ledger row: 3 claimed founding horses costing "3 horses, 6 a day to keep"; upkeep charged correctly on each of several consecutive ticks with no double-charging; nothing charged across three ticks while paused, confirmed against `/admin/world`'s own tick sequence moving while game day did not; `/admin/money` driving a stable to −140 and the Money page showing it in red; booking a covering refusing with the stable named and the exact deficit; `/admin/money` topping the balance back up and the same booking then succeeding; entering a horse in a show, advancing to its judging day, and the result page showing "6th - green ribbon - paid 50" with a matching `+50` row on the Money page in the same tick as that tick's upkeep charge; a horses-only reset preserving the balance and full ledger history; a full world reset clearing it; and a stable created after a full world reset getting a fresh, correct opening ledger row. **Not yet deployed to a live Cloudflare account in this session** - whoever does the next real deploy should re-read `migrations/0043_stables_upkeep.sql`'s own warning before applying it: getting that backfill wrong empties every balance in the game on the first tick.

**2026-08-02 — Slice 0009, Part B (turns and the events log) built, per that document's §5 and §6.** Migrations `0047`-`0049` (the next free numbers, confirmed by reading `migrations/` rather than trusting a number in the slice document, per the 2026-08-03 numbering entry above). What a future session needs to know:

- **The tick never touches `accounts` at all - the turn budget is derived at read time, not written by a tick stage, and this is the single most important thing to not "fix."** `src/lib/actions.ts`'s `actionsRemaining(account, tickSeq, actionsPerTick)` returns the account's stored `actions_remaining` when `actions_reset_tick_seq === tickSeq`, otherwise the full `actionsPerTick` - so a tick that fires twice or is missed entirely changes nothing about anyone's turns (idempotent by construction), a new tick discards whatever was left rather than adding to it (never banks, per overview §6c), and turns refill during a pause because `world.tick_seq` itself still increments on a paused tick (slice 0001's own deliberate choice - a pause stops the world, not a child's afternoon). `src/lib/context.ts`'s `actionsLeftFor(ctx)` is the one place this gets called from a route; every render call site takes `actionsLeft: number | null` (null when logged out) rather than recomputing it.
- **Spending is one atomic conditional `UPDATE`, in `src/db/accounts.ts`'s `spendAction`** - the check (`actions_reset_tick_seq <> ?tick OR actions_remaining >= ?cost`) and the decrement are the same statement, so two forms submitted at the same instant cannot both spend the last turn. **Every call site follows check-act-spend, in that order**: read the budget and refuse up front if it looks empty (with `src/lib/context.ts`'s `turnsRefusalMessage`, which names the next tick's local time from `world.tick_times_local` - reading the wall clock here is display only, never a decision, per CLAUDE.md §5.3), do the actual game action, and only spend afterward - and if `spendAction` loses a race and returns `false`, the caller lets the action stand and charges nothing anyway. This is deliberately asymmetric: a child charged for something that didn't happen has no way to find out why or get it back, so the rare failure mode is a free turn, never an unexplained charge. The three call sites - `stableBreedRoute`'s `book` action and `horseEnterShowRoute`/`showRoute`'s `enter` action and `stableFoundingRoute`'s `claim` action - all follow this same shape; `src/lib/actions.ts`'s `ACTION_COSTS` is the one map future slices add to (`genotype_test`, `buy_horse`, `list_horse`, `start_training` are named as the next candidates, per the slice document's own §5.3).
- **`src/db/events.ts` is new, alongside the `events` table.** `buildEventStatement` builds the insert (or an empty array, when `accountId` is null - the NPC show barn's own stable has no account, so nothing is ever written for it, per §6.1) for the three kinds whose subject horse already has a known id (`covering_conceived`, `covering_missed`, `show_result`). **A foal has no known id at the point its event needs writing** - `buildFoaledEventStatement` is the one exception, using `(SELECT id FROM horses ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT 1)` the same way `buildFoalInsertStatements`' own ancestor rows already do, and it must be the last statement in `foalOnePregnancy`'s batch for that subquery to stay valid. Every write happens inside the same batch as whatever caused it (a resolved covering, a foaling, a judged class) - an event can never exist for something that did not happen.
- **`horseDisplayName` moved from `render/horses.ts` into `db/horses.ts`.** The naming rule (registered name, else barn name, else "Unnamed filly/colt") is needed by db-layer event payloads (`coverings.ts`, `pregnancies.ts`, `shows.ts`), which must not import from `render/`. `render/horses.ts`'s `displayNameFor` is now just `export const displayNameFor = horseDisplayName;` so every existing render/route call site is untouched.
- **`show_result` events are written for every placed entry, not only paid ones** - `prize` is `0` in the payload when a placing falls outside the class's prize schedule, per §3.6's "prize money appears in the feed only as part of the show-result event that would have been written anyway." Skipped for the show barn's own entries via the same `accountId === null` guard.
- **The "While you were away" panel (`/stables`, `src/render/stables.ts`'s `awayPanel`) shows unread events only, newest first, capped at 30, with a `POST /stables` "Mark all read" button** (`stablesMarkReadRoute` in `src/routes/stables.ts`) that never fires from a GET. **A stable's own home page shows a separate "Recent happenings" feed, read and unread together**, most recent 20 - the two are deliberately different views over the same table, built as two small render functions (`awayPanel` vs `stableFeed`) rather than one parameterised over "unread only." `eventSentence` (also in `render/stables.ts`) is the one place a payload's JSON becomes the sentence either view shows - a missing payload key is legal (the same `"v"` convention `horses.genotype` uses) and renders defensively rather than throwing.
- **The events retention tick stage (`deleteOldEvents` in `db/events.ts`) sits inside `tick.ts`'s existing `paused === 0` branch**, right after `chargeUpkeep`, and is naturally idempotent - `DELETE FROM events WHERE game_day < ?` against the same `newGameDay` twice just deletes the same (or, after a missed tick, a larger) set once.
- **`src/db/reset.ts`: `events` is added to `HORSE_TABLES`, not `WORLD_ONLY_TABLES`, unlike `ledger`.** It has a real foreign key into `horses` (`subject_horse_id`), so a horses-only reset must clear it too, or that foreign key would be left dangling the moment horses are deleted - there is no balance-equals-sum invariant here holding it back the way there is for `ledger`. Positioned right before `horse_ancestors`/`horses` in the delete order. `test/reset.test.ts`'s `REFERENCES` map updated to match.
- **A genuine edge case, not a bug:** on a truly fresh install, before the first tick has ever fired, `world.tick_seq` is `0` and a brand-new account's `actions_reset_tick_seq` is also `0` (its column default) - these *match*, so `actionsRemaining` returns the account's stored `actions_remaining` (also `0`), not a full budget. This is the literal reading the slice document itself gives ("Both default 0, which reads as 'reset at tick 0, none left'") and it self-corrects the moment any tick fires (cron or manual), which will have happened many times over before a real family logs in. Confirmed live: a fresh account showed "0 turns left" before the first tick, and "6 turns left" immediately after one manual advance.
- Verified end-to-end against a live `wrangler dev --local` with all 49 migrations applied through the CLI: the header's turn count appearing on every logged-in player page and reading `0` pre-tick, `6` post-tick; booking a covering spending a turn (6→5); a founding-batch claim spending a turn (6→5); a covering conceiving and the resulting `covering_conceived` event appearing on `/stables`' away panel with a working link to the mare, and on the stable home page's own feed; "Mark all read" clearing the away panel while the stable home feed still showed the same event (read and unread together, as designed); turns refilling to a full 6 after advancing the tick, including while the world was paused (game day unchanged, turns still back to full); and, with an account's turns forced to zero by hand, a booking attempt refusing with the exact message *"You've used all your turns for now. More arrive at the next tick - 19:00."* and confirming no covering row was written. Show-result and foaled events were verified by code review and the shared `buildEventStatement`/`buildFoaledEventStatement` machinery rather than a full show cycle or a live foaling in this pass - both reuse the same tested insert path the covering events used live.

**2026-08-02 — Slice 0010 (health, first pass) built, both parts in one session.** Migrations `0050`-`0057` (the next free numbers). What a future session needs to know:

- **`src/engines/health/status.ts` is the new pure engine** — same no-database pattern as `engines/genetics`, `engines/breeding`, `engines/showing`, `engines/conformation`. `conditionStatus(genotype, trigger)` counts copies of a trigger's mutant allele via the existing `getMendelianPair` (so slice 0002's missing-locus rule pays for itself again — every horse alive before this slice reads clear for all four conditions, with nothing backfilled) and maps to clear/carrier/affected, with a hard rule that a **dominant never produces the word "carrier"**. `ownerVisibleStatus(genotype, trigger, signsVisible, known)` is the sharper function this session pulled out of the horse-page route once it became clear the truth-versus-knowledge boundary needed to be independently testable (`test/health/knowledge.test.ts`) rather than embedded in `routes/horses.ts` where a database mock would have been needed to exercise it. `lethalTerminalGameDay(bornGameDay, window)` is a one-line pure function for the same reason — it exists so the "snapshotted, not read live" contract (CLAUDE.md §5.5) is checkable in isolation.
- **`LOCI` in `src/engines/genetics/loci.ts` gained four entries, appended after `DMRT3`** — `HYPP`, `PSSM1`, `HERDA`, `GBED`, wild type `N` at `alleles[0]` for all four (unlike most of the original five, where the wild type is `alleles[1]` — `wildType` is spelled out per-locus for exactly this reason, never assumed from position). Appending, never inserting, matters because `inheritance.ts` draws one allele per locus in `LOCI` order from a single RNG stream — inserting anywhere but the end would shift every draw after it and the same stored seed would stop producing the same horse. `test/genetics/consistency.test.ts`'s first `describe` block now scans **both** `0015_seed_loci.sql` and `0050_seed_disease_loci.sql` in sequence and asserts the combined result against the whole of `LOCI` — that combined assertion is what proves the four new loci are both correct *and* last, rather than two separate checks that could each pass while the ordering was still wrong.
- **`signs_visible` and `bars_showing` are columns on `conditions`, not branches in code.** Grep for `condition.code === 'GBED'` and you will not find it anywhere — GBED's silence before death (`signs_visible = 0`) and HERDA's show ban (`bars_showing = 1`) are both data. This is CLAUDE.md's design-record rule 6 (breeds, loci and conditions are rows) applied to health, and it is what lets the colour-linked conditions (Frame Overo, Silver, Grey, white patterning) arrive later as rows against the same `conditionStatus`/`ownerVisibleStatus` machinery with no new engine.
- **The truth-versus-knowledge rule (CLAUDE.md §12) has exactly two enforcement points, and both are worth knowing if you're about to touch health code.** (1) `src/engines/health/breedingWarning.ts`'s `breedingHealthWarnings` takes **only** two `Map<code, result>` knowledge maps and a list of recessive conditions — no genotype parameter exists on its signature, so there is nothing to accidentally read. `src/db/health.ts`'s `breedingHealthWarningsFor` is the thin async wrapper that loads knowledge rows and calls it; it is deliberately the *only* place in the codebase that assembles the breeding-preview health line, and it has no access to either horse's genotype in its own function body even though `routes/horses.ts`'s `stableBreedRoute` has both loaded two lines above (for the COI preview) — that's the whole point of the extraction. (2) `ownerVisibleStatus` (above) is where "affected is free if signs are visible, carrier never is" actually lives. Both are unit tested directly; nothing about this boundary is verified only by hand.
- **The founding-stock generator gained a clamp, not a re-roll.** `engines/founding/generate.ts`'s `generateCandidate` takes an optional `lethalTriggers: {locus, mutant}[]` and, after drawing, replaces one mutant allele with the locus's wild type wherever a lethal condition would otherwise read homozygous-affected — deterministic, no extra RNG draw, and the generator still does not know what GBED is (the caller derives the trigger list from `conditions.severity_class = 'lethal'` rows via `src/db/health.ts`'s `getLethalTriggers`). `buildFoundingHorseInsertStatement` (singular) became **`buildFoundingHorseInsertStatements`** (plural, returns `D1PreparedStatement[]`) because a founding horse can now also need `horse_conditions` rows written in the same batch — every call site (`createFoundingHorse`, `claimOffer`, `stockShowBarn`) was updated to spread the array rather than push a single statement, and `createFoundingHorse` now runs `env.DB.batch()` instead of a single `.run()`, reading `results[0].meta.last_row_id` for the new horse's id.
- **`horse_conditions` rows are written at both places a horse comes into being**, via `src/db/health.ts`'s `buildHorseConditionStatements` — called from `buildFoalInsertStatements` (foaling) and `buildFoundingHorseInsertStatements` (founding/admin-founder/NPC-show-barn), both of which already had the "(SELECT id FROM horses ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT 1)" pattern for `horse_ancestors`; the conditions rows reuse it. A `condition_signs` event fires in the same batch for any newly-affected `signs_visible = 1` condition, via a new `buildConditionSignsEventStatement` in `src/db/events.ts` (same subquery-id pattern as `buildFoaledEventStatement`). **This must stay before `buildFoaledEventStatement` finds the right id** — it does, because nothing here inserts into `horses`, only `horse_conditions` and `events`.
- **The tick's new stage, `killDueLethalFoals` in `src/db/health.ts`, sits between `foalDuePregnancies` and `createDueShows`** (both satisfy the slice's "after foaling, before upkeep" requirement). Idempotency is free from `h.status = 'alive'` in the selection query's `WHERE` clause — a re-fired tick finds nothing because the horse is already dead, a missed tick still catches up because the comparison is `<=` against a snapshotted day, and no processed-marker column exists. `terminal_game_day` is computed once, at birth, via `lethalTerminalGameDay` and never re-read from config afterward. The horse row is never deleted — `status = 'dead'`, `ended_game_day`, `end_reason = <condition code>` — so it stays in every pedigree and produce record forever, which is the point (a pairing that produced a lethal is the teaching artifact).
- **The barn list (`stableHorsesRoute`/`renderBarnList`) now shows dead horses, marked "Died"** — a genuinely new behaviour, since nothing could die in this game before this slice. A new `listStableHorsesWithDead` function in `db/horses.ts` (status IN ('alive','dead')) is used **only** there; every other reader of a stable's horses (`stableBreedRoute`, the NPC show barn's field-filling query in `db/shows.ts`, the image picker's "also used by" check) still calls the original alive-only `listStableHorses`, unchanged — do not swap those, a dead horse must never appear as a breeding or show candidate.
- **`ledger.kind` gained `'vet'`**, via a genuine table rebuild (`migrations/0057_ledger_add_vet_kind.sql` — SQLite cannot `ALTER` a `CHECK` constraint): create `ledger_new` with the widened constraint, copy every row, drop the old table, rename, recreate the index. Verified nothing has a foreign key pointing *into* `ledger` before writing it. `src/db/ledger.ts`'s `LedgerKind` TypeScript union was updated to match in the same change — easy to forget, since the type and the migration's `CHECK` have to agree by hand and nothing enforces that at build time beyond the compiler catching a literal that's no longer assignable.
- **A genotype test purchase (`/horses/:id/test`) follows the exact check-act-spend shape slice 0009 established**: turns checked first, then `canTakeOnCost` (a discretionary purchase, not a way out of debt — unlike showing, like breeding; `src/lib/money.ts`'s comment now documents two call sites instead of one), then one `env.DB.batch()` writing both the `horse_knowledge` rows and the `vet` ledger entry together, then `spendAction` **after** the batch succeeds. A panel purchase spends **one** turn regardless of how many conditions it buys (`ACTION_COSTS.genotype_test = 1`), and its cost is split across the purchased rows' `cost_paid` as evenly as whole numbers allow, remainder on the last row — money is always an integer, and the per-row receipts have to sum to what the ledger actually charged. The unique index on `(stable_id, horse_id, subject_code)` is the real guard against double-buying the same result; a race that trips it fails the whole batch (one transaction) and the route reports "already known, nothing charged" rather than throwing.
- **Two new event kinds, `condition_signs` and `horse_died`** — no migration needed, `events.kind` has no `CHECK` (per 0048's own comment). `horse_died`'s payload carries the condition's `event_text` **copied in at the moment the event is written**, not looked up fresh when rendered — an append-only event should read the same in a year even if the reference text on `conditions` is edited later. `eventSentence` in `render/stables.ts` builds the personalised heading itself ("Juniper's foal has died.") from the payload's `dam_name`, then appends the stored `event_text` below it — the heading uses the **dam's** name deliberately, since a foal that dies from a lethal this early is very often still unnamed. A new `.event-long` CSS class (`white-space: pre-line`) keeps the four-paragraph GBED wording's blank lines visible in the feed instead of collapsing to one run-on line, applied whenever a rendered sentence contains a newline.
- **Show eligibility gained a `barred_by_condition` reason**, computed from the horse's genotype directly (not knowledge — an affected, signs-visible condition is already free-to-see truth, so reading it here crosses no boundary) via `src/db/health.ts`'s `isBarredFromShowing`, threaded into `EligibilityHorse.barredByCondition` and checked first in `checkEligibility`. Breeding an affected horse is never blocked or warned about beyond what the player has already learned — §2.5 of the slice document names this as the central dilemma the whole system exists to produce, and a rule that resolves it for the player would delete the reason the slice exists.
- **`src/db/reset.ts`: `horse_knowledge` and `horse_conditions` join `HORSE_TABLES`, before `horses`** — both have real foreign keys into it. `conditions` (like `loci`, `breeds`, `quantitative_traits`) is reference data and is never cleared by either scope. `test/reset.test.ts`'s `REFERENCES` map updated to match.
- **No admin force-affected tool was added.** Slice 0010 §11 offered the existing `/admin/breeding` force-twins control as precedent for a one-shot admin flag to make manual GBED-death verification easier, but left it optional ("if you decide one is warranted"). Since the admin founder form (`/admin/horses/new`) already iterates the full `LOCI` list generically — the disease loci fields simply appeared on that form once `LOCI` grew, with no changes needed to that route — creating two GBED-carrier founding horses and breeding them repeatedly until an affected foal lands is already straightforward by hand. Flagged here in case a future session finds that tedious enough to be worth a dedicated shortcut after all.
- **This codebase has no D1 mock anywhere**, confirmed by grepping before writing `test/health/lethal.test.ts` — every DB-touching stage in every prior slice is verified by hand against a live `wrangler dev`, not unit tested with a fake database. `lethalTerminalGameDay`'s snapshot contract and the death-selection query's `WHERE`-clause idempotency are both covered by pure-function tests instead (the query's guard is modelled as a small local predicate function mirroring the real SQL, documented as such) — consistent with the existing convention rather than introducing a new one.
- **`npm test` (259 tests, all passing) and `npx tsc --noEmit` are both clean.** `test/rng.test.ts`'s golden values are untouched, confirmed directly rather than assumed — the RNG algorithm and the `LOCI` ordering are both unchanged in a way that would have broken them if either were wrong.
- **Verified end-to-end against a live `wrangler dev --local`, all 57 migrations applied through `/admin/migrations` itself (not the CLI, which uses a different splitter) — including 0053's four-paragraph GBED prose and 0057's ledger rebuild, both landing clean.** Walked all eleven steps of the slice document's §1: the admin founder form (which needed no changes to show the four new loci — it already iterates `LOCI` generically) creating a HERDA-affected/GBED-carrier mare and a HYPP-affected/GBED-carrier stallion; the Health card showing "Shows signs" for the visible conditions with no test and "Not tested" for the hidden carrier; buying a single GBED test (250, a `vet` ledger row, a spent turn) and seeing the result flip to "Carrier"; the breeding preview's health line giving the exact "about one in four... about one in two" wording once both parents were tested, and nothing at all beforehand; booking the covering, waiting out the season restriction and gestation, and a real foal inheriting HYPP from its sire — with the `condition_signs` event firing at birth and showing in both the away panel and the stable's own feed; HERDA barring a horse from the show ring by name and reason while breeding remained unblocked; and `/admin/health` showing real clear/carrier/affected counts. **The lethal death path itself was verified by writing a synthetic `terminal` `horse_conditions` row directly via `wrangler d1 execute --local`** (a real GBED-affected foal is a 1-in-4 event per pairing and gestation alone takes ~35 real tick-advances at this game's settings, too slow to wait out live) rather than breeding for luck — advancing the tick past the row's `terminal_game_day` killed the horse, set `end_reason = 'GBED'`, marked it "Died" in the barn list, and wrote the full drafted wording to the feed with paragraph breaks intact via the new `.event-long` CSS class.
- **A real bug this pass caught that neither `tsc` nor `npm test` could have:** `buildKnowledgePurchaseStatements`' `INSERT INTO horse_knowledge` bound seven values against six `?` placeholders — a stray `nowUtcSeconds()` left over from an earlier draft that assumed a `created_real_ts` column this table does not have. D1 threw `Wrong number of parameter bindings for SQL query` on every test purchase. Fixed by dropping the extra bind argument and the now-unused import. **This is exactly the class of bug this codebase's test suite structurally cannot catch** — there is no D1 mock anywhere in `/test` (every DB-touching stage is verified by hand, per the existing convention this session followed rather than introduced), so a parameter-count mismatch inside a `.bind()` call is invisible to both the type checker (the SQL string and the bind args are two unrelated pieces of syntax as far as TypeScript is concerned) and a pure-function unit test. Worth remembering next time a slice feels "done" on green `tsc`/`npm test` alone: those two only prove the *shape* of the code, never that a hand-written SQL string and its bind list actually agree.

**2026-08-02 — Slice 0011 (ageing, death, and removal) built, both Part A and Part B in one session.** Migrations `0058`-`0060`. What a future session needs to know:

- **`src/engines/ageing/lifespan.ts` is the new pure engine.** `rollLifespanGameDays(rng, config)` is a clamped normal draw, rounded to a whole day — the clamp is a plain `Math.min(max, Math.max(min, raw))`, not a re-roll loop, so a degenerate `min === max` config can't spin forever. `ageState(horse, gameDay, config)` returns one of five states (`young`/`adult`/`veteran`/`failing`/`ended`), checked in that order: `ended` first (status ≠ `'alive'` beats everything), then `failing` (about *remaining* life — `natural_death_game_day - gameDay <= frailty_window_game_days`, which is why a short-lifespan horse can read failing before it ever reads veteran, a deliberate oddity §4.4 accepts), then `veteran`/`young`/`adult` (about age). A **null** `natural_death_game_day` can never read `failing`, at any age — the whole population born before this slice deployed sits safely in `adult`/`veteran` until the tick backfills them.
- **The lifespan is rolled once, at creation, from the horse's own seed (`deriveSeed(rng_seed, 'lifespan')`), and snapshotted onto `horses.natural_death_game_day` — never a hazard rolled every tick.** This is CLAUDE.md §5.4/§5.5 applied literally: a stored day compared with `<=` is idempotent for free (a re-fired tick finds the horse already dead; a missed tick still catches up), and retuning `lifespan_mean_game_days` etc. can never move a death date already assigned. `buildFoundingHorseInsertStatements` and `buildFoalInsertStatements` in `src/db/horses.ts` both gained a `lifespanConfig: LifespanRollConfig` parameter and roll+write the column themselves — every call site (`createFoundingHorse`, `claimOffer`, `stockShowBarn`, `foalOnePregnancy`) was updated to pass `config.values` (structurally satisfies the narrower `LifespanRollConfig` interface). Existing horses are backfilled by a tick stage, not a migration — see below — because a lifespan needs a genuine distribution and SQLite has no normal draw (unlike `0021_backfill_cycle_anchor.sql`'s uniform backfill, which could get away with `rng_seed % n`).
- **`src/db/ageing.ts` is the new database layer**, home to the shared exit path and the tick's two new stages. `buildEndHorseParticipationStatements(env, { horseId, sex, gameDay, status, endReason })` is the **one** function death and removal both call (§6.5 of the slice document is explicit about this: don't write the pregnancy cancellation twice) — it returns the `horses` status update, cancels this horse's own in-progress pregnancy and booked covering (on whichever side it appears — `dam_id OR sire_id`, `mare_id OR stallion_id`), and withdraws its entries in classes that haven't been judged yet. The `cancelled_reason` string (`dam_died`/`sire_died`/`dam_removed`/`sire_removed`) is computed by a tiny pulled-out pure function, `cancelledReasonFor(sex, status)` — pulled out **specifically so a test could call it without a D1 mock** (this codebase still has none; `test/ageing/removal.test.ts`'s own header comment says so directly, following `test/health/lethal.test.ts`'s precedent).
- **"Live" for a `coverings` or `pregnancies` row is now a two-part condition: `status` says so, AND `cancelled_game_day IS NULL`.** Both halves, every time — `migrations/0059_breeding_cancellation.sql` adds `cancelled_game_day`/`cancelled_reason` to both tables rather than widening `status` (SQLite can't `ALTER` a `CHECK` constraint without a full table rebuild, too much risk for a rare event). `resolveDueCoverings` (`src/db/coverings.ts`) and `foalDuePregnancies` (`src/db/pregnancies.ts`) both gained `AND cancelled_game_day IS NULL` on their due-selection queries — the single easiest thing in this slice to miss, and missing it means a dead mare's foal is born weeks after she's gone. Verified live: booked a covering, retired the mare away mid-season, and the covering never resolved on subsequent ticks (no `covering_conceived`/`covering_missed` event, no error).
- **The tick order that makes "foals first, then dies" true is real, not decorative.** `db/tick.ts` runs `foalDuePregnancies` → `killDueLethalFoals` → `assignLifespansAndNoticeFrailty` → `killDueOldHorses` → `createDueShows`. Because killing a horse *cancels* her still-`in_progress` pregnancy (via the shared exit path), running the death stage first would cancel a pregnancy before it could foal on the very same tick it was due — `test/ageing/tick.test.ts` models both effects explicitly (a "reverse order" test that shows the foal *not* being born) rather than just asserting the happy path, because the happy path alone wouldn't prove the order matters.
- **Two new event kinds, `horse_failing` and `horse_died_old_age`** — no migration needed, same `events.kind` free-text column as `condition_signs`/`horse_died`. `horse_died` is untouched and still means "died of a condition" — old age gets its own kind rather than a branch on payload, because `horse_died`'s render arm is written in the voice of a foal dying. Both wordings live in `render/stables.ts`'s `eventSentence` (feed voice) and are echoed, shorter, on the horse page itself (`renderHorsePage`'s `failingParagraph`) — same content, never a number of days or a date, per the slice's deliberate vagueness about `natural_death_game_day` (never rendered to a player, in any form, ever).
- **`listStableHorsesWithDead` gained a `cutoffGameDay` parameter** (`gameDay - barn_shows_ended_game_days`, live config) and now includes `removed` horses alongside `dead` ones — a retired-away horse gets the same "stays visible for a while, marked, then drops out" treatment a dead one already had from slice 0010. A new `listPastHorses` (no cutoff, `dead`/`removed` both, newest-ended first) backs `/stables/:id/past`, linked from the barn list.
- **`/horses/:id/retire`** (GET renders a confirmation page, POST performs it) is owner-only, 404s for a non-owner or a horse that's already ended, spends **no turn** (`src/lib/actions.ts`'s `ACTION_COSTS` deliberately did not gain a `retire_away` entry) and moves **no money**. The confirmation page's warnings (an active pregnancy, a booked covering, an unjudged show entry) are built fresh on every render by `buildRetireWarnings` in `routes/horses.ts`, naming the *other* horse involved on each side — a stallion can have several bookings across different mares, unlike a mare who has at most one.
- **A horse page's "manage" actions (Enter in a show, Test, Choose/Change picture, Retire away) are gated by one new flag, `canManage = owner && horse.status === 'alive'`** — distinct from `owner`, which still governs whether *content* (a paid-for health result, the barn-name form) is shown. Reading is never gated by `canManage`; only the four actions §8.1 explicitly names are.
- **`/admin/ageing`** (oldest living horses, deaths in the last 360 game days, and a bring-forward control) and **`/admin/shows`' new headcount-vs-target-plus-oldest-five block** both read `ageState` fresh via `src/db/ageing.ts`'s `listLivingHorsesForAdmin`/`listRecentDeaths`. The bring-forward control (`bringHorseDeathForward`) only ever moves `natural_death_game_day` to today — never age, pedigree, COI, or show eligibility — which is the argument for why it doesn't cross overview §6b's warning about advancing an individual horse; flagged in the slice document as the one place in this slice reasonable people could still differ.
- **`npm test` (296 tests, all passing) and `npx tsc --noEmit` are both clean.** `test/rng.test.ts`'s golden values are untouched. Four new files, `test/ageing/{lifespan,state,tick,removal}.test.ts` — the latter two are pure-predicate models mirroring the real SQL (no D1 mock exists, same convention slice 0010 established), documented as such in their own header comments so nobody mistakes them for integration tests.
- **Verified end-to-end against a live `wrangler dev --local`, all 60 migrations applied through `/admin/migrations` itself.** Created a mare, confirmed no Veteran/Failing marker at age 5; used `/admin/ageing`'s bring-forward control to move her death to "today"; confirmed the **Failing** badge and paragraph appeared immediately on her page (age-state is computed live, not tick-gated) even before advancing; advanced one tick and confirmed both the `horse_failing` event ("Juniper is 5 now, and she is starting to slow down...") **and** her death (`killDueOldHorses` ran in the same tick, since her death day had already arrived) landed correctly, with the death event correctly dropping the "leaves N foals" sentence for a childless mare rather than saying "zero foals"; confirmed she stayed in the barn list marked **Died**, with a game day, and appeared on `/stables/:id/past`. Separately: booked a covering between two other horses, confirmed the retire-away confirmation page named the exact booking and horse by name, confirmed the **Retired away** badge and game day after confirming, and confirmed the covering never resolved on five subsequent ticks (no event, no error). Confirmed every manage-action link (Retire away, Choose a picture, Enter in a show) is absent from both the dead and the retired-away horse's page. Confirmed `/admin/shows` shows the show barn's 0-of-6 headcount and a visible "below target" warning, and `/admin/config` renders all seven new fields without error.

**2026-08-02 — All eight breeds' conformation ideal vectors are written down in `docs/breed-ideal-vectors.md`. Seven of them are a data record only, not seeded.** No code and no migration in this change.

- **`breeds.ideal_vector` is still `NULL` for every breed but the Quarter Horse.** The document holds ready-to-paste JSON for the other seven, plus the reasoning behind each number. Whoever seeds them should read its §6 first: `createShowIfMissing` (`src/db/shows.ts`) creates one class per breed with a non-null vector, and `stockShowBarn` (`src/db/npc.ts`) generates **Quarter Horses only** — so seeding the seven today turns every show into eight classes, seven of which have no NPC padding at all. A breed-aware show barn, and a decision about what to do with classes nobody in the family can enter, both have to land alongside the migration.
- **Targets are deliberately kept off 50, and that is a constraint rather than a style choice.** Slice 0006 §155 flagged that a breed ideal sitting near the population middle would make inbreeding *help* on that trait, because realization and inbreeding depression both pull expressed values toward the bidirectional anchor of 50. That rules out the obvious "easy breed" vector of a flat 50/50/50/50 — it would reward exactly what the health slice's central dilemma is built to punish. The document's §5 carries an exposure index per breed; the Nokota needed a real shape rather than a shrug because of it, and the Quarter Horse's already-shipped hock target of 50 is why that trait carries the lowest weight on its row.
- **Ideal vectors are one of five things overview §4a calls breed identity.** `eligible_class_types`, `discipline_aptitudes`, `height_range`, `weight_range` and the seven non-Quarter-Horse disease panels are still unwritten anywhere, for all eight breeds. Recording the vectors does not close the breeds stage.

**2026-08-02 — Discipline shows: the framework, plus one discipline (Barrel Racing).** `docs/slices/0012-discipline-shows.md` built to the point of a real, playable second show class, deliberately scoped down from its own six disciplines to one — see below for why and what that changed.

- **The world reset this slice's §4.2 calls a hard precondition had NOT happened as of this build.** The operator confirmed in conversation that they will run a full-scope `/admin/reset` before this deploys, but it had not been run yet when this session ended. **Whoever deploys this: confirm the reset actually happened first.** The failure if it hasn't is silent per §4.2 — every pre-existing horse reads `agility ≈ 1`, dead last in Barrel Racing, with no error anywhere. Before doing any of this, the reset logic itself (`src/db/reset.ts`, `test/reset.test.ts`) was audited against every foreign key in the schema — delete order and table coverage were already correct, but `conditions` (health slice reference data) was missing from the "never cleared" documentation and test on both files, the same way `breeds`/`loci`/`judges` are listed. Fixed alongside `disciplines`, this slice's own new reference table, which needed the same treatment from the start.
- **`agility` is TRAITS' tenth trait, appended after `fertility`, never inserted.** `src/engines/genetics/polygenic.ts`. Appending is what keeps every earlier trait's RNG draw sequence reproducible (`polygenicGamete`/`inheritPolygenic` draw per trait, in `TRAITS` order, off one stream) — golden values in `test/rng.test.ts` and the inheritance tests stayed unchanged, confirmed by a full `npm test` run before and after. The list already reads as append-ordered (fertility broke the tidy category grouping first, with its own comment explaining why) — that visible oddity is deliberate, so the next session that wants to add a sixth ability trait sees the pattern and appends rather than inserting. `TRAIT_CATEGORY`/`TRAIT_DIRECTION` in `src/engines/conformation/traits.ts` gained an `agility: 'ability'`/`'higher_better'` entry, and that file gained a new export, `ABILITY_TRAITS` (`TRAITS` filtered to category `'ability'`) — `CONFORMATION_TRAITS`'s exact counterpart, and what `scoreAbilityEntry` iterates instead of `Object.keys(weights)`, for the same "a result's breakdown always reads in the same order" reason `scoreEntry` iterates `CONFORMATION_TRAITS`.
- **`abilityValues()` lives in `src/engines/conformation/model.ts`, beside `conformationValues`, not in a new file.** Same potential → geneticValue → realization → expressedValue pipeline, mapped over `ABILITY_TRAITS` instead, with `anchorFor`'s existing 0-for-higher_better branch doing the real work (a young horse is straightforwardly worse, not pulled toward a midpoint). **Naming debt, now real and worth knowing:** this file's name and path (`engines/conformation/`) no longer describe everything it holds — `traits.ts:44`'s own comment anticipated exactly this ("a later slice displaying ability traits needs no new expression function, only this lookup"). Flagged at the top of the file itself, not just here, so a session grepping for "conformation" and finding ability-trait code isn't left wondering if it's misplaced.
- **`scoreAbilityEntry` is a new file, `src/engines/showing/abilityScore.ts`, not a widened `scoreEntry`.** `rawScore = Σ(weight·expressed)/Σweight`, `finalScore = rawScore · careModifier · tackModifier · trainingFactor + noise` — three modifiers, all pinned at 1.0, carried ahead of care/tack/training exactly the way `scoreEntry`'s and `realization()`'s own modifiers already are. A missing weight key reads as **0** here (a discipline that doesn't care about a trait at all), the opposite of `judgeWeights`' missing-key-means-1.0 in `scoreEntry` — easy to get backwards, worth restating anywhere it matters.
- **`show_classes` was widened to hold a `discipline` class alongside a `breed_conformation` one — migration `0064`, and it needed a real correction to the slice document's own plan.** The slice cites migration `0057` (the `ledger` rebuild) as the worked precedent for a SQLite table rebuild, but doesn't carry forward the caveat `0057`'s own comment states: that rebuild was safe *specifically* because nothing had a live foreign key into `ledger`. `show_entries.class_id` **does** reference `show_classes`, and — verified directly against a live `wrangler d1 execute --local` scratch test before writing the migration, not assumed — D1 enforces foreign keys, so `DROP TABLE show_classes` while `show_entries` still references it fails immediately with `FOREIGN KEY constraint failed`. `PRAGMA foreign_keys=OFF` is not an escape hatch either: it's a no-op once a transaction has begun (confirmed the same way), and every migration's statements land in one `env.DB.batch()`, i.e. one transaction, so there is no way to toggle it mid-migration. **The fix:** move the referencing table out of the way first. `0064` copies `show_entries` to a plain, constraint-free holding table (`CREATE TABLE ... AS SELECT`, which copies data, not constraints), drops the real `show_entries` (safe — nothing references *it*), rebuilds `show_classes` with nothing pointing at it, then recreates `show_entries` in its original shape and reloads it from the holding table. Verified end to end against `wrangler d1 execute --local` on scratch parent/child tables before trusting the pattern, then again via all six real migrations applying clean through both `wrangler d1 migrations apply --local` and a fresh `npx tsc`/`npm test` pass. **Any future session rebuilding a table with a live incoming foreign key needs this same two-step, not `0057`'s single-step version** — `0057` remains the right precedent only for a table nothing points into.
- **`show_entries.conformation_snapshot` renamed to `trait_snapshot`, migration `0065`, a plain `ALTER TABLE ... RENAME COLUMN`** (no rebuild needed — nothing references `show_entries`). The blob shape never changed, only the name; it was already trait-agnostic (`{"v":1,"traits":{...},"age_years":...,"coi":...}`), and a column called `conformation_snapshot` holding `{"speed":62}` is exactly the kind of thing that misleads a session with no memory of this one.
- **`score_breakdown` gained a `"kind"` key** (`"conformation"` or `"ability"`) so the result-explanation route/render can branch cleanly — an ability entry has no `target` column (there is no target for an ability trait, only a weight and a contribution), so `render/shows.ts`'s `renderEntryResultPage` now takes a discriminated-union `traits` array and picks its table header accordingly. An old row with no `kind` key reads as conformation (there is no such row after the world reset actually happens, but the fallback costs nothing).
- **The tick needed no new stage.** `createShowIfMissing` (`src/db/shows.ts`) now also loops `getEnabledDisciplines`, capped at the new live tunable `show_discipline_classes_per_show` (config, migration `0066`, seeded at 6 per the full design even though only one discipline exists to draw from today), numbering discipline classes after breed classes in the same `class_N`/`judge_N` sub-seed sequence. `judgeOneClass` branches once at the top on `cls.class_type` — everything after (NPC top-up, placings, `horse_show_summary`, prize ledger rows, the single atomic batch, closing the show) is unchanged and doesn't care which scorer produced the numbers. A conformation judge's `trait_weights` contributes nothing to an ability score (`scoreAbilityEntry` iterates `ABILITY_TRAITS`, which no conformation judge has keys for) — correct per the slice, not a bug, though it does mean a Barrel Racing class can be judged by someone whose blurb is about conformation standards, which reads a little oddly on the show page. Flagged, not fixed — §15 of the slice document raises "should disciplines have their own judges" as an open question for later.
- **The horse page's "Enter in a show" section now lists every open class, not just the first.** Before this slice there was only ever one open class at a time, so `buildEnterShowInfo` (singular) only ever checked `getOpenClasses(...)[0]`. Renamed `buildEnterShowInfos` (plural), checks every open class, returns a list; `render/horses.ts`'s `enterShowBlock` renders one button-or-reason line per class. `EnterShowInfo` itself didn't need to change shape, only its cardinality.
- **`/admin/shows`' recent-shows summary (`listShowsForAdmin`) still assumes one class per show** — its own comment already flagged this ("a later slice adding a second class type should give this its own per-class rows"), and this slice didn't fix it: with two classes per show now, the admin list's judge/winner columns pick one of the two classes arbitrarily (no crash, just an imprecise summary). Left as pre-flagged technical debt rather than expanded scope; worth a real fix whenever `/admin/shows` gets touched next for its own reasons.
- **Only Barrel Racing is seeded** (migration `0063`, `disciplines` migration `0062`) — a deliberate, explicit deviation from the slice document's own §6.3, which seeds all six. Asked and confirmed with the operator before writing any code: seed one now, add the rest later as pure data (`CLAUDE.md` §9's "do not build ahead"), rather than seeding all six with five disabled via `disciplines.enabled` (which the slice's own §13.1 designs as a later kill-switch, not a not-built-yet marker). Consequence worth knowing: slice §11 item 5's "every ability is dominant in exactly one discipline" test is a property of the full six-discipline design and doesn't hold for one discipline — `test/genetics/consistency.test.ts` instead checks the property that does hold today (agility is Barrel Racing's strict max weight) and says in a comment why the fuller test waits.
- Verified end-to-end against a live `wrangler dev --local` with all 66 migrations applied through the CLI: two 5-year-old Quarter Horse founders bred through `/admin/horses/new`; a tick advance to game day 30 creating both a Quarter Horse Conformation and a Barrel Racing class in the same show, and — since `show_entry_window_game_days` also equals 30 — both classes immediately judging themselves with zero entries in the same tick with no error (the "nobody entered" branch working for a discipline class exactly like a conformation one); entering both horses in both classes of the *next* show and confirming `trait_snapshot` held the right four-vs-five trait sets (conformation entries: `neck_length`/`shoulder_angle`/`back_length`/`hock_set`; discipline entries: `stamina`/`jump_scope`/`speed`/`trainability`/`agility`); judging that show and watching the two horses split the wins (the conformation-strong horse won breed conformation, the agility-strong one won Barrel Racing — the exact "two children can see the horses are good at different things" outcome §1 of the slice document describes); the entry-result page rendering the ability breakdown with no target column and contributions summing correctly to the raw score (hand-verified: `6.8+0+47.6+49.6+102.0=206.0`, `÷3.9=52.82`, `+1.89 noise=54.71`, matching the page exactly); prize money landing in the ledger for both class types and `horse_show_summary` counting both starts correctly; re-firing the tick a further time with no double-judging, no second prize row, no summary double-count; and both `/shows` and `/admin/shows` rendering without error. `npm test` (all 41 files, 310 tests) and `npx tsc --noEmit` both clean.

**2026-08-02 — Founding-horse names: a player-typed name word on the claim form, and origin prefixes shortened to a letter-combo stamp.** Two rounds on the operator's "let me name founding horses, the generator isn't creative" ask, converging on something that respects slice 0005 §2.6/§6.5's rule that a founding horse's `registered_name` prefix must never be the claiming stable's own (it means *bred by*, and this horse wasn't).

- First pass (superseded, see below): a separate barn-name field at claim time. Reverted — it left the actual registered name exactly as generated, which wasn't the fix that was asked for once the operator clarified.
- **What shipped instead:** the claim screen (`/stables/:id/founding`, "open" state) lets a player retype the *name word only* — the part after the origin prefix — for any candidate, before claiming. The origin prefix (the part that carries the "bred by" meaning) stays generator-assigned; only the word after it is ever player-supplied. `src/routes/founding.ts`'s `claim` handler reads `name_part_<slot_index>` off the form, validates it with the same `validateHorseNamePart` the existing "register a name" form already uses, and builds a `Map<candidateId, string>` of overrides passed into `claimOffer` (`src/db/founding.ts`). `resolveUniqueName` there now takes that optional override: with one, it tries exactly that name once and reports `{ ok: false, error: 'name_taken' }` on a collision (no silent substitution — a player who typed a name gets told if it's taken, not handed something else); with none, it falls back to the original deterministic-sequence walk unchanged. `ClaimOfferResult` gained the `name_taken` branch and `claimErrorMessage` a matching sentence.
- **Origin prefixes are now two-letter codes, not full stable names.** `src/engines/founding/names.ts`'s `ORIGIN_PREFIXES` string array became `ORIGIN_STABLES`, a `{ code, full }` list ("Silver Creek" → `SC`, "Rocking R" → `RR`, ...) — `generateFoundingName` now returns `entry.code` as `originPrefix`, so a claimed horse's registered name reads like a real breeder's stamp ("SC Comet"), not a stable's whole name spelled out in front of every horse. This flows automatically into the NPC show barn too (`src/db/npc.ts`'s `resolveUniqueBarnName` calls the same `generateFoundingName`). The full name didn't disappear — `originStableFullName(code)` is a new lookup export, used by the founding claim screen's candidate cards ("bred by Silver Creek") and by `renderHorsePage`'s bred-by line (`src/render/horses.ts`, "SC — Silver Creek (a founding stable, not one in this game)") — cosmetic only, never stored on the row; `horses.breeder_prefix` holds just the code.

`horseDisplayName` (barn name over registered name) is untouched by any of this — this round only changes what the *registered* name looks like and how much of it a player can type themselves. `npm test` (41 files, 310 tests) and `npx tsc --noEmit` both clean after each round.

**2026-08-03 — Care (Part A only): farrier and wellness timers, feed, and the ±5% show-score modifier.** `docs/slices/0013-care-and-condition.md`. Part B (condition management for HYPP/PSSM1) was deliberately not built this session — the slice's own §12 sanctions dropping it when a session runs long, and this one did. `conditionDelta` is a real parameter on `careModifier()` but is always passed as `0`; no `horse_conditions.management_state` or `conditions.management_options` column exists yet, per that same section's instruction not to add a column until something writes it.

- **New directory, `src/engines/care/modifier.ts`** — the reusable shape for the next timer-based mechanic (tack wear will likely want the same one): a piecewise-linear ramp (`timerState`) that is `+bonus` the moment a call is made, decays linearly to `0` at the interval, then ramps linearly to `-penalty` at the "fully overdue" day and stays flat forever past it. No cliff edges anywhere, by construction — `test/care/modifier.test.ts` has a continuity test that walks every day from 0 to `overdueGameDays + 50` and asserts no jump exceeds the ramp's own slope, which is the test worth copying wholesale for any future ramp. `careModifier()` sums every component's delta and clamps **last**, to `[care_modifier_min, care_modifier_max]` — the clamp, not the components, is what guarantees the band, so a future component (Part B's condition penalty, eventually tack) is just one more addend into the same sum.
- **`conformation/model.ts`'s `careFactor` parameter is left unused forever, on purpose.** Slice §2.3's reasoning, worth restating here since it's easy to "fix" by accident: care multiplies the *show score*, never the *displayed conformation numbers* — wiring it into `realization()` as well would double-count it (realization feeds `rawScore`, which the care modifier then multiplies a second time) and would make a horse's neck visibly change length because the farrier came, which teaches a false thing about horses. If a future slice wants care to affect a horse's actual body (weight, muscling), that's a new idea needing `horses.weight` and a body-condition model — not this one.
- **`horses.care_notice_game_day` is the reusable pattern for a "once per crossing" tick notice**, distinct from `horses.frailty_notice_game_day` (slice 0011) only in that a care call *clears it back to NULL* (frailty's marker never resets — a horse only fails once). Any future once-per-threshold-crossing notice that the underlying condition can also *recover from* wants this clear-on-recovery shape, not frailty's fire-once shape.
- **A horse's care timers can start non-null at creation, not just at deploy.** The backfill migration (`0068`) only covers horses alive when this shipped; going forward, founding-stock claims are minted at 4-8 game years (`founding_age_min/max_game_days`) — already well past the 3-year `care_start_age_game_days` — so a freshly claimed horse with `last_farrier_game_day = NULL` would read as **immediately, fully overdue** the moment a child claims it, for care nobody ever had the chance to give. Caught by reasoning through the founding flow, not by a failing test. Fixed in `buildFoundingHorseInsertStatements` (`src/db/horses.ts`): when a horse's age at creation already exceeds `care_start_age_game_days`, both timers are seeded to the creation game day instead of `NULL`. This threads two new required fields (`careStartAgeGameDays`, `currentGameDay`) through every founding-horse creation path — `createFoundingHorse`/the admin form, `claimOffer`/a founding-stock claim, and `stockShowBarn`/the NPC barn — so check all three if this input shape ever changes again. A genuine newborn foal is untouched: it's always below care-start age at the moment of birth, so it keeps reading `NULL` → `not_yet`, exactly as before.
- **Feed multiplies the existing upkeep charge; it does not create a second ledger kind.** `computeUpkeep` (`src/lib/upkeep.ts`) gained an optional `feedMultiplier` (default 1, so every pre-existing call site is untouched), applied and **then rounded once** — money is always an integer. `farrier` is a genuinely new ledger kind (migration `0070`, a `ledger` table rebuild identical in shape to `0057`'s); wellness visits and (eventually) Part B's management plans reuse the existing `vet` kind, and feed rides the existing `upkeep` kind — three services, one new kind, on purpose.
- **The barn round's idempotency guard is worth re-reading if you touch it**: `UPDATE horses SET last_farrier_game_day = ? WHERE id = ? AND (last_farrier_game_day IS NULL OR last_farrier_game_day < ?)`, and the charge is computed from `meta.changes` on the statements that actually landed — never from the candidate count read a moment earlier. A double-tapped round charges once; the second request's guard fails for every horse the first request already touched.
- **NPC horses are kept current by the tick (`noticeCareDue`'s `stampNpcHorsesCurrent`), never exempted in the scorer** — `db/shows.ts`'s scoring loop calls the identical `careModifierForHorse` for every horse regardless of who owns it, per `CLAUDE.md` §13's "no parallel scoring path for NPC horses."
- **The show-result snapshot (`show_entries.care_modifier_applied`, migration `0071`) is written at judging time and never recomputed** — `db/shows.ts`'s `judgeOneClass` now looks up each involved horse's owner stable's `feed_level` (one query per unique stable, not per horse) and folds `careModifierForHorse(...)` into both `scoreEntry`/`scoreAbilityEntry` calls. The `score_breakdown` JSON also gained a `care: { modifier, farrier_status, wellness_status }` object so the result page can say *why* ("shoes overdue") without recomputing the ramp against a horse whose care state has since moved on.
- **`config.flags.care_notice_enabled`**, not a `config.values` key, even though the slice document describes it as a plain 0/1 value — this codebase already has exactly one home for a boolean on/off switch (`flags`, alongside `force_next_twins`), and using it keeps `ConfigValues` free of a lone boolean-shaped number. Toggled from a button on `/admin/care`, not from the `/admin/config` form (which has no flag-editing UI at all today — every existing flag is set from its own feature's admin page).
- **`/admin/care`** (read counts of fresh/due-soon/due/overdue per timer, a ten-bucket care-modifier distribution, each stable's feed level and board cost, the notice on/off toggle, and the "make every horse overdue" testing control) follows `/admin/ageing`'s exact shape for the same reason: the real verification window (weeks of a horse going overdue) is otherwise inconvenient to wait for by hand.
- **`src/db/reset.ts` needed no change** — confirmed by reading it, per the slice's own §8.6 instruction: care lives in columns on `horses` and `stables`, both already fully cleared or fully kept by the existing scopes, and every ledger/event row this slice writes already has a home in the existing table lists.
