- **Verified the migration SQL directly against Python's `sqlite3` module** (this sandbox had no `wrangler`/`sqlite3` CLI and no live Cloudflare account to `wrangler dev` against) rather than only reading it: both the `json_set(... , json('true'))` flags boolean and the full `feed_levels` JSON blob round-trip exactly as `FeedLevelsConfig` expects, and the `ledger` table-rebuild migration accepts a `farrier` row afterward with the old `vet` row still intact. Whoever does the next real deploy should still run it through `/admin/migrations` once and sanity-check `/admin/care` and a farrier call by hand, per the slice's own §11 hand-verification list.
- `npm test` (43 files, 334 tests — 24 new, covering the ramp's anchor points/continuity/monotonicity/clamp, the feed lookup's unknown-key fallback, `careModifierForHorse`'s care-start-age handling, and a `scoreEntry` regression pinning the modifier to the correct side of the noise addition) and `npx tsc --noEmit` both clean.

**2026-08-03 — the location flag: `src/engines/care/location.ts`, and why the settling period is load-bearing.** A horse is either `barn` or `pasture` (`horses.location`). At pasture it still ages, still grows, still carries every gene, and still dies of old age on the day `natural_death_game_day` says — but its care timers are frozen, it costs no board, and it cannot be bred or shown. Three things a future session should not undo without understanding them:

- **`workAvailability()` is the single implementation of "can this horse work".** Show eligibility takes it as a resolved `WorkAvailability` value rather than re-deriving it from `(location, changedDay, settleDays)`, and breeding calls the same function directly. That is deliberate: breeding is not a show and never goes through `checkEligibility`, so a copy of the rule would have been the second place it lived, and the two would have drifted the first time the settling period was retuned.
- **`horses.location_changed_game_day` does two jobs on purpose.** While the horse is out it is when the care timers stopped, so bringing it in credits `game_day - that` back onto both (`bringInFromPasture`, with a `MIN(..., game_day)` belt-and-braces cap). While the horse is in it is when the settling window started. The invariant making the credit exact is that **care calls are refused for a horse at pasture** — the route checks and `callOneHorseCare`'s own `UPDATE` re-checks with `AND location = 'barn'`. If you ever add a path that shoes a pastured horse, that credit silently over-counts.
- **The settling period (`pasture_settle_game_days`, 30) is what makes free board defensible.** Pasture costs nothing (`pasture_upkeep_multiplier`, 0 — a live tunable, raise it if board stops being a real sink). Without a wait on the way back in, the optimal play is to park every horse outside, pay nothing, and bring one in on show morning. With it, turning a horse out is a commitment to skipping the next show. Do not set it to 0 without replacing the trade-off with another one.

`timerState` gained an optional `atPasture` and a sixth `CareStatus`, `at_pasture` — optional so every pre-flag caller and test kept meaning what it meant. A pastured horse also carries **no feed delta**: feed is what is in the barn's mangers, and a horse at grass is outside that trade, so it comes out at exactly 1.00 with every component neutral. `computeUpkeep` takes `pastureHorses`/`pastureMultiplier` alongside `aliveHorses`/`feedMultiplier`, with the two groups **rounded separately then added** so a barn with nothing out is charged byte-for-byte what it was before the flag existed.

**2026-08-03 — slice 0014, all three parts (age decline, condition management, the robustness genes), and the one argument a future session most needs from it.**

- **`TRAITS` being append-only is not a style preference — it is the whole reason Part C exists as a separate, time-boxed piece of work.** `polygenic.ts`'s `TRAITS` array is the source of truth for both display order *and* the RNG draw sequence every polygenic stream reads in lockstep (`pool_polygenic`, `founder_polygenic`, the two inheritance gametes, `birth_noise`) — each draws a fixed number of values per trait, in `TRAITS`' own order, so appending a trait only ever adds draws at the *end* of every stream and never perturbs an earlier one (proved, not asserted, by `test/founding/generate.test.ts`'s "appending robustness traits changed no earlier draw" test, which pins byte-exact bit strings captured at the moment the traits were appended — capture those before editing `TRAITS`, because they cannot be recovered afterward). The cost of this discipline is that `TRAITS` reads as an odd, un-tidy list — conformation, then ability, then `fertility` (slice 0003), then `agility` (slice 0012), then the three robustness traits (slice 0014) — and that oddness is deliberate: it is what tells the next session that inserting into the middle of this list is never safe, only appending to the end. **The reason this couldn't wait for the slice that actually uses these traits:** a genotype is written once, at birth, and a missing polygenic key reads as zero-out-of-twenty (the bottom of the range) forever — `getPolygenicString`'s pad-with-zeros behaviour, which is correct for "this horse predates this trait and truly can't be measured on it" but is a *wrong answer masquerading as a real one* for a soundness trait, where zero means "maximally fragile". A horse born before the reset in slice 0014 §11 would have been permanently, incorrectly unsound the moment anything read this trait. Compare this to a **Mendelian** locus (frame overo, say): a missing locus reads as two copies of wild-type via `getMendelianPair`, which is the *correct* answer (a horse that predates the locus genuinely doesn't carry it) — that is why colour loci were safe to defer indefinitely (`docs/horse-game-overview.md` §3b, corrected this session) and polygenic traits were not.

- **`ROBUSTNESS_TRAITS` (`src/engines/conformation/traits.ts`) is the band-exemption convention** for any future hidden trait that must not ride a founding batch's quality band. `src/engines/founding/generate.ts`'s polygenic-draw loop now branches per-trait: `ROBUSTNESS_TRAITS.includes(trait) ? input.robustnessOneChance : input.polygenicOneChance`. `robustnessOneChance` is a **required** field on `GenerateCandidateInput`, deliberately not optional-with-a-default — an optional field would silently fall back to the quality band the day a second caller of `generateCandidate` is added (there are two today, `mintOffer`'s founding-batch path and `stockShowBarn`'s NPC path, both updated), and that silent fallback is exactly the failure this exists to prevent. If a fourth robustness-shaped trait is ever added, add its code to `ROBUSTNESS_TRAITS` and nothing else needs to change — the generator, the category exclusion (`'hidden'`), and the display exclusion all key off that one list plus `TRAIT_CATEGORY`.

- **`ageModifier` is a fourth multiplier on both scorers (`scoreEntry`/`scoreAbilityEntry`), deliberately not folded into `careModifier`.** The reason is the clamp: `careModifier` is clamped to `[care_modifier_min, care_modifier_max]` (±5%), and slice 0013 §2.4 was explicit that the *clamp*, not the components, is what guarantees that band. A −0.15 age delta living inside that clamp would mean a twenty-five-year-old and a neglected three-year-old produce the same number, and the clamp would silently absorb ageing's entire effect. `ageModifier` therefore has its own parameter, its own default of 1.0, and its own snapshot column (`show_entries.age_modifier_applied`, mirroring `care_modifier_applied` exactly) — two lines in the result explanation, one for the fixable thing (care) and one for the unfixable one (age), per slice 0013 §13.4's own anticipation of this. `src/engines/ageing/performance.ts`'s `agePerformanceModifier` reads `gameDay - bornGameDay` and nothing else — it does not import or reference `ageState`/the Failing marker (`lifespan.ts`) at all, which is what makes it structurally impossible for a future edit to key age decline off remaining life instead of age in days (slice 0011 §3.2's decline-refusal argument, reversed on the numbers but not on that distinction).

- **Condition management's entitlement is resolved through `ownerVisibleStatus` (`src/engines/health/status.ts`), never a second version of the rule** — `src/db/health.ts`'s new `manageableConditionStates` calls it directly, passing a synthetic `known` object only when a genotype-kind knowledge row exists. The trap named in the slice document is real and worth repeating: HYPP and PSSM1 both have `signs_visible = 1` in the seed data, so `ownerVisibleStatus` returns affected for free on both, and the knowledge boundary **never fires in play** for either real condition. `test/health/management.test.ts` exercises it against a fabricated `signs_visible = 0` condition instead — the only way to actually test the boundary rather than the screen.

- **The judging path batches condition-management lookups per class, not per horse** (`conditionDeltaMapForHorses`, `src/db/health.ts`): one query for every entered horse's manageable `horse_conditions` rows, one for their `horse_knowledge` genotype rows, grouped in JS before the scoring loop — same shape `feedLevelByStableId` already used for care. `careModifierForHorse` (`src/db/care.ts`) gained a `conditionDelta` parameter defaulting to 0, so every caller that predates Part B (there were several) keeps compiling and keeps meaning what it meant.

- **NPC show-barn horses get management plans kept current by the tick, alongside their shoes** — `stampNpcHorsesCurrent` (`src/db/care.ts`) now also `UPDATE`s `horse_conditions.management_state`/`management_until_game_day` for every NPC-owned horse's manageable rows, in the same batch as the farrier/wellness stamp. This is the same rule slice 0013 §2.6 established for care: NPC horses are kept current by the tick, never exempted at the scorer, because `CLAUDE.md` §13 forbids a parallel scoring path and a special case here would have been exactly that.

- **`conditions.management_text` is plain text, not the `management_options` JSON both slice 0010 and slice 0013 sketched.** Nothing in this slice consumes a structure (no priced options, no per-option effects), and an unread JSON column is a promise nobody keeps (`CLAUDE.md` §7). If a later slice needs structured options, add them against a real reader then.

- **The world reset (slice 0014 §11) is a precondition for Part C, not a suggestion** — every horse alive before the deploy is permanently missing the three robustness traits (see the append-only argument above), so shipping Part C without the reset produces a population where some horses carry real, inherited robustness genes and others read zero-out-of-twenty forever. The order that matters: deploy, apply migrations `0075`–`0082`, hand-verify against the old world (§10), *then* `/admin/reset` full-world-scope, *then* re-found stables and hand out logins. This is the operator's step to run from the dashboard, not something a build session does from the repository.

- `npm test` (46 files, 380 tests — 30 new, covering the age curve's worked examples/flat-before-start/floor/degenerate-config behaviour, the scorers' default-1.0 promise for `ageModifier`, the robustness traits' band-independence and inheritance, the append-only-`TRAITS` reproducibility pin, and the condition-management knowledge boundary/stacking/clamp/renewal-boundary-day) and `npx tsc --noEmit` both clean. All 82 migrations, including the eight new ones, verified to apply cleanly against a real local D1/SQLite database via `wrangler d1 migrations apply --local` — not just parsed, actually run.

One rule is a judgement call worth revisiting if it grates: **a mare in foal cannot be turned out at all.** In life a broodmare on grass is completely ordinary. The reason for the restriction here is that "at pasture" in this game means "nothing is happening to this horse", and a foal arriving into a location whose definition is that its care timers do not run is the one day that matters most. It is enforced in `turnOutBlockedReason()`, used by both the page (to explain instead of offering the button) and the POST (to refuse), so the two can never disagree.

**2026-08-03 — Slice 0015 (NPC stables), Part A only: the data, the upkeep exemption, and `src/engines/npc/`.** Followed `docs/slices/0015-npc-stables.md` §11's own split at the tick stage, per the operator's explicit choice this session — the tick stage, NPC foal naming, the generalised show top-up and `/admin/npc` are all still to build. Nothing in this half changes what a player sees.

- **`src/engines/npc/selection.ts` is new** — same pure-function pattern as `src/engines/showing/`. `selectBreedingPairs` reuses `scoreEntry`/`scoreAbilityEntry` directly (`noise: 0`) rather than a second scoring formula; `clampedScore` is exported on its own because it's the one place a future retune of the judge could silently change what "the ceiling" means without a test at this level noticing. **The pattern worth reusing elsewhere:** when you need a bounded-but-not-falsified comparison of a *quality*, clamp the quality metric the scorer already returns per-trait (`traitScore` for a bidirectional/conformation trait, `expressed` for a unidirectional/ability trait) and re-aggregate with the scorer's own weights — clamping the raw expressed value instead is the tempting version and the wrong one, because a bidirectional trait's "high" isn't "good" (slice 0015 §2.4's own worked example: hock 50 and hock 62 should read identically against a ceiling of 76, hock 90 should not, and only clamping `traitScore` produces that shape).
- **Two new sub-seed labels**, both via `deriveSeed` off the caller-supplied `seed` (a stable id + the cycle's `game_day`, once Part B wires a caller in — nothing stores a new seed column for this): `npc_selection_noise_<horseId>` and `npc_retention_<horseId>`, keyed on the horse rather than on iteration order, so the ranking and the retention roll don't depend on what order a caller happens to list candidates in.
- **The round-robin pairing detail worth remembering if you touch this file:** a mare's *default* stallion partner is the one at `rank(mare) % stallions.length`, not "the next available one" — a COI exclusion tries subsequent stallions in rotation order for that mare only, without disturbing any other mare's default partner. Test 6 in `test/npc/selection.test.ts` (one stallion, three mares) is the test that would catch a regression to strict rank-for-rank pairing.
- **`npc_policy`'s `target_kind`/`target_breed_id`/`target_discipline_code` replace the schema document's sketched free-form `selection_weights` JSON** — the weights already exist on `breeds.ideal_vector`/`disciplines.ability_weights`, and a second copy would drift the moment either is retuned. Same reasoning, no `quality_ceiling` column on `npc_policy` at all: §2.4 reads the active `npc_ceiling_schedule` row live, every cycle, never caches it — this is the live-tunable half of `CLAUDE.md` §5.5, deliberately not the snapshotted half, because the ceiling is supposed to move under a stable that never changes anything about itself.
- **`chargeUpkeep` (`src/db/upkeep.ts`) now filters `AND is_npc = 0`** — third instance of the "an NPC stable is exempt from X because there's nobody behind the wheel to respond to it" pattern, after `noticeCareDue`'s farrier/vet skip (slice 0013 §2.6) and now board. Migration `0086` does the one-off correction: every NPC stable's balance back to zero via a paired `adjustment` ledger row (never a bare `UPDATE stables SET balance`), so the balance-equals-sum-of-ledger invariant in `src/db/ledger.ts` survives.
- **A gap found and closed that the slice document itself puts in Part B, moved forward into Part A instead, because it's a correctness bug rather than new behaviour:** `npc_policy` has a real foreign key into `stables(id)`, and D1 enforces foreign keys (confirmed again directly against a live local D1 this session — `DELETE FROM stables WHERE prefix='Cedar Hollow'` with a live `npc_policy` row pointing at it fails immediately with `FOREIGN KEY constraint failed: SQLITE_CONSTRAINT`). Leaving `npc_policy` out of `src/db/reset.ts`'s `RESET_TABLES` until Part B would have meant `/admin/reset`'s full-world scope breaking outright the moment this migration landed — not a future gap, a live one. `npc_policy` is now in `WORLD_ONLY_TABLES`, positioned before `stables` (children before parents, `CLAUDE.md`-established convention for that list), and the world-reset re-seed block (previously just Fair Meadow) now recreates all three NPC stables and all three `npc_policy` rows. `npc_ceiling_schedule` stays out of `RESET_TABLES` entirely, in the same reference-data category as `breeds`/`loci`/`disciplines` — added to that category's list in the file's own header comment and in `test/reset.test.ts`'s `NEVER_CLEARED`. **Real, current limitation this leaves:** Cedar Hollow and Willow Creek Barrels have no stocking control yet at all (that's Part B's outcross-batch control, slice 0015 §7.3) — both stables are real, seeded, and empty, and stay empty until either Part B ships or someone writes rows by hand.
- **New stable-prefix constants**, `CEDAR_HOLLOW_PREFIX` and `WILLOW_CREEK_BARRELS_PREFIX` in `src/db/npc.ts`, next to the existing `SHOW_BARN_PREFIX` — used by `reset.ts`'s re-seed block today; Part B's tick stage and `/admin/npc` should reuse these rather than restating the literal strings.
- Verified against a live local D1 (`wrangler d1 migrations apply heritage-hooves --local`, all 86 migrations, including a full teardown and clean re-apply): the seeded rows read as expected (Cedar Hollow/Willow Creek Barrels stables, all three `npc_policy` rows, the four `npc_ceiling_schedule` rows), no adjustment ledger row was written on a fresh database (correct — nothing had accrued board yet), and the delete-order fix was checked both ways by hand — the wrong order reproduces the FK failure, the corrected order (prefix history, then policy, then the stable) succeeds. `npm test` (48 files, 393 tests — 8 new in `test/npc/selection.test.ts`, plus `test/reset.test.ts`'s existing order-checking test extended to cover `npc_policy`) and `npx tsc --noEmit` both clean.
- **What's left for Part B**, in the order slice 0015 §6/§7 lays out: the `runNpcBreedingDecisions` tick stage (`src/db/npcBreeding.ts`, new) wired in before `resolveDueCoverings`; NPC-bred foal naming in `foalDuePregnancies`; `judgeOneClass`'s show top-up generalised to every `is_npc = 1` stable rather than only Fair Meadow; and `/admin/npc` (roster/ceiling display, the ceiling-schedule editor, "found a new NPC stable," and the outcross-batch control that's the only way Cedar Hollow or Willow Creek ever get horses). Nothing in Part A needs revisiting to build any of that — it's additive from here.

**2026-08-03 — Slice 0016, all six parts (barn tabs, shows filters + NPC-only results, `/world`, the placing screen, `/admin/accounts` modify/delete, and the admin PIN gate) built in one session.** No formula moved — every part is a screen, a query, or an admin capability, per `docs/slices/0016-admin-and-ui-improvements.md` §1's own promise.

- **`src/lib/barnFilter.ts`'s `bucketFor` is the reusable shape for the next "which tab does this row belong to" question.** One pure function, one bucket per row, no overlap — a colt under `foal_max_age_game_days` reads as Foals and nothing else, never Foals-and-Stallions. `foal_max_age_game_days` is display-only by construction: nothing outside `stableHorsesRoute` reads it, and the migration comment says so explicitly so a future session doesn't quietly wire it into a mechanic.
- **The barn tabs' hidden `show` field is a pattern worth repeating wherever a POST redirects back to a filtered list**: `stableCareRoute`/`stableFeedRoute` read `form.show` and append it to the redirect target with a `joiner` variable (`?` vs `&`) computed once, rather than string-concatenating an assumed `?`. A round or a feed save now returns you to the tab you were on.
- **`src/db/shows.ts`'s `classMatchesShowsFilter` is the one predicate every filtered view of a show's classes shares** — the SQL `EXISTS` clause `listRecentJudgedShowsFiltered` builds and the in-memory filter `loadVisibleClasses` (routes/shows.ts) applies are built from the same three conditions, so a show appearing in the filtered list can never show a different set of classes when opened. Item 8 (a judged class with no player entry publishes no results) is folded into the same helper's sibling, `classHasPlayerEntry`, applied uniformly to the list query, the per-show class list, and the entry-result "Why?" page — three call sites, one rule.
- **`/world` is a genuinely separate render module (`src/render/world.ts`) that has never seen a `HorseRow`.** `src/routes/world.ts`'s `buildPublicHorseView` is a pure function typed against `Pick<HorseRow, ...>` — a narrow, explicit subset — so passing the full row in is harmless (structural typing) but the function's own body cannot read `coi`/`genotype`/care state even by accident. `test/world.test.ts` attaches those forbidden fields to a fake input and asserts they don't survive into the output — the test that fails loudly the day someone `...horse`-spreads instead of naming fields. **This is the pattern worth reusing for the next "public view of private data" screen**: a Pick-typed input, a hand-built output object literal (never a spread), and a render module that imports the output type and nothing from the owner-only layer.
- **`horsePageRoute`'s non-owner branch changed from `notFound()` to a redirect into `/world/horses/:id`** (§6.5) — a link pasted between two children now resolves to something instead of a dead end. The owner/admin path is untouched.
- **The placing table's `Stable` column and the entry-result page's stable/owner line both come from one query change**: `listClassEntriesForDisplay` (`src/db/shows.ts`) gained a `JOIN stables` + `LEFT JOIN accounts` it wasn't doing before, reusing the same query every class listing already made rather than adding a second lookup. `showEntryResultRoute` now resolves the *entering* stable (`entry.entered_by_stable_id`), not the horse's *current* owner stable — the two agree today (no transfer path exists yet) but only one of them is actually the field that means "who entered this."
- **`/admin/accounts` delete is refuse-not-cascade, exactly as decided (§2.3)**: owns any stable (active or inactive), has granted a founding batch (`import_offers.granted_by_account_id`, a real foreign key — checked up front rather than letting the `DELETE` fail raw), is the caller's own account, or is the last admin. A `deleteAccountRow` call is still wrapped in try/catch for a foreign key this session didn't think to name (`pin_attempts`, `config_audit`) — an account with those rows has, by definition, used an admin page, which is not the "created with a typo and never used" case this button exists for, but the catch means a raw SQLite error message never reaches the operator either way.
- **The admin PIN gate (`accounts.pin_hash`, `pin_attempts`, `src/lib/pin.ts`'s `decidePinAttempt`) is built exactly to slice 0005 §7.3/§7.4's shape, spent only on `/admin` today** — when slice 0005 §7's parent-PIN grant lands, it inherits both tables and the lockout function rather than duplicating them. `decidePinAttempt` only counts *failures* toward the lockout (a correct PIN doesn't add to the tally), and `retryAfterSeconds` is computed from the oldest failure still inside the window, not the newest — the test worth reading if this is ever retuned (`test/pin.test.ts`).
- **The wall-clock exception (§9.4) is real and singular**: the lockout in `adminUnlockRoute` compares `nowUtcSeconds()` against `pin_attempts.real_ts` directly, never `world.game_day` — the one place in this codebase a security control is allowed to read the real clock, per the three reasons written at the comparison site (a paused world must not stop a lockout; the threat is real-time; no game state depends on it). **Added to `CLAUDE.md` §5.3 as the one-line pointer this rule asked for.**
- **`/admin/migrations` gets its own, separately-implemented PIN check** (`routes/migrations.ts`), not a shared call into the router's version — it bypasses `buildContext` entirely and can't read `config.values.admin_pin_grace_seconds`, so it uses a hard-coded `MIGRATIONS_PIN_GRACE_SECONDS = 1800` instead, and the whole check is wrapped in try/catch that lets the request through on any failure (a missing `pin_hash` column being the expected one, before this slice's own migration has been applied on a given database). This is the deliberate hole `CLAUDE.md`'s slice 0016 entry documents: a locked-out operator with no working migrations page has no recovery route at all, which is worse than a missed PIN check on this one page.
- **The nag banner ("Anyone holding this phone can reach these pages") is spliced into the rendered HTML at the router level (`withAdminPinNag` in `src/router.ts`) rather than threaded as a parameter through the ~15 render functions in `src/render/admin.ts`.** This is a deliberate shortcut worth flagging rather than hiding: it works because `pageShell` always emits a literal `<main>` tag and admin responses are always `text/html` or a bodyless redirect (checked via `Content-Type` before touching the body), but it means the banner's markup lives in `router.ts`, not next to the rest of `render/admin.ts`'s markup. If a future session finds this surprising while looking for the banner, that's why.
- **`accounts.pin_hash IS NULL` means the gate is off for that account — never "locked."** This is the load-bearing line for not locking the operator out on first deploy (§9.6), and it falls out for free rather than needing a special case: even if the `pin_hash` column doesn't exist yet on a given database (pre-migration), `SELECT *` simply omits the key, `account.pin_hash` reads `undefined`, and every truthiness check here (`ctx.account.pin_hash !== null`, `if (account.pin_hash)`) already treats that as "no PIN, gate off."
- **Deploy note for the operator, repeated here because it matters**: after this deploys, apply the three new migrations at `/admin/migrations`, then visit `/admin/security` and set a PIN. Until that's done, admin pages nag but still open — that's deliberate, not a bug, and is the whole point of §9.6.
- `npm test` (53 files, 415 tests — 12 new: `test/barn-filter.test.ts`, `test/world.test.ts`, `test/pin.test.ts`) and `npx tsc --noEmit` both clean. Not verified against a live `wrangler d1` this session (no CLI/account available in this sandbox, same constraint earlier sessions have noted) — the three new migrations are plain `ALTER TABLE`/`CREATE TABLE`/`UPDATE config` statements following exactly the shape of migrations already verified live in earlier sessions; still worth a real `/admin/migrations` run and a hand-check of `/admin/security` before trusting this in play, per the deploy note above.

**2026-08-03 — Two follow-ups to slice 0016, from the operator in conversation.**

- **The admin PIN now re-locks the moment you leave `/admin`, not just after the time-based grace window elapses.** `src/router.ts`'s `handleRequest` now delegates every logged-in route to a new `routeForLoggedInAccount`, then — for an admin account, on any response whose path isn't under `/admin` — appends `expireAdminUnlockCookie()`. Coming back into `/admin` seconds later asks again; moving between admin pages in one visit still doesn't re-prompt, since none of those requests hit that branch. `admin_pin_grace_seconds` is kept as a secondary bound (an idle tab left open on an admin page still eventually re-locks on its own), not removed.
- **An admin viewing someone else's horse at `/horses/:id` now sees the true health status, not names-only.** `healthRowsFor` (`src/routes/horses.ts`) gained an `isAdmin` branch that reads straight off the genotype via `conditionStatus` — bypassing `horse_knowledge`/`ownerVisibleStatus` entirely, since the truth-vs-knowledge split protects one player from another, not the operator from their own game. `healthCard` (`src/render/horses.ts`) now takes `canSeeFullHealth` (`owner || isAdmin`) instead of `owner` alone; the Test button stays owner-only, since buying a test is a purchase on a stable's own account. Nothing else on the horse page needed this fix — conformation, care, COI and (for admins) genotype were already unredacted for a non-owner admin, health was the one gap.
- **New `/admin/horses`**: a name search (registered or barn name, either matches) across every horse in the game, each result linking to its now-fully-visible `/horses/:id`. `src/db/horses.ts`'s `searchHorses` is the new query — one `JOIN stables`/`LEFT JOIN breeds`, capped at 50 results, no pagination (same "this is a small family game" reasoning `CLAUDE.md` §3 already applies everywhere else). This is the missing way in: before this, an admin had to go stable by stable to find one horse.

**2026-08-03 — Founding claim: any gender mix up to 3, not a fixed 2-mares-1-stallion split.** Asked for directly by the operator, while slice 0015 Part B (the other half of NPC stables) was being built by a separate session — this change touched only the founding-claim path, not anything Part B is working on. Candidate generation is unchanged: an open offer still shows 4 mares and 2 stallions (`offer.mare_candidates`/`offer.stallion_candidates`, still config-driven). What changed is `claimOffer` in `src/db/founding.ts` — it no longer checks `mareCount`/`stallionCount` against `offer.mare_claims`/`offer.stallion_claims` separately (the old `wrong_mare_count`/`wrong_stallion_count` errors are gone); it now only checks the total chosen against `offer.mare_claims + offer.stallion_claims` (still 3) via a single new `too_many_chosen` error, with no floor — 0, 1, 2 or 3 horses of any gender split are all valid claims. No migration: `mare_claims`/`stallion_claims` stay in the schema and in config (`founding_mare_claims`/`founding_stallion_claims`) exactly as before, just summed rather than checked individually, since renaming or restructuring them for a two-line behavior change wasn't worth it. `render/founding.ts`'s claim-page copy changed from "Choose 2 mares and 1 stallion" to "Choose up to 3 horses, any mix of mares and stallions." `npm test` (406 tests) and `npx tsc --noEmit` both clean.

**2026-08-03 — Slice 0015 (NPC stables), Part B: the tick stage, foal naming, the generalised show top-up, and `/admin/npc`.** Follows the same day's Part A entry above. NPC stables now actually breed — this is the first session in which anything about how the game plays changes because of slice 0015.

- **`src/db/npcBreeding.ts` is new** — the database layer around the Part A engine, and also where `npc_policy`/`npc_ceiling_schedule` get their first read/write functions at all (Part A shipped the tables and the engine, but nothing queried them yet). `runNpcBreedingDecisions` is the tick stage; `listNpcPolicies`, `listNpcCeilingSchedule`, `activeNpcCeilingRow`, `upsertNpcCeilingScheduleRow`, `listNpcStablesForAdmin` and `foundNpcStable` are what `/admin/npc` is built on. Wired into `src/db/tick.ts` immediately before `resolveDueCoverings`, per the slice's own §6.2 ordering.
- **No new eligibility rule was invented.** `eligibleMaresAndStallions` in that file re-checks exactly what `validateBooking` (`src/routes/horses.ts`) already checks for a player's own booking — `availabilityForHorse`, `min_breeding_age_game_days`, `mare_recovery_game_days`, `getActivePregnancyForMare`, `getBookedCoveringForMare` — minus the stable-capacity/debt checks, which are checked once per stable per cycle rather than once per horse (§2.5/§2.6). `isInBreedingSeason` is checked once per stable too, since it's a global condition, not a per-horse one. If a future session changes any of these in one place, both paths pick it up.
- **`loadPedigreeContextForMany` (`src/db/horses.ts`) is the batched generalisation of `loadPedigreeContext`** — same two-query shape (ancestor ids, then those horses' own rows), generalised from a fixed pair to any set of horse ids. `loadPedigreeContext(sireId, damId)` is now a one-line wrapper calling it with `[sireId, damId]`. **The pattern worth reusing**: computing N pairwise values that each need pedigree context should load the context once for the whole candidate set and compute every pair from the in-memory map, never once per pair — the NPC breeding stage's own comment does the arithmetic (a 6-mare, 6-stallion stable would otherwise be dozens of D1 round trips per cycle just for COI).
- **`npc_policy.last_bred_game_day` updates once per *due* cycle, unconditionally — including when the cycle books zero pairs.** `runOnePolicy`'s structure is deliberately one function with a single marker-update statement at the end (not an early return per guard), so a stable skipped for debt, at capacity, out of season, or simply out of eligible stock still has its marker advanced to the current game day. This is why `/admin/npc`'s "restore the balance" case waits for the *next* interval rather than firing on the tick right after the balance clears — confirmed by hand (see the verification note below).
- **An NPC-bred foal is named via `src/db/npc.ts`'s new `resolveUniqueNpcFoalName(env, prefix, seed)`**, called from `foalOnePregnancy` (`src/db/pregnancies.ts`) only when the owning stable's `is_npc = 1`. It reuses `generateFoundingName`'s word list and its deterministic collision-walk shape (the same one `resolveUniqueBarnName` already used), but discards `originPrefix` — that field means "founding origin" (bred by someone outside the game), which would misdescribe a horse a stable bred itself. `buildFoalInsertStatements` (`src/db/horses.ts`) gained one new optional field, `registeredName`, defaulting to `undefined`/`null` — every existing caller (a player's own foaling) is completely unaffected and keeps producing `registered_name = NULL` exactly as before.
- **`src/db/npc.ts`'s `stockShowBarn` and the new `stockNpcStable` now share one internal `mintFoundingHorses(env, { stable, breed, ..., count, band })`** rather than either hardcoding a stable or the Quarter Horse. `stockShowBarn` keeps its exact existing signature and behaviour (resolves Fair Meadow + QH, tops up to a target size) so `/admin/shows` needed no changes at all. `stockNpcStable` is deliberately **not** top-up-to-a-target shaped like its sibling — it mints an exact `count` of new horses regardless of how many the target stable already has, because "add an outcross batch" means "add N," not "reach N," and a populated Cedar Hollow with a top-up call would often mint nothing at all.
- **`listNpcStableHorses` (`src/db/npc.ts`) replaces `getShowBarnStable` in `judgeOneClass`'s top-up** (`src/db/shows.ts`) — one `JOIN` query over every `is_npc = 1` stable's alive horses, in place of one hardcoded stable. Nothing else in that function changed: the shuffle, the eligibility check, the `is_npc = 1` flag on the resulting `show_entries` row, and the documented "never top up a class no player entered" departure are all untouched.
- **`/admin/npc` is the fourth "roster + one editable schedule + a couple of mint-shaped forms" admin page**, same family as `/admin/shows`/`/admin/care`/`/admin/ageing`. One render-layer decision worth flagging: the ceiling-schedule table is **read-only display**, with editing done through a single "edit or add a row" form below it (a `<select>` of existing rows, defaulting to "add a new row") rather than one `<form>` per table row — a `<form>` cannot legally wrap a single `<tr>`, and per-row forms would have needed a non-table layout to stay valid HTML. "Found a new NPC stable" refuses a taken prefix with a plain sentence (`stables.prefix` is `UNIQUE COLLATE NOCASE`) rather than surfacing the raw constraint error, matching every other admin form's convention.
- **Verified by hand, end-to-end, against a local `wrangler dev --local`** (after `wrangler d1 migrations apply --local`, all 89 migrations clean, and a first-run `/setup` account — `.dev.vars` needs a `SESSION_SECRET` locally, which is not committed): outcross-stocked Cedar Hollow and Willow Creek Barrels with 6 horses each from `/admin/npc`; advanced the tick by hand repeatedly past every stable's `breeding_interval_game_days`; watched `last_bred_game_day`/pairs-last-cycle update on `/admin/npc` (Cedar Hollow booked 1 pair, Willow Creek booked 2 — confirming the round-robin, since it has 3 stallions and 3 mares and any pairing at all confirms the mechanism runs) with **no change to any player's events feed** (there were no player stables in this test world, but the guard is the same one `buildEventStatement` already has and is asserted directly in the new test file too); after further ticks, a foal was born and named **"Willow Creek Barrels Talon"**, visible via the new `/admin/horses` search and its own `/horses/:id` page; edited a ceiling-schedule row's numbers and confirmed the "Active now" marker followed; founded a fourth NPC stable ("Amber Fields") live from the browser, confirmed a duplicate-prefix attempt ("Cedar Hollow") was refused with a plain sentence rather than a raw SQL error; and ran a full `/admin/reset` (world scope), confirming exactly the original three NPC stables came back with fresh, unbred `npc_policy` rows and the admin-founded fourth stable did not (expected — it isn't reference data, `reset.ts`'s existing behaviour, unchanged by this session).
- `npm test` (54 files, 417 tests — 11 new in `test/npc/breeding.test.ts`: the due-this-cycle predicate, idempotency across two runs with the marker advanced in between, the capacity/debt guards excluding a stable independently, and the event-guard regression the slice document's own §9 asks for) and `npx tsc --noEmit` both clean.

**2026-08-03 — Slice 0017 (the market), Part A only: player listings, the transfer, and the ledger's two dormant columns finally getting a writer.** Built to `docs/slices/0017-market.md` §1-§10; Parts B (NPC selling), C (NPC buying) and D (stud services) are specified in §11-§13 of that document and deliberately untouched. Migrations `0090_listings.sql`, `0091_ledger_add_market_kinds.sql`, `0092_config_market.sql`.

- **`src/engines/market/` is a new engine directory**, same pure-function pattern as `showing/`, `npc/` and the rest. `appraise.ts` reuses `scoreEntry` for the quality term and `agePerformanceModifier` for the decline term rather than inventing second versions of either — a retune of a breed's ideal vector or of the age curve flows straight through to what the game thinks a horse is worth, because it is the same function and not a copy of it. **The rule at the top of that file is the one to keep**: `horses.natural_death_game_day` is never an input, in any form, because an owner watching the guide value drop could read the rolled death date off the curve. What it may read is `frailty_notice_game_day`, the announced Failing notice, which arrives as a plain `isFailing` boolean. `test/market/appraise.test.ts` asserts the parameter list itself, so adding a lifespan field breaks a test rather than quietly shipping a leak.
- **"Not tested" is a rendered row, not an absence.** The listing's health panel (`src/render/market.ts`'s `healthPanel`) renders one row per condition the game *tests for*, not one row per result the seller *holds* — a list of three clear results with two conditions silently missing reads to a nine-year-old as a clean bill of health. It reads `horse_knowledge` only, never `horse_conditions` or `horses.genotype`. This is the single detail in the slice most likely to be got subtly wrong by a later "tidy-up", and step 2 of the slice's own §15 hand-check exists for it.
- **The sale's race guard is an assignment into a `NOT NULL` column, not a `WHERE` clause — and this is a correction to the slice document.** §7.3 asks for `WHERE status = 'open'` plus a `meta.changes` check afterwards. That is not sufficient, and it was caught by running two concurrent buys against a real database rather than by reading the spec: a `WHERE` that matches nothing is not an *error*, so the batch carries on to the ledger inserts and the balance updates, and the losing second buyer is charged in full for a horse they did not get — `meta.changes` then tells you so only after the money has moved. `buildSaleStatements` instead assigns `CASE WHEN <still valid> THEN <new value> ELSE NULL END` into `listings.status` and `horses.owner_stable_id`, both `NOT NULL`: a stale sale attempts a NULL, SQLite raises, and D1 rolls the whole batch back. `sellListing` catches it and returns a sentence. **If you touch either statement, keep the trip-wire.** The same reasoning applies anywhere else a multi-statement batch's validity depends on a row still being in a particular state — matching zero rows is silent, raising is not.
- **`ledger.counterparty_stable_id` and `same_account` have a writer at last**, and slice 0009's single-writer rule survived it intact: `buildLedgerStatements` gained two optional fields and nothing else, it is still the only function in the codebase that changes `stables.balance`, and the sale's three ledger rows go into the same `env.DB.batch()` as the horse's ownership change. Three kinds (`sale`, `purchase`, `commission`) rather than one net movement, because the ledger is a thing a child reads. The Money page now shows a Kind column and the counterparty stable's name, resolved in `getLedgerForStable`'s own query rather than a lookup per row.
- **A deliberate departure from schema §7.1: `listings.disclosed_knowledge` is not built.** Disclosure is compulsory (§2.3), so there is no per-listing choice for the column to record, and a column nothing writes is a column a future session has to work out the meaning of. Recorded in the schema doc against the sketch.
- **Which stable buys, and what "yours" means on `/market`.** Every existing purchase in this codebase is reached through a horse, which names its own stable; `/market` is a global page and an account may hold several stables, so the buy form carries an explicit `stable_id` (re-checked server-side, collapsing to a hidden field for a one-stable account). The knock-on: §6.1's "a row for a horse the viewing stable owns is marked yours" is implemented at *account* level, because there is no single viewing stable in scope. Flagged to the operator on delivery rather than decided silently.
- **`test/market/sale-d1.test.ts` is the first test in this repo that touches a database**, and it is worth knowing about before you write the next one. It applies the real migration files to an in-memory SQLite database through **node:sqlite — built into Node, not a dependency** — behind a ~30-line shim that looks enough like D1 (prepare/bind/run/all/first/batch-as-one-transaction) to run `src/db/listings.ts`'s real functions unmodified. It is what caught the race bug above on its first run. Two caveats, both deliberate: it is **not** D1 (same SQLite engine, so constraints/indexes/transactions/`changes` behave alike; different client, different error wording, no real concurrency), and node:sqlite is experimental and needs Node 22+, so the suite **skips rather than fails** where it is unavailable. It is also the one file excluded from `tsconfig.json` — it needs `@types/node`, whose globals conflict with `@cloudflare/workers-types`, and weakening the type checking on all of `src/` to type-check one test file is a bad trade. Reach for this pattern for the next piece of gnarly multi-statement SQL; do not reach for it as a general testing strategy.
- **`src/db/reset.ts`**: `listings` goes in `HORSE_TABLES`, first, ahead of `horses` and `stables` — a horses-only reset that left it behind would leave every listing pointing at a deleted horse and break `/market` on the first page view. Added to `test/reset.test.ts`'s `REFERENCES` map, as that file's own instructions require.
- Verified: `npm test` (55 files, 457 tests — 40 new across `test/market/`) and `npx tsc --noEmit` both clean, and **all 92 migrations applied cleanly to a real SQLite database with `PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON`**, including the ledger table-rebuild and the config `json_set`. **Not** verified in a browser against a live `wrangler dev` — no Cloudflare account or wrangler login in this sandbox, the same constraint several earlier sessions have noted. The slice's §15 hand-verification list is the thing to walk through before the children trade for real; step 2 (the "not tested" panel) and step 8 (the `same_account` flag reading 1 for a two-stable sale under one account) are the two that most deserve a real look.

**2026-08-03 — Slice 0017 (the market), Part B: NPC stables sell.** Built to `docs/slices/0017-market.md` §11; Parts C (NPC buying) and D (stud services) are still specified only, in §12-§13. Migrations `0093_npc_policy_market_columns.sql`, `0094_config_npc_market.sql`.

- **`src/db/npcMarket.ts` is new** — `runNpcMarketListings`, called from `src/db/tick.ts` immediately after `runNpcBreedingDecisions`, both NPC-stable tick-cycle decisions living next to each other. It writes into the same `listings` table Part A built, through the same `createListing`/`appraiseHorseForStable` functions a player's own listing form uses — there is no second sale path, an NPC listing is just a `listings` row with an NPC `seller_stable_id`, exactly as §11 asked.
- **The trigger is capacity-only, a deliberate simplification of §11's two triggers** ("at or near capacity, **or** when a horse falls below what the policy wants to keep"), agreed with the operator before building rather than defaulted silently. When an NPC stable's free stalls drop below `npc_market_capacity_buffer` (live config), it lists its own lowest-scoring horses — scored against its personality's own target, reusing `expressedFor`/`resolveSelectionTarget` (now exported from `npcBreeding.ts` for this) rather than a second "how good is this horse" formula — until the buffer is restored or `npc_market_max_listings_per_tick` is hit. There is no separate always-on quality floor. **Note for whoever picks this up**: listing a horse does not free a stall (a listing is an advert, not a sale — §2.8), so a stable that stays near capacity keeps listing fresh candidates on every later tick until it either sells enough to make room or runs out of eligible unlisted stock. That is intended, not a bug — the alternative (list once and stop) would leave real surplus stuck.
- **No interval marker was needed**, unlike `runNpcBreedingDecisions`'s `last_bred_game_day`: the stage is naturally idempotent, since a horse already open-listed is excluded from the candidate pool and `idx_listings_one_open_per_horse` would refuse a double listing anyway. It simply runs every tick and finds nothing to do once a stable is back under its buffer.
- **The per-personality asking-price multiplier and spread live on `npc_policy`** (`market_price_multiplier`, `market_price_spread` — new columns, migration `0093`), not in global config — they're a property of the personality, the same way `selection_noise_sd`/`retention_bias` already are, not a game-wide tunable. Migration `0093` differentiates the three existing personalities (specialist 1.25x/±8%, discipline barn 1.10x/±10%, volume breeder 0.85x/±15%); `reset.ts`'s three hand-written `npc_policy` inserts were updated to match, since a full reset doesn't replay old migrations. `foundNpcStable` and `/admin/npc`'s "Found a new NPC stable" form both gained the two fields — there was no way to set them for a stable founded after migration `0093` otherwise.
- **§11's own recommendation was taken as-is**: an NPC stable pays for a full genotype panel out of its own balance before listing an untested horse, through the exact same `buildKnowledgePurchaseStatements` path the player test-purchase route uses (so a tested-clear NPC horse is tested the same way a player's is), charged as a `vet`-kind ledger row. If the stable can't afford the panel it lists untested rather than skipping the cycle — a tight NPC economy (§12's own risk) shouldn't also freeze the exit for its surplus stock. A running local `balance` variable tracks spend across several horses listed in one cycle, since each purchase is its own `env.DB.batch()` (needed so `appraiseHorseForStable` reads the freshly-written knowledge before the price is set) and `stables.balance` isn't re-read between horses.
- **The listing price**: `appraise().value × market_price_multiplier × spreadFactor`, rounded to the nearest 10, floored at 10, capped at `market_max_price`. `spreadFactor` is seeded per horse-per-day (`deriveSeed(stableId, npc_listing_price_<horseId>_<gameDay>)`), not per stable, so two horses listed by the same stable on the same day don't ask the identical fraction over guide value (CLAUDE.md §5.2 — no `Math.random()`, ever).
- **`test/npc/market-d1.test.ts` is the second file to use the `node:sqlite` harness** `test/market/sale-d1.test.ts` introduced (see that slice's own build-log entry) — copied rather than shared, since the existing file's helpers aren't exported anywhere yet; if a third file wants this pattern, that's the point to extract a shared harness module. Also added to `tsconfig.json`'s exclude list for the same reason `sale-d1.test.ts` is there (Node types conflict with `@cloudflare/workers-types`). One thing worth knowing if you extend these tests: `config-cache.ts`'s `getConfig` caches in module scope for 60 real seconds with no per-env key, which is fine in production (one env) but wrong across tests that each build a fresh in-memory database — the test file reads the `config` row directly instead of calling `getConfig`, and any new D1-backed test should do the same rather than risk a stale config leaking between `it()` blocks.
- Verified: `npm test` (56 files, 462 tests — 5 new in `test/npc/market-d1.test.ts`: no listings with stalls to spare, a capacity-triggered listing, the per-tick cap enforced independently of the buffer, no horse ever listed twice across two ticks, and the minimum-age gate) and `npx tsc --noEmit` both clean. **Not** verified against a live `wrangler dev` or real D1 — no Cloudflare account/login in this sandbox, the same constraint every session in this log has noted. Before trusting this in play: apply the two new migrations at `/admin/migrations`, watch `/admin/npc`'s balance column on Cedar Hollow after a few ticks past its breeding interval with its stock near 40, and confirm a listing actually appears on `/market` with a plausible price and (once tested) a real health panel rather than "not tested" on everything.

**2026-08-03 — Amendment 0017a, in full: colour testing, the consignment dealer (Part E), and breeds in play.** Built to `docs/slices/0017a-amendment-colour-testing-and-the-consignment-dealer.md`. Migrations `0095_config_market_colour.sql` through `0098_config_consignment.sql`.

- **`horse_knowledge.subject_code` now carries two families, distinguished by a `locus:` prefix** — `locus:CR`, `locus:E`, etc, alongside the disease codes (`HYPP`, `PSSM1`, …) that were already there. **Every reader of that table that assumes a `kind = 'genotype'` row is a disease result had to be found and guarded** — `db/health.ts`'s `knowledgeMap` now filters the prefix out explicitly (it wasn't a live bug — nothing that reads that map ever looks up a `locus:`-shaped key — but it was one `for (const [code] of map)` away from becoming one, so it's guarded now rather than left as a footgun). `untestedConditions` and `getKnownGenotypeSubjectsForHorses` were checked too and didn't need changes (bare condition codes never collide with `locus:`-prefixed ones). **If you add a new table scan over `horse_knowledge` in the future, check for this prefix before assuming every row is a disease result.**
- **`src/engines/genetics/inference.ts` (`inferFromPhenotype`) is the one place "what does looking at this horse tell you, before any test" is computed** — it takes a `Phenotype` and returns, per locus, the set of allele pairs still possible. The subtle part is the epistasis and masking rules: a chestnut horse's Agouti is completely unknown (E masks it), a bay/black horse's own zygosity is unknown but its Agouti-presence is not, and a black horse's cream status is ambiguous between clear and one copy — smoky black is genuinely indistinguishable from plain black by eye. Grey masks the base entirely **except at the foal-grey stage**, where `expression.ts`'s own `visibleColourFor` already returns the base colour, not the grey — get this wrong and a grey foal's colour card either over- or under-claims what looking tells you.
- **The smoky-black masking is a render-layer concern, not an engine one, and it is not viewer-scoped by default.** `src/render/colour.ts`'s `displayColourName(name, creamTested)` is the one mapping function; every call site that surfaces `visibleColour`/`bornColour` to a template passes through it. **The default is `creamTested = false` — mask unless the caller has actually checked this specific viewing stable's own `horse_knowledge` for `locus:CR`.** That default is deliberately the safe direction: a stranger at the rail cannot tell smoky black from plain black either, so "untested" is the *correct* public reading, not merely a conservative fallback. Wired with a real per-stable check on the barn list, the breed (breeding) page, the horse's own page and the image picker (all owner-viewed); left at the safe default on `/world` and the founding-candidate screens (no owning stable exists yet to check).
- **`appraise()` gained a `colourFactor` term (§4.7 of the amendment) — `visibleColourFactor × carriedAlleleFactor`.** The split is the whole design: the visible half needs no knowledge check at all (a cremello is visibly a cremello — the same "a stranger at a show can see it" rule slice 0016 already established for visible health signs), the carried half only counts a locus where `inferFromPhenotype`'s own *untested* set already had more than one member — a palomino's cream dose is visible on its chestnut base and earns nothing extra for testing it, but a black horse's cream test (resolving the smoky-black ambiguity) or a bay's Agouti-zygosity test both do. That count is computed by a new pure function, `hiddenColourAlleleCount(phenotype, testedLocusCodes)` in `inference.ts` — **this is the one function that decides whether a test "counts" for the market premium, and it is the thing to check first if colour pricing ever looks wrong.** `AppraiseParams` gained two required fields (`visibleColour`, `knownHiddenColourAlleleCount`) computed by the caller (`listings.ts`'s `appraiseHorseForStable`) from the expressed phenotype and the seller's own `locus:` knowledge rows — `appraise()` itself still never reads `horses.genotype`, in any form, which `test/market/appraise.test.ts`'s structural key-list test now also covers for these two fields.
- **The consignment dealer (`src/db/consignment.ts`) is a new NPC stable, `Consignment Yard` (migration `0097`), with deliberately no `npc_policy` row** — that absence is what keeps `runNpcBreedingDecisions`/`runNpcMarketListings` from ever touching it. **Its `stables.balance` is a documented fiction**: it performs and pays for genotype tests on the horses it mints (real `horse_knowledge` rows, real `cost_paid`), but writes no ledger row and moves no real money for any of it — unlike Part B's NPC stables, whose balances are real and matter for a later Part C. If a future session is tempted to unify the two "NPC stable that lists horses" code paths, read `runOneMarketPolicy` in `npcMarket.ts` (real ledger writes) against `mintConsignmentBatch` in `consignment.ts` (none) first — they look similar and are not.
- **The injection queue (`consignment_injections`, migration `0096`) is a consumed-once instruction table, not a config row.** `queueInjection`/`cancelInjection`/`listQueuedInjections`/`listInjectionHistory` are its whole API; `src/engines/founding/injection.ts`'s `applyConsignmentInjection` is the pure function that overwrites one locus's pair on an already-generated candidate genotype (never resampled, never re-rolled) — `hom` writes two copies, `het` writes one copy of the injected allele plus that locus's *other* allele (every locus in `LOCI` is biallelic), never a copy drawn from whatever the candidate happened to roll, so the result is fully deterministic from the injection alone. `applies_to = 'one'` is removed from the batch's working list the instant it matches a horse; `'all'` keeps matching every eligible horse for the rest of that batch, but the row is still marked `applied` (not left `queued`) once the batch finishes either way — a queued row is consumed by the next batch that can use it, never left to silently reapply forever.
- **`runConsignments`'s idempotency has no marker column**, the same shape `expireListings` already uses: it derives its own next-due day from `MAX(listings.listed_game_day) WHERE seller_stable_id = <dealer>`, so a re-fired tick recomputes the same due day and finds it already past. A dealer with no prior batch is due *immediately* (not one full cadence after the migration deploys) so the operator sees it working on the very next tick rather than waiting 90 game days. Wired into `src/db/tick.ts` immediately before `expireListings`, inside the same `paused === 0` branch as everything else.
- **`getBreedsInPlay()` (`src/db/breeds.ts`) is the one helper every breed-admission call site now reads instead of `getBreeds()`** — the founding-offer breed dropdown and its POST-time validation (`routes/founding.ts`), the admin "create a horse" form and the NPC-policy "found a new stable" dropdown (`routes/admin.ts`), the consignment dealer's own eligible-breed filter, and `createShowIfMissing`'s new-class creation (`db/shows.ts`, intersected with `ideal_vector IS NOT NULL` as before). **`getBreeds()` (all breeds, `enabled` or not) is still correct and still used** everywhere that resolves a `breed_id` to a name, or that needs literally every breed for an admin screen that manages the flag itself (`/admin/breeds`) or a target-breed display that must still resolve a since-disabled breed's name. Read `enabled` as "admits new arrivals," never as "hide this breed" — a foal from two existing horses of a disabled breed is untouched, and `/admin/breeds` says so in a paragraph next to the toggle. `setBreedEnabled` (same file) carries the two guards (refuse the last enabled breed; refuse enabling one whose `founding_allele_pool` is missing a locus, via `parseAllelePool`'s own throw) and writes a `config_audit` row under a reused key shape (`breed:<code>:enabled`) rather than a second history table.
- **`test/founding/consignment-d1.test.ts` is the third file using the `node:sqlite` real-database harness** the market slice introduced (see that entry, and `test/market/sale-d1.test.ts`'s own header) — copied again rather than shared (still nobody has extracted a common module; three copies is probably the point to do that, if a fourth ever needs it). Added to `tsconfig.json`'s exclude list for the same Node-types-vs-Workers-types reason the first two are there. It caught real, working behaviour on first run: idempotency across two same-day calls, cadence-gated re-minting, an injected allele landing in the actual stored genotype and getting pre-tested, the sweep-then-`expireListings` handoff removing an unclaimed horse, and — the one that would have been a real money bug — a *sold* dealer horse surviving arbitrarily far past its original listing's expiry.
- **Scope deliberately left for a follow-up session**: the listing page's own colour rows (§4.5 point 3 of the amendment — laying `locus:` knowledge out next to condition rows on `/market/:id`) were not built; a listing already carries every knowledge row the seller holds (0017 §2.3's existing mechanism copies it), so the data is there, only the rendering isn't. The breeding preview (§4.5 point 1) and the horse's own "what this horse can pass on" card (§4.5 point 2) **were** built, reusing `foalColourPossibilities` and `inferFromPhenotype` respectively.
- Verified: `npm test` (63 files, 493 tests — 5 in `test/genetics/inference.test.ts`, 5 in `test/genetics/foal-colours.test.ts`, 5 in `test/founding/injection.test.ts`, 5 real-database tests in `test/founding/consignment-d1.test.ts`, plus new cases in `test/market/appraise.test.ts`) and `npx tsc --noEmit` both clean, including all 98 migrations applied cleanly to a real SQLite database. **Not** verified against a live `wrangler dev` or real D1 — no Cloudflare account/login in this sandbox, the same standing constraint every session in this log has noted. Before trusting this in play: apply the four new migrations at `/admin/migrations`, queue a colour allele from `/admin/consignment` and watch it show up pre-tested on the next batch, and buy a colour test on a real horse to confirm the guide-value premium moves the way §4.7's worked examples describe.

**2026-08-03 — Bug fix: the `/shows` "All" and "Conformation" tabs were showing no classes at all, plus conformation/discipline criteria surfaced in admin.** Reported by the operator from two screenshots — "All" said "No open classes match this filter right now" for a show that plainly had an open Barrel Racing class, and they suspected conformation classes had stopped running entirely.

- **The actual bug, in `routes/shows.ts`'s `resolveShowsFilter`**: `Number(params.get('breed'))` was used to parse the optional `breed` query param, but `Number(null)` (param absent, the normal case for the "All" and "Conformation" tabs) and `Number('')` (the breed picker's own "All breeds" option) both evaluate to `0` in JS, not `NaN`. `Number.isInteger(0)` is true, so a page load with no breed filter at all was silently resolving `breedId = 0` instead of `null`. `classMatchesShowsFilter` (`db/shows.ts`) then excluded every class from the result — a real breed's `breed_id` is never `0`, and a discipline class's `breed_id` is `NULL`, which also fails `!== 0`. **Conformation classes were never broken and the tick was creating them correctly the whole time** (confirmed by reading `createShowIfMissing`) — this was purely a display filter defaulting to "show nothing" instead of "show everything." `routes/market.ts`'s equivalent breed-param parsing already guarded against this (`rawBreed > 0`); `shows.ts`'s did not.
- Fixed by extracting the parsing into a small pure function, `parseBreedIdParam(raw: string | null): number | null`, exported from `routes/shows.ts` and unit-tested directly (`test/showing/showsFilter.test.ts`) rather than only through a route — the same "pure function, thin route" instinct CLAUDE.md §5.1 asks of engines, applied here to a plain parsing rule so a future session can change this logic without spinning up a request. The test file also exercises `classMatchesShowsFilter` itself with the corrected `null` to lock in the regression.
- **Conformation and discipline judging criteria are now visible on `/admin/shows`**, asked for directly: two new cards, each holding one `<details class="section-collapse">` dropdown per breed (conformation) or per discipline, reusing the existing collapsible style `public/style.css` already defines. Each conformation dropdown lists that breed's ideal vector — trait name, target, weight — parsed via `parseIdealVector` (`engines/showing/score.ts`); each discipline dropdown lists its ability weights — trait name, weight only, no target, since an ability trait has none — parsed via `parseAbilityWeights` (`engines/showing/abilityScore.ts`). Both iterate `CONFORMATION_TRAITS`/`ABILITY_TRAITS` (`engines/conformation/traits.ts`) rather than `Object.keys()`, the same ordering discipline `scoreEntry`/`scoreAbilityEntry` themselves follow, so this can never disagree with what judging actually does. A breed with no ideal vector at all is left out (mirrors `createShowIfMissing`'s own "no ideal vector, no class" rule); a disabled breed or discipline still appears, labelled `(not in play)` / `(disabled)`, since its standing classes keep running to completion per `getBreedsInPlay`'s own contract.
- Verified: `npm test` (64 files, 503 tests — 8 new in `test/showing/showsFilter.test.ts`) and `npx tsc --noEmit` both clean. Not verified against a live `wrangler dev` — no Cloudflare account/login in this sandbox, the standing constraint this log has noted every session. No migration involved (no schema change) — this deploys as a plain code push.

**2026-08-03 — `docs/analysis/` added, and slice 0018 specified: why selective breeding currently stops working after about two real years.** No game code changed; this entry records a new directory, a new tool, and a measurement a future session should not have to redo.

- **`docs/analysis/` is a new directory `CLAUDE.md` §4's layout does not list.** It holds analysis tools that reason *about* the game without being part of it. The first inhabitant is `population-sim.mjs`, a standalone Node script (`node docs/analysis/population-sim.mjs`, no dependencies, no build step) that runs the live conformation and showing formulas over a simulated closed population. **It is deliberately not in `test/`** — `vitest run` only collects `*.test.ts`, so a `.mjs` there would be dead weight that looks like a test; and deliberately not in `src/`, where it could break a deploy the operator cannot fix. Nothing imports it and nothing depends on it.
- **It keeps its own copy of the game's constants and its own PRNG, on purpose, and that copy can drift.** The header names the source migration or engine beside every constant. `CLAUDE.md` §5.2's "no `Math.random()`, ever" is about the *game* being reproducible from stored seeds; this file is seeded too, but keeps no dependency on `src/lib/rng` so that it cannot be broken by a change to it. The trade is real: **if you retune `conformation_noise_sd`, `inbreeding_depression_factor`, `show_ideal_falloff`, `show_noise_sd`, or the QH ideal vector, update the CONSTANTS block or delete the file.** A drifted simulation is worse than none.
- **What it measured, at migration 0098.** Five stables, six mares and two stallions each, truncation selection on show score, same-stable breeding as `routes/horses.ts:415` enforces today: mean show score climbs 65.7 → ~84 by generation 15 and then **stays there through generation 50**, with COI settling around 60%. On the real clock (30 game days per real day, ~4 game years per generation) that plateau lands about **two real years** into play.
- **The three findings worth carrying forward even if slice 0018 is never built as written:**
  1. **Reliability is a packaging problem, not a merit problem.** Two parents whose potential sits exactly on the QH ideal produce foals averaging 81.6 with 10% reaching a score of 90 when their alleles are packaged heterozygously — and 89.6 with 50% reaching 90 when packaged homozygously. Same merit, five times the hit rate. Nothing on any screen shows a player zygosity, and the inbreeding penalty punishes the only route to it.
  2. **`realization = 1 − COI × factor` is a tax selection routes around, not a brake.** Because it shrinks expression toward the anchor of 50, players answer it by selecting more extreme genotypes — until they run out. At COI 60% a correct QH shoulder (target 70) requires 20 alleles of 20, the maximum in the game. That ceiling, not selection pressure, is what stops the curve.
  3. **Stored COI understates true homozygosity in a long-closed pool**, because `PEDIGREE_DEPTH` = 6 treats deeper ancestors as unrelated founders. This is the right trade for the reasons `loci.ts` gives, but it means a population can be far more fixed than its COI column admits, and finding (1) is invisible on every screen we have.
- **Selection accuracy is not the bottleneck, and a future session should not start there.** Measured: r(own show score, true genetic merit) = 0.74; a twenty-foal progeny test reaches only 0.80. An estimated-breeding-value system is the obvious first idea and buys very little. Recorded so it is rediscovered as *rejected* rather than as new.
- **`docs/slices/0018-genetic-progress-and-inbreeding.md` is filed as a spec with seven open questions in its §10, and `CLAUDE.md` §10 now carries a "specified, not built" row pointing at it.** The largest of those questions — how hard the replacement fitness penalties should bite, and whether a foal lost to inbreeding is acceptable for these players — are `CLAUDE.md` §2 "stop and ask" material. **Do not build Part A's conformation change on its own**: dropping `inbreeding_depression_factor` without the fitness penalties that replace it makes inbreeding strictly free, which is worse than today. Note also that this factor is read fresh on every page view, so changing it re-scores every inbred horse in the game the moment it deploys.
- Verified: `node docs/analysis/population-sim.mjs` runs clean and reproduces the tables quoted in slice 0018 §1. **`npm test` and `npx tsc --noEmit` were not run** — `node_modules` was not installed in this session's sandbox. That is safe here and only here: no game code, schema or migration was touched, and `tsconfig.json`'s `include` is `["src", "test"]`, so the one added file (`docs/analysis/population-sim.mjs`) is outside both the type-checker's and vitest's view. Nothing in this change deploys. A session that touches game code must not borrow this reasoning.

**2026-08-03 — `docs/analysis/stable-timeline.mjs` added, and slice 0019 specified: how long a child actually waits before a breed feels won.** No game code changed. Companion entry to the slice-0018 one above; read that one first for why `docs/analysis/` exists and how its copied constants can drift.

- **The second analysis tool answers a different question from the first.** `population-sim.mjs` asks "does the population improve at all?" (it plateaus). `stable-timeline.mjs` asks "how long until one child has six good horses?", simulating a single stable at tick resolution — real oestrous cycles, conception rolls, gestation, ageing and death, the four QH disease loci at their seeded frequencies, GBED foals lost, affected horses excluded from breeding, carrier × carrier vetoed, and capacity culling on what the player can *see* rather than on the truth. It carries `missRate` and `colourDistraction` knobs standing in for a real child: heats missed because nobody logged in, and pairings chosen for a pretty foal.
- **Measured, for a casual child (45% of heats missed, 35% of pairings colour-driven), at gestation 60:** six horses whose conformation genes score ≥90 takes a median of **1.2 real years**, and **a third of children never get there** — 19 lines in 200 died out entirely, 48 more were alive but stuck at COI ~45%. The discipline line is worse: six horses that dominate the NPC barrel field takes **1.8 years**.
- **The cause is that founding stock is a coin flip.** Every polygenic allele is drawn at `quality_bands.mid` = 0.50, so on the Quarter Horse's ideal (11/14/7/10 of 20) **the shoulder is four alleles short on every founding horse in the game**, and most of a child's first year goes into closing a gap that is the same gap for every child, every breed, every time. The alleles are already in the pool — it is a frequency shift, not a search. **Speeding the clock up does not help**: the operator's gestation change from 340 to 60 game days made everything ~30% faster and moved the failure rate by one run in two hundred.
- **Two findings a future session should not have to re-derive:**
  1. **Conformation and ability are on different scales, and must not share a success bar.** Conformation targets are *intermediate*, so a mid-band founder already scores ~78 of 100. Ability traits are `higher_better` with anchor 0 and no target at all (`TRAIT_DIRECTION`, and `scoreAbilityEntry` is a plain weighted mean), so a mid-band founder scores ~50 of 99. Reaching "90% of perfect" in a discipline was **0 of 200 runs**. That is fine and not a bug — the NPC show barn is stocked at the *same* mid band, so ~50 is the field, and discipline success means winning rather than approaching perfection.
  2. **`jump_scope` has weight 0.0 in Barrel Racing, the only seeded discipline**, and `stamina` has 0.2. Anything that hands a horse a "special" ability trait must draw only from traits some *enabled* discipline actually weights, or it will hand children horses whose one special quality cannot be scored anywhere. Today that pool is agility, speed and trainability.
- **`docs/slices/0019-founding-specialists.md` is filed as a spec**, and `CLAUDE.md` §10 carries a "specified, not built" row. It gives every founding horse one conformation trait near its breed's target and one ability trait high-with-headroom, and doubles the consignment rate; measured together that is ~200 days and a 3% failure rate on both lines. **Specialists and consignment flow do different jobs and neither works alone** — specialists fix speed, the outcross flow fixes the failure rate.
- **Two traps recorded in that slice's §7, worth repeating here because both are silent.** (1) **Do not make the specialist trait homozygous.** It is the obvious way to make a trait breed true, and slice 0018 §1.1 measures homozygosity as *the* long-term reward — handing it out in the founding grant spends it on the first five minutes. (2) **Do not skip the existing `pool_polygenic` draws for the specialist trait.** That loop draws 20 alleles per trait off one stream; skipping any of them shifts every trait after it and the same stored seed stops producing the same horse. Let the existing loop run untouched and *overwrite* the specialist trait afterwards from a separate stream with a fresh label — the same generate-then-overwrite pattern `applyConsignmentInjection` already uses.
- **The blocker on the operator's "eight breeds" goal is data, not code:** the conformation half of slice 0019 does nothing for a breed with no `ideal_vector`, and **seven of the eight breeds have none seeded**. `docs/breed-ideal-vectors.md` has all eight drafted; turning it into a migration is the prerequisite.
- Verified: `node docs/analysis/stable-timeline.mjs` runs clean and reproduces the tables in slice 0019 §1-§2. **`npm test` and `npx tsc --noEmit` were not run** — `node_modules` is not installed in this sandbox. Safe here for the same narrow reason as the previous entry and no other: no game code, schema or migration was touched, and `tsconfig.json`'s `include` is `["src", "test"]`, so the added file is outside both the type-checker's and vitest's view. Nothing in this change deploys.

**2026-08-03 — Slice 0017 Part C: NPC stables buy.** Built to `docs/slices/0017-market.md` §12, both routes the operator asked for. Migrations `0099_buy_offers.sql`, `0100_config_npc_buying.sql`.

- **`src/db/buyOffers.ts` is the new file** for the standing offers board — `buy_offers` table CRUD, `evaluateOfferForHorse` (does a horse fit an offer, and what does it score), and `sellIntoOffer`. **`src/db/npcBuying.ts` is a second new file** for the two tick stages, `refreshNpcBuyOffers` and `runNpcMarketPurchases` — kept separate from `buyOffers.ts` on purpose: the first file is what a player-facing route reaches into (evaluating and selling), the second is what only the tick calls (an NPC stable's own cycle decisions). The same split `npcMarket.ts` (Part B, selling) already models.
- **Selling into an offer is not a second sale path.** `sellIntoOffer` creates an ordinary `listings` row at the offer's own price via the existing `createListing`, then calls `sellListing` on it immediately — the exact commission, knowledge-copy, covering/entry-reassignment and ledger-writing batch a fixed-price sale already runs. The only new failure mode is `'already_listed'`, when the horse already has an open listing of its own (`idx_listings_one_open_per_horse` refuses a second one) — the honest answer is withdraw it first, not a confusing error at the sell step.
- **`buy_offers` holds at most one active row per NPC stable** (`idx_buy_offers_one_active_per_stable`, a partial unique index, the same shape `idx_listings_one_open_per_horse` already established). `refreshNpcBuyOffers` runs every tick and **updates that row in place** rather than closing and reopening one each cycle — a player mid-way through looking at an offer should not find its link dead a moment later because nothing about it actually changed. `criteria` is JSON (`{v, breedId, minAgeGameDays, maxAgeGameDays, minQuality}`); geldings are excluded from every offer in code, not in that JSON, since the entire reason NPC buying exists is outcross breeding stock.
- **Both buying routes reuse `qualityFor`**, exported from `npcMarket.ts` for exactly this reason — the same "how good is this horse against my own target" question Part B's listing-selection already asks, asked again from the buying side rather than re-derived. Neither route invents a second scoring formula, matching the rule slice 0015 §2.2 set for NPC selection in the first place.
- **Both routes exclude every NPC seller**, via `seller_account_id IS NOT NULL` on the candidate listing (an NPC stable's own `account_id` is always null) — this is what stops an NPC from ever buying its own stock back, or another NPC's, in one filter rather than a special case. Both also exclude geldings, for the reason above.
- **The buying-side trigger is the mirror of Part B's selling-side one**: `npc_market_capacity_buffer` says "list surplus once free stalls drop below this many"; the new `npc_buying_capacity_buffer` says "only buy once free stalls are at least this many" — a stable that is nearly full has no reason to buy more, whichever route is asking. `npc_buying_budget_fraction` caps what either route will commit to a single horse, as a fraction of the stable's *current* balance (read fresh, never cached) — the standing offer's `max_price` and a tick purchase's affordability check both apply it the same way.
- **`runNpcMarketPurchases` needs no interval marker.** A horse already sold this tick cannot be sold again (the sale batch's own trip-wire, `listings.ts`'s `buildSaleStatements`), so a re-fired tick simply re-evaluates a smaller open market. Same reasoning `refreshNpcBuyOffers` gets for free from recomputing and upserting the identical numbers.
- **`/admin/npc` gained two columns** — what each NPC stable has spent buying and earned selling since the current game year began, read live from the ledger's `purchase`/`sale` kinds (`getSeasonTradeSummary`, new in `ledger.ts`) rather than a running counter. This is the slice document's own named mitigation for the risk it flags: NPC balances are real and never topped up automatically, so a stable can quietly go broke and stop buying on both routes at once — this table is how an operator sees that coming. `listNpcStablesForAdmin` now takes `gameDay`/`gameDaysPerYear` to bound the season window; its one caller (`adminNpcRoute`) was updated to pass them.
- **`buy_offers` sits in `reset.ts`'s `WORLD_ONLY_TABLES`**, right beside `npc_policy` for the same reason: a real foreign key into `stables` and no dependency on `horses` at all, so a horses-only reset leaves it exactly as it leaves every other stable-scoped row.
- **`ActionKind` gains `'sell_to_offer'`** (cost 1) — selling into a standing offer is a decisive commitment with no browsing period, the same reasoning `buy_horse` already costs the buyer, mirrored onto the seller here since there is no buyer turn to spend in this direction.
