- **Test harness note**: `test/npc/buying-d1.test.ts` and `test/market/sell-into-offer-d1.test.ts` are the fourth and fifth files using the `node:sqlite` real-database harness the market slice introduced (`test/market/sale-d1.test.ts`'s own header explains why) — copied again rather than shared, same as every prior file in this pattern. Both added to `tsconfig.json`'s exclude list for the same Node-types-vs-Workers-types reason the others are there. One real bug caught while writing them: a test helper that inserted NPC stock via a bare `INSERT ... (sex, ...)` with no `id` column relied on SQLite's autoincrement landing on a specific row id, which broke the instant another horse was inserted first in the same test — fixed by giving the helper an explicit `id` column, a trap worth remembering for the next session that copies this pattern.
- Verified: `npm test` (64 files, 519 tests — 16 new) and `npx tsc --noEmit` both clean, including both new migrations applying cleanly to a real SQLite database alongside all prior ones. **Not** verified against a live `wrangler dev` or real D1 — no Cloudflare account/login in this sandbox, the same standing constraint every session in this log has noted. Before trusting this in play: apply the two new migrations at `/admin/migrations`, watch an NPC stable's offer appear on `/market`'s Offers tab once it has room and a balance, sell a test horse into one, and check `/admin/npc`'s new columns move the way this entry describes.

**2026-08-04 — Slice 0017 Part D: stud services, the last unbuilt piece of the market.** Built to `docs/slices/0017-market.md` §13, including the NPC half (§13.4's third question). Migrations `0103_stud_listings.sql`, `0104_stud_bookings.sql`, `0105_ledger_add_stud_kinds.sql`, `0106_config_stud.sql`.

- **Three open questions named in §13.4 as "what that session must decide, and should not default" were put to the operator before any code was written, per CLAUDE.md §2.** Decided: **no live-foal guarantee** (a stud fee pays for the covering, not the outcome — same as an ordinary same-stable booking today, where a missed conception is not refunded); **the market's commission applies to a stud fee exactly as it applies to a sale**; and **build NPC stallions at stud in this same session** rather than deferring them.
- **`src/db/stud.ts` is the new file** for the player-facing half — `stud_listings`/`stud_bookings` CRUD, `validateStudBooking` (re-deriving every check `validateBooking` in `src/routes/horses.ts` already applies to a same-stable pairing, since that function assumes both horses share one `StableRow` and can't be reused as-is), and `bookStud` (one `env.DB.batch()` for the covering, the booking row, three ledger rows and an event). **`src/db/npcStud.ts` is a second new file** for the NPC half's tick stage, `runNpcStudListings` — kept separate for the same reason `npcMarket.ts`/`buyOffers.ts` are split: one file is what a player-facing route reaches into, the other is only the tick's own cycle decision.
- **This is the first cross-stable breeding in the game** (§13.2's own framing) — the covering path previously assumed both horses lived in one stable. `bookStud` reuses `coverings` unchanged: the covering's own `stable_id` is the **mare's** stable (§13.3's decided rule, "the mare's owner" gets the foal, its prefix, and the conceived/missed event), which already falls out of `resolveOneCovering`/`foalDuePregnancies` reading `dam.owner_stable_id` — no code needed to change for that half of §13.3 to hold.
- **`bookCovering` (src/db/coverings.ts) was split into a statement-builder (`buildBookCoveringStatement`) plus a thin wrapper**, the same `buildX` + caller pattern `buildLedgerStatements`/`buildEventStatement`/`buildSaleStatements` already use — needed so the covering insert can land inside the *same* batch as the stud fee's ledger rows, rather than as a separate awaited call with a gap in the middle where a crash could charge a fee with no covering created.
- **A deliberate, named departure from the sale path's race-safety posture.** `sellListing`'s `buildSaleStatements` uses a CASE-into-NOT-NULL trip-wire to stop a losing second buyer being charged for nothing — a real bug, caught by testing (see that file's own comment). `bookStud` does **not** use the same trick: its one scarce resource, `season_cap`, is a soft rate limit on a stallion rather than an asset that vanishes, and the existing same-stable booking path (`stableBreedRoute`) already accepts exactly this level of check-then-write race risk with no trip-wire. `stud.ts`'s own header comment explains the reasoning at length so a future session doesn't "fix" it by importing the heavier machinery for a problem that doesn't apply here.
- **`season_cap` is counted live**, `COUNT(*) FROM stud_bookings WHERE stud_listing_id = ? AND season_index = ?`, never a running counter on `stud_listings` — the same "boring implementation" reasoning `listings` never got a `sold_count`. `stud_bookings` is append-only and carries `covering_id`, so whether a specific booking actually took is one join away rather than duplicated.
- **A sold stallion's active stud listing is withdrawn, not carried to the buyer** — added to `listings.ts`'s own `buildSaleStatements`, in the same batch as the rest of the sale. This is a genuine design call this session made (not specified in the slice document): the fee and season cap were the previous owner's own choice, unlike pedigree or health knowledge, which the slice explicitly says travel with the horse.
- **A one-line circular-import avoidance worth knowing about**: `listings.ts` now imports `buildWithdrawStudListingsForHorseStatement` from `stud.ts` (for the point above), so `stud.ts` keeps its own tiny local copy of `commissionFor` rather than importing it back from `listings.ts` — both files' own comments cross-reference this, and the two copies must be kept in step by hand if the arithmetic ever changes (it is one line: `Math.floor(price * pct / 100)`).
- **The NPC ceiling now gates a second thing.** `npcStud.ts`'s `runNpcStudListings` reuses `qualityFor` (already exported from `npcMarket.ts`) against the same `npc_ceiling_schedule` row `runNpcBreedingDecisions` reads, and withdraws a listing the instant a stallion's realised quality exceeds it. This is new reasoning this session added, not copied from an existing pattern: buying an NPC horse outright only ever hands a player that horse's own (ceiling-bound) quality, but breeding to one *combines* it with the player's own stock — so a stud offering is a route around the ceiling in a way a sale isn't, and §13.4 names this risk by name.
- **Unlike `buy_offers`' `upsertActiveOfferForStable`, an NPC stallion's stud listing is not refreshed every tick once created** — only created if missing, or withdrawn if the ceiling check now fails. A buy offer's price tracks the posting stable's live wealth and target on purpose (it's standing demand); a stud fee reads more like a price an owner picked once, and churning it under a mare owner mid-decision seemed worse than a slowly stale number. Documented as a deliberate choice in `npcStud.ts`'s own header, in case a future session wants to reopen it.
- **A new `ActionKind`, `'book_stud'`** (cost 1), rather than reusing `book_covering` — a deliberate choice so the turns log can tell a cross-stable stud booking from an ordinary same-barn one at a glance. Offering a stallion at stud stays free, same reasoning as listing a horse for sale.
- **Two new ledger kinds**, `stud_fee_paid`/`stud_fee_received` (widened via the same table-rebuild shape every prior `ledger.kind` CHECK widening has used), plus the existing `commission` kind reused unchanged. The Money page's `KIND_LABELS` renamed `commission`'s label from "Sale commission" to "Market commission," since it now covers both.
- **The screens**: `/market/stud` (browse, All/Yours tabs, breed picker reused from the sale listing page) and `/market/stud/:id` (detail, health panel, book form) in `src/render/market.ts`/`src/routes/market.ts` — reusing `healthPanel` (newly exported) unchanged, since a mare owner deciding whether to book needs the exact same "not tested is a displayed row" disclosure a buyer does, read from the **stallion's own stable's** knowledge rather than the mare owner's (who typically has none of their own for a horse they don't own). A stallion's own page gains an "Offer at stud" / "Standing at stud" card (`studCard`, `src/render/horses.ts`), mirroring `sellCard` beside it — the suggested fee on the offer form reuses the same `npc_stud_fee_fraction` config key an NPC stallion's own fee is derived from, rather than a second number for the same question.
- **Retiring a listed stallion away withdraws his stud listing in the same batch as his own status change** (`buildWithdrawStudListingsForHorseStatement`), and the retire-away confirmation page names it, the exact same pattern the existing sale-listing withdrawal on retire already established. A stallion who dies or is retired away while standing at stud is also swept lazily by a new tick stage, `closeDeadStudListings`, the same "the tick catches what a route missed" shape `expireListings` uses for a dead horse's sale listing — no event is written for this one, since the horse's own death/retirement event already told the owner what happened.
- **`reset.ts`**: `stud_bookings` and `stud_listings` added to `HORSE_TABLES`, first in the list (children of `coverings`/`horses`/`stables`/each other, so they must empty before all four) — `test/reset.test.ts`'s own dependency-order test caught the exact position needed.
- **Test harness note**: `test/market/stud-d1.test.ts` and `test/npc/stud-d1.test.ts` are new files using the `node:sqlite` real-database harness (`test/market/sale-d1.test.ts`'s own header explains why), both added to `tsconfig.json`'s exclude list for the same reason every prior file in this pattern is there. The NPC-stud one includes a real regression test for the ceiling withdrawal: seed a stallion, list him, retune `npc_ceiling_schedule` far below anything a real horse could score, re-run, and assert the listing is gone.
- Verified: `npm test` (67 files, 550 tests — 14 new) and `npx tsc --noEmit` both clean, including the four new migrations applying to a real SQLite database alongside all prior ones. **Not** verified against a live `wrangler dev` or real D1 — no Cloudflare account/login in this sandbox, the same standing constraint every session in this log has noted. Before trusting this in play: apply the four new migrations at `/admin/migrations`, offer a stallion at stud from his own page, book a mare to him from another stable's account, and check both stables' Money pages show the fee and commission the way this entry describes.

**2026-08-04 — Slice 0021 specified: colour/pattern loci and `head_profile`.** `docs/slices/0021-colour-loci-and-head-profile.md`. Written from two SQL drafts the operator supplied; the content of both survived review largely intact, the mechanics did not. Four things a future session working anywhere near loci or traits should know, independent of whether this slice is ever built:

- **D1 is SQLite: `json_set`, not `jsonb_set`, and no `::jsonb` cast.** Both drafts were Postgres and would have failed at `/admin/migrations` — an error the operator cannot debug. The pattern that works, and that preserves an already-seeded JSON column rather than retyping it: `UPDATE breeds SET ideal_vector = json_set(ideal_vector, '$.traits.head_profile', json('{"target":35,"weight":0.8}')) WHERE code = 'QH';`. The `json()` wrapper is what makes SQLite store an object rather than a quoted string — without it the column silently gains a string where every reader expects an object.
- **Adding a locus to `LOCI` breaks every breed until the founding pools are re-seeded in the same push.** `parseAllelePool` (`src/engines/founding/pool.ts:17`) iterates every locus in `LOCI` and throws on a missing key, so founding stock, the consignment dealer, admin horse creation and `/admin/breeds` enabling all fail together. A breed that carries none of an allele needs the key present at `{"<wild>":1.0}`, not omitted.
- **The consistency tests hardcode migration filenames.** `test/genetics/consistency.test.ts` parses a fixed list (`0015` + `0050` for loci; `0029` + `0061` + `0081` for traits) and asserts exact agreement with `LOCI`/`TRAITS`. A new seed migration must be added to the relevant array — and the SQL alone or the TypeScript alone will fail the suite, which is the intended behaviour.
- **`scoreEntry` iterates `CONFORMATION_TRAITS`, not the ideal vector's own keys** (`src/engines/showing/score.ts:88`). A trait added to a breed's `ideal_vector` without being added to `TRAITS` is silently ignored — no error, no score change, nothing to see. Conversely `judges.trait_weights` defaults a missing trait to 1.0, so a new conformation trait quietly changes every seeded judge's character unless their weights are set deliberately.

Also recorded there: a fifth conformation trait cannot be given to a horse already born (`TRAITS` is append-only, a genotype is written once), so it costs either a world reset or an rng_seed-derived stand-in of the `fertilityPotential()` shape. The operator chose the reset.

**2026-08-04 — Slice 0021 Parts A-B built: `head_profile` and the ten colour/pattern loci land in the schema and the genetics engine — expression (Part C onward) is not built.** Migrations `0110`-`0114`. This is deliberately a partial build: `head_profile` is now a real trait a horse's genotype carries and a judge scores, and the ten new loci are now real Mendelian loci every founding horse can carry — but nothing yet turns those loci into a phenotype string, so no horse's *displayed colour* changes yet. Parts C (`expressPhenotype`), D (knowledge/market), E-F (the LWO lethal, the collapsed testing page) and G (the reset) are still exactly as specified and still unbuilt.

- **`TRAITS` (`src/engines/genetics/polygenic.ts`) gained `head_profile`, appended last** — after the three robustness traits, per the append-only rule §4.1 states at length. Unlike `fertility`, this needed no `*Potential()` stand-in: slice 0021's own §8 reset means no horse predates the key, so nothing reads a horse missing it. **That reset (Part G) has not happened yet** — this build only lands the code and the migrations; a session finishing Part C onward should do the reset once, at the end, not per-part.
- **`LOCI` (`src/engines/genetics/loci.ts`) gained ten loci, appended after `GBED`**: `D`, `Z`, `CH`, `RN`, `TO`, `O`, `SW1`, `SB1`, `LP`, `PATN1`. Dominant white (`W`) is dropped per the slice's own §1. Frame overo's `inheritance` is seeded `dominant`, not `recessive` — the pattern shows on one copy, only the `O/O` lethal is recessive, and the slice document names this as a correction to the operator's original draft.
- **`splitSqlStatements` (`src/lib/sql.ts`) splits on bare `;` with no string-literal awareness** — worth knowing before writing teaching-text prose in any future migration. A semicolon inside a `teaching_text` value (three of the new rows originally had one, e.g. "...is completely safe; two carriers...") silently truncates the `INSERT` mid-statement and produces a baffling "unrecognized token" error pointing at the *next* fragment, not the semicolon itself. Fixed by rephrasing as two sentences; there is no comment in `sql.ts` warning about this today and there probably should be if a future session has time.
- **The breed `ideal_vector` and `judges.trait_weights` patches use `json_set` against the already-seeded JSON**, exactly as the slice specifies, so `0035`/`0107`'s four existing targets per breed and `0033`'s three judges' existing weights are never retyped. This has one consequence for tests: `test/genetics/consistency.test.ts`'s breed-ideal-vector check and `test/founding/generate.test.ts`'s `QH_IDEAL` fixture both used to build a breed's ideal vector by regex-parsing a single migration's literal JSON — now they parse `0035`/`0107` for the base four traits and separately parse `0111`'s `json_set` calls for `head_profile`, merging the two by breed code before asserting. A future session patching a fifth breed field the same way should follow the same merge pattern rather than trying to make one regex do both jobs.
- **The founding pools (`0114`) rewrite each breed's whole pool as a literal**, per the slice's own recommendation ("easier to read and easier to check") rather than `json_set`-patching the ten new keys onto `0051`'s rows — this table is edited approximately never, so a literal costs nothing and stays parseable by the same regex `test/genetics/consistency.test.ts`'s pool test and `test/founding/generate.test.ts`/`test/health/pools.test.ts`'s own `poolJsonForBreed` helpers already use. **All three of those now point at `0114` as the authoritative pool migration, not `0051`** — a future session adding an eleventh locus should extend `0114`'s literals (or add a new migration and repoint these same three spots), not patch `0051`.
- **`CONFORMATION_TRAITS` and `ROBUSTNESS_TRAITS` are both derived by filtering `TRAITS`**, so `head_profile` joined the founding-specialist pool (slice 0019's `generate.ts:164`) with no code change — verified directly rather than assumed, per the slice's own §3.5 instruction to check. Conformation display (`conformationCard` in `src/render/horses.ts`) is likewise generic over whatever `ConformationDisplayRow[]` it's handed, so the fifth trait row appears with no rendering change either.
- Verified: `npm test` (68 files, 577 tests — 29 new/updated) and `npx tsc --noEmit` both clean, including all five new migrations applying to a real SQLite database alongside every prior one. **Not** verified against a live `wrangler dev` or real D1, and **not** verified visually — Part C (expression) doesn't exist yet, so there is nothing on any screen to look at that would show these new genes; the only observable effect right now is that `/horses/:id/test` would show more rows once Part D's testing-page work lands, and a founding horse's conformation card would show a fifth row. Before building Part C on top of this: read §5 of the slice closely, especially the CPU-ceiling warning in §6.2 — `foalColourPossibilities` going from 3 loci to 12 is the one place in this slice a naive implementation would actually break production.

**2026-08-04 — Slice 0021 Part C built: `expressPhenotype` turns the ten colour/pattern loci into a real displayed colour and pattern list.** `src/engines/genetics/expression.ts`, per the slice's own §5. Dun/champagne/silver compose into the colour string in a fixed step order (base → cream → dun → champagne → silver → roan → grey), with a small named-combination table (grullo, amber champagne, silver bay, dunskin, ...) as an override on a general "adjective order + cream/base name" fallback — so an eleventh modifier is one more adjective, not another 72-row table. Patterns (tobiano, frame overo, splash, sabino, appaloosa) are computed separately into `Phenotype.patterns`, never folded into `visibleColour` — that combinatorial explosion is exactly what `market_visible_colour_factors` (a flat colour→number map) can't survive. Migration `0115` seeds `config.values.pattern_penetrance`.

- **`expressPhenotype` gained two new parameters, `patternSeed` and `penetrance`**, both required in spirit though `penetrance` defaults to the slice's own suggested values for a caller with nothing worth plumbing (only `foal-colours.ts`'s synthetic E/A/CR-only genotype qualifies — every pattern locus reads absent there regardless of seed, so the default is inert, not a shortcut). Every real caller derives `patternSeed = deriveSeed(horse.rng_seed, 'pattern_expression')` and passes `config.values.pattern_penetrance` explicitly — this matters because the penetrance roll is not snapshotted onto the horse, so two call sites reading different penetrance values for the same horse at the same moment would show it with and without a pattern simultaneously. That consistency requirement, not just "plumb the type through," is why every one of `expressPhenotype`'s callers changed in this pass, not just the engine.
- **`describeHorseRow` (`src/routes/horses.ts`) gained a required `rng_seed` field and a `patternPenetrance` parameter**, which rippled into `OpenListingRow` (`src/db/listings.ts`) and `OpenStudListingRow` (`src/db/stud.ts`) both needing `h.rng_seed` added to their join `SELECT`s — neither had ever needed the horse's seed before this. Every one of the eight call sites across `src/routes/horses.ts`, `market.ts` and `world.ts` was updated to pass `ctx.config.values.pattern_penetrance` (or the local `cfg`), not a hardcoded default, for the same reason above.
- **Frame overo and splashed white are the only two loci with a real penetrance** (`config.values.pattern_penetrance: { O, SW1 }`); tobiano, roan, dun, champagne, silver, Sabino1 and the LP/PATN1 pair are fully penetrant, read straight off the genotype with no roll. This is a genuine mechanism this slice adds (not merely a data seed) — without it, a horse with no visible frame marking is `n/n` with certainty and the lethal (Part E, still unbuilt) could never be walked into by surprise, which is the whole teaching point of the eventual LWO event. Setting both penetrance values to `1.0` in config disables the mechanism with nothing else to change.
- **Roan's traditional names (blue/red/bay roan) only override when the horse is otherwise plain and undiluted** — checked as `dilutedColour === baseColour`, not by inspecting the dose/dun/champagne/silver flags directly. A buckskin roan or grullo roan falls through to the generic `"<colour> roan"` form, which is what the slice's own §5.3 examples show.
- **`render/founding.ts`'s `BREED_BLURBS`** had three sentences (Paso Fino, Icelandic, Nokota) claiming their signature pinto/dun/silver/roan colours "aren't in the game's genetics yet" — true when written, false as of this part. Updated to describe what those breeds' founding pools (`0114`) actually seed, since this is exactly the copy a child reads while choosing a breed.
- Verified: `npm test` (68 files, 595 tests — 18 new, in `expression.test.ts` and `describe.test.ts`; `inference.test.ts`'s 12 existing calls updated for the new signature but not rewritten) and `npx tsc --noEmit` both clean. **Not** verified against a live `wrangler dev` or real D1, and **not** verified visually — no world reset has happened (Part G, still unbuilt), so no existing horse carries any of these loci yet and there is nothing to look at on a real screen. Before building Part D on top of this: `inferFromPhenotype` and `foalColourPossibilities` still only know about `E`/`A`/`CR` — extending the latter to twelve loci is the CPU-ceiling risk the slice's own §6.2 warns about, unchanged by this part.

**2026-08-04 — Slice 0021 Parts D-F built: colour/pattern loci are now visible in knowledge, prediction and the market, and the testing page stopped being a wall of text.** `docs/slices/0021-colour-loci-and-head-profile.md` §6-7. Picks up where Parts A-C (schema, genetics, expression) left off.

- **`inferFromPhenotype` (`src/engines/genetics/inference.ts`) now covers all ten new loci**, not just E/A/CR/G/DMRT3. `D`/`CH`/`Z`(silver)/`RN` follow the same "visible → dominant present, zygosity unknown; not visible → homozygous wild type" shape, masked to fully-open under the same grey-stage check E/A/CR already used (patterns, unlike those five, are read straight off `phenotype.patterns` and are never masked by grey — `expressPhenotype` computes them independently of `greyStage`). `TO` is the same shape without a grey mask. `O` (frame overo) is the one asymmetric case: visible collapses to certain `O/n` because `O/O` never survives birth, not visible stays a genuine two-way open question because of the pattern's own penetrance (§5.5's cryptic carriers). **`SW1` (splashed white) needed the same asymmetric-but-non-lethal treatment, which the slice text didn't spell out explicitly** — since `SW1/SW1` is viable (no lethal mode), a visibly splashed horse is `SW1/SW1` or `SW1/n` (zygosity genuinely open, not certain like frame), and "not visible" stays open too (a cryptic carrier is possible) rather than collapsing to certain-clear the way the fully-penetrant D/CH/Z/RN/TO do. `SB1` always resolves to a single pair (three visibly distinct doses, matching the slice's own "a genotype test on this locus is worthless" convention). `LP`+`PATN1` are read together: no visible spotting → `LP` certain `lp/lp` and `PATN1` fully open (it has no effect without `Lp`); visible spotting → `LP` present with open zygosity, and `PATN1`'s status depends on whether the visible pattern is blanket/leopard (present, zygosity open) or plain appaloosa/few-spot (certain absent).
- **`Phenotype` gained four new fields** (`dunPresent`, `champagnePresent`, `silverPresent`, `roanPresent`) in `expression.ts` — raw, unconditional truth exactly like the existing `baseColour` field, populated from `expressPhenotype`'s own already-computed local booleans. These exist because `inferFromPhenotype` needs to know whether dun/champagne/silver/roan are individually present, and the named-combination table (`grullo`, `silver bay`, `dunskin`, ...) makes that unrecoverable by parsing the composed string back apart. Additive fields on an interface nothing constructs as a literal outside test fixtures (two of which needed updating) — nothing else broke.
- **`foalColourPossibilities` (`src/engines/genetics/foal-colours.ts`) is a full rewrite**, and the reason is worth recording for the next session that touches it: the straightforward reading of §6.2's "fold progressively over phenotype state" advice — one combined `(dose, dun, champagne, silver, roan, patterns[])` state map, naming each final leaf with a real `expressPhenotype` call — is *correct* (it does avoid the 3^13 genotype blowup, and the map itself tops out around 5,760 states on a maximally heterozygous pairing at every locus, "not half a million" as the slice's own §6.2 promised) but still measured at **~320-400ms** for that worst case, because naming ~40,000 leaf combinations (5,760 states × up to 7 base-genotype branches) each means building a `Genotype`, seeding an `Rng`, and walking every locus through `expressPhenotype` — cheap per call, ruinous at that volume, and nowhere near the free-tier 10ms budget. The fix actually shipped: the colour NAME only ever depends on five things (base category, dose, dun, champagne, silver, roan) — patterns are appended as plain words afterward, never folded into the name — so the file now runs **three independent folds**: a *dilution* fold (`CR`/`D`/`CH`/`Z`/`RN`, at most a few dozen distinct states, named with the real engine) crossed against at most 3 base categories (chestnut/bay/black — real E/A zygosity collapses to just these three for naming purposes, so the E/A loop no longer calls the engine per branch either), and a *pattern* fold (`TO`/`O`/`SW1`/`SB1`/`LP`+`PATN1`) that is pure string/array bookkeeping with **no engine calls at all**. The two are crossed at the very end with cheap string concatenation. Measured: the same worst-case fully-heterozygous-at-every-locus pairing now completes in low single-digit milliseconds. The lesson for a future session: "fold progressively" alone isn't sufficient when the thing you're folding *toward* (a full engine call) is itself not cheap at the volumes a large locus set produces — collapse to the smallest state that determines what you're about to compute, and defer anything that's just string concatenation until after the expensive part is done.
- **`unlockedColours` hints** (the "if tested, X becomes possible" teaching text) needed restructuring alongside the above: `unlockedNamesFor` now takes a `composeCandidate: (pair) => string` closure rather than doing per-locus dispatch internally, because E and A vary the *base* a name is composed from while every other locus varies *state* — conflating the two (an earlier draft of this part did) silently produced identical hint text regardless of which allele was hypothesised, which would have taught a child the wrong thing about exactly the two loci (E and A) most central to reading a horse's colour at all. Caught before landing, not after — worth flagging because it's the kind of bug that passes every test that doesn't specifically assert hint *content* varies with the candidate allele.
- **`market_pattern_factor`** (new, `appraise.ts` + `AppraiseParams.hasPattern`) is one flat multiplier applied once if `phenotype.patterns.length > 0`, never stacked per pattern — the same restraint `market_visible_colour_factors` was already written with (`0095`'s own comment: colour must never out-compete conformation). `market_visible_colour_factors` picked up the new named dilutions (`grullo`, `amber champagne`, `silver bay`, `dunskin`, the three named roans, ...) via `json_set` patching individual keys — confirmed first that SQLite's JSON path syntax accepts an unquoted space in a dotted key segment (`'$.market_visible_colour_factors.bay dun'`) using Node's built-in `node:sqlite` against a throwaway in-memory table, since the project has no local `sqlite3` CLI and D1 uses the same SQLite JSON1 implementation.
- **Migration numbering drifted from the slice's own §9 table** because `0115` had already landed (Part C) as `config_pattern_penetrance` alone, not the combined `config_colour_patterns` the table names — `market_pattern_factor` and the colour-factor additions became a new `0116_config_colour_patterns_market.sql`, pushing the LWO condition (the slice's own `0116`) to `0117_condition_lethal_white.sql`. Both registered in `src/db/migrations.ts`.
- **Lethal white overo (`0117`) needed no new code at all**, exactly as the slice predicted — one `INSERT INTO conditions` row (`LWO`, locus `O`, `{"mode":"recessive"}`, `severity_class: 'lethal'`), modelled line-for-line on `0053`'s GBED row. The existing lethal-foal-death path (`src/db/pregnancies.ts`, `src/engines/health/status.ts`) and the breeding preview's recessive-risk health line both pick it up automatically because they already iterate `getEnabledConditions()` rather than a hardcoded list. One thing worth being explicit about for whoever reads this next: LWO's health-line warning is gated on a stable having bought the **disease** test (`horse_knowledge.subject_code = 'LWO'`, bare code) — separate from the **colour** test on the `O` locus (`subject_code = 'locus:O'`). Testing colour alone (which most players will do, since it's the cheaper/first thing to test on a visibly-patterned horse) does not surface the lethal-pairing warning; only the dedicated disease-panel test does. This is consistent with how GBED etc. already work, but it means a player who tests `O` for colour reasons and never buys the `LWO` disease test gets no warning before breeding two frame carriers — worth flagging to the operator as a possible follow-up (e.g., bundling `LWO` into the colour test when `O` shows a carrier result) if it turns out to be confusing in play.
- **The testing page's four collapsed groups** (`Base colour`, `Dilutions`, `Patterns`, `Gait`) are presentational only (`colourGroupFor` in `src/render/horses.ts`), not a second copy of `loci.category` — `PATN1` (category `modifier`) sits in Patterns exactly as the slice specified. **Grey (`G`, also category `modifier`) wasn't mentioned anywhere in the slice's own four-group enumeration** — an omission, not a decision recorded anywhere. Judgment call: grey joined Dilutions (it modifies how the base colour shows, the same conceptual bucket as cream/dun/champagne/silver), flagged here rather than silently resolved so a future session can override it if the operator disagrees. The horse page's own Colour and gait card got the same collapse treatment but as a single `<details>` (not four sub-groups, which the slice's wording for that specific section didn't ask for) — summary line is the horse's actual visible colour plus pattern words, e.g. "bay tobiano".
- **Part G (the world reset) was deliberately not run.** It's a one-way, operator-confirmed action per the slice's own §8, and this build only lands the code and migrations behind it. Until the operator triggers a `world`-scope reset from `/admin`, no existing horse carries a `head_profile` genotype or any of the ten new loci — this was already true after Parts A-C and remains true now.
- Verified: `npm test` (68 files, 609 tests — 33 new, across `inference.test.ts`, `foal-colours.test.ts` and `appraise.test.ts`) and `npx tsc --noEmit` both clean. The CPU-guard test (`foal-colours.test.ts`, "the CPU guard") is the one that would have caught the ~320ms regression above had it existed before the rewrite — a future session touching this file should run it before assuming a change is CPU-safe, not just correct. **Not** verified against a live `wrangler dev` or real D1, and **not** verified visually, for the same reason as the Parts A-C entry above: no world reset has happened, so there is nothing on a real screen yet to look at. `/admin/migrations` should show `0116` and `0117` as pending in any environment that already had `0110`-`0115` applied.

**2026-08-04 — Slice 0020 built: the twelve acquired conditions (colic, laminitis, and the rest).** Migrations `0118`-`0121`. `docs/slices/0020-acquired-conditions.md`. The single-gene panel (slice 0010) got a sibling category with a genuinely different shape: not a fixed genotype-derived fact, but a real event with an open outcome, resolved after a treatment window that reads care state, workload, location, feed and (for four of the twelve) a heritable robustness score nothing had read since slice 0014 built it.

- **A fifth `severity_class`, `'acute'`, added with no migration of its own** — confirmed `conditions.severity_class` carries no `CHECK` (migrations/0052), so this is a new value in an already-open set, the same way slice 0010's `horse_conditions.state` values were.
- **`src/engines/health/acquired.ts` is the whole risk model, and it is genuinely two functions, not twelve.** `onsetProbability` takes pre-derived 0..1 factors (`carePenaltyFactor`, `workloadFactor`, `feedRiskFactor`, `robustnessPotential`) rather than the raw entities the slice's own sketch signature named — the care/workload/feed derivations live in `src/db/acquiredConditions.ts` instead, which keeps the pure engine free of any config-key or timer-shape knowledge and matches this codebase's existing "engine takes numbers, caller derives them" split (`careModifier` itself takes a pre-computed `feedDelta`, not a feed level). `rollOutcome` draws once against a condition's own `outcomesUntreated`/`outcomesTreated` cumulative distribution.
- **Two of the slice document's own outcome tables didn't sum to 1.0** — Strangles untreated summed to 0.85 (missing 0.15) and treated to 0.95 (missing 0.05); Eye injury untreated summed to 0.95 (missing 0.05). Found by writing the exact test the slice's own §10 test 4 specified ("a table that does not sum to 1.0 is a bug in this document, not just in code") before touching the seed data at all — the test failed against the document's own numbers on the first run. Fixed by raising each row's `resolved` share to close the gap (Strangles: 0.90/0.05/0/0.05 untreated, 0.95/0.03/0/0.02 treated; Eye injury: 0.70/0.30/0/0 untreated), preserving every other row's ratios and the named death rates from §4.1's own table. Worth remembering for any future slice that ships probability tables by hand: write the sum-to-1.0 test *first*, against the seed data, not just against the engine.
- **`careStateFor` doesn't exist — `carePenaltyFactorFor` derives neglect from the existing `TimerResult.delta` instead of a new ramp.** Takes the worse (not the average) of the farrier and wellness timers' own overdue fraction, normalised by each service's own configured penalty. `feedRiskFactorFor` similarly avoids hardcoding feed-level names: it normalises the current level's `upkeep_multiplier` against the min/max across whatever levels are configured, so Premium (the richest, priciest level) always reads as the highest laminitis risk without the engine or its caller ever writing the string `"premium"`.
- **A death outcome reuses `buildEndHorseParticipationStatements` (`src/db/ageing.ts`), not `killDueLethalFoals`'s own status-update path**, despite the slice's own §7.2 naming the latter. `killDueLethalFoals` never cancels a live pregnancy/covering or withdraws a show entry because it doesn't need to — a GBED-affected foal is 30 game days old and has none of those. An acquired-condition death can hit a horse of any age with real breeding and show activity in flight, so the fuller adult-horse exit path is the correct one. Verified against a real database (`test/health/acquired-d1.test.ts`) that a covering and a show entry are actually cancelled/withdrawn on death — this would have been silently wrong (a "dead" horse still booked to cover a mare) had the letter of §7.2 been followed instead of its intent.
- **No `horse_died` event is written on an acquired-condition death, deliberately departing from §7.2's literal instruction.** That event's render (`src/render/stables.ts`) opens "A mare's foal has died" — worded specifically for a newborn, and would misrender for, say, a fourteen-year-old show horse dying of untreated colic. `incident_resolved`'s own death-outcome sentence carries the calm, blameless narrative instead, in the register slice 0010 §5.6 established and this slice's own §4.5 drafted.
- **`getEnabledConditions()` now returns the twelve acquired rows alongside the single-gene panel, and four existing call sites assumed every condition had a `locus_code`** — the consignment dealer's disease-panel pre-testing (`src/db/consignment.ts`, both `runConsignments` and `forceConsignmentBatchNow`), the NPC market's own genotype-panel purchase (`src/db/npcMarket.ts`), the Health card (`src/routes/horses.ts`'s `healthRowsFor`), the genotype-test page (`buildTestPageRows`), and the market listing's disclosed-conditions list (`src/routes/market.ts`) all crashed or would have rendered "Colic: Not tested" nonsense before being filtered to `category !== 'acquired'` (or `locus_code !== null`, for the two NPC paths that select genotype tests). **Any future session adding a third `conditions.category`** should grep every `getEnabledConditions(` call site rather than assume the existing filters are exhaustive — this list of five was not obvious from reading any single file.
- **Two new show-eligibility reasons** (`acute_incident`, `degenerative_incident`) added to `src/engines/showing/eligibility.ts`'s closed set, computed by `acquiredBarringFlags` (`src/db/acquiredConditions.ts`) from `horse_conditions` truth — an open incident clears the moment it resolves; a `degenerative` outcome is permanent and lives on the per-*incident* `outcome` column, not a per-*condition* `bars_showing` flag (unlike the single-gene panel), because two different incidents of the same acquired condition on the same horse can resolve differently.
- **`careModifier`'s `conditionDelta` slot gained its second contributor** (`acuteCarePenaltyMapForHorses`), summed with slice 0014's unmanaged-single-gene delta at both call sites that read it (the horse page, and the show-judging loop in `src/db/shows.ts` — a horse entered before an incident started and not yet judged still scores with the penalty applied). The barn list's own care display does **not** get this treatment, because it never read `conditionDeltaMapForHorses` either — a pre-existing, unrelated omission, not something this slice introduced or was asked to fix.
- **Tested against a real database**, not just pure functions: `test/health/acquired-d1.test.ts` uses the same node:sqlite-backed D1 shim `test/market/sale-d1.test.ts` established, applying every real migration file. One gotcha worth recording for the next session writing a `-d1.test.ts` file: `getConditions`/`getConfig` both cache in module scope for 60 seconds (correct in a real Worker isolate, where module state is per-isolate) — but every test in a `-d1.test.ts` file builds a *fresh* in-memory database, so without `vi.resetModules()` between tests and a dynamic `await import(...)` per test, a later test's `env` would silently read an earlier test's cached rows. `test/db/disciplines.test.ts` already established this pattern for a fake `Env`; it applies equally to a real one.
- **`/admin/config` and `/admin/incidents` are separate on purpose**: the fifteen new config keys (workload window/ceiling, the onset-probability ceiling, the acute care penalty, and twelve per-condition treatment costs) joined `/admin/config`'s existing `NUMERIC_CONFIG_KEYS`/`DECIMAL_CONFIG_KEYS` generic-parsing lists and got real form fields, so the operator can retune them from a browser with no deploy — §12 names this as the single biggest open question in the whole slice. `/admin/incidents` stays read-only-plus-one-testing-control (open counts, the real outcome split, a force-incident button), the same division `/admin/health` vs `/admin/config` already models for the single-gene panel.
- Verified: `npm test` (70 files, 635 tests — 26 new, across `test/health/acquired.test.ts` (16, pure engine + the seed-migration sum-to-1.0 check), `test/health/acquired-d1.test.ts` (7, real-database) and `test/showing/eligibility.test.ts`'s three new cases) and `npx tsc --noEmit` both clean, including all four new migrations applying to a real SQLite database alongside every prior one (121 total). One pre-existing, unrelated failure on this machine (`foal-colours.test.ts`'s CPU-budget test, a 50ms budget that runs 49-60ms depending on machine load — confirmed present before this slice's changes and flaky independent of them). **Not** verified against a live `wrangler dev` or real D1, and **not** verified visually — no browser was available in this session to click through the Incidents card, the barn badge, or `/admin/incidents`.

**2026-08-04 — NPC stables have income again: an income floor and the pet home.** New files `src/db/npcFinance.ts`, `src/db/petHome.ts` and `src/db/horseRemoval.ts`; migrations `0126`-`0128`. Specified in conversation, not a slice document.

Numbered `0126`-`0128` rather than `0118`-`0120` as first written: slice 0020 landed on `main` while this branch was open and took `0118` (the consignment change) and `0122`-`0125`. These three were renumbered to sit after all of it before merging, which was safe only because none of them had been applied anywhere yet — the forward-only rule (CLAUDE.md §8) bites the moment one has. Checked at the same time, and worth repeating as the routine for this situation: slice 0020 adds no table referencing `horses` and no `ALTER TABLE ledger`, so `deletableHorseSql`'s list below is still complete and `0128`'s ledger rebuild still copies the right columns.

Background, because the reasoning is the whole point: NPC stables only show when a player enters the same class, and only take the slots the players left (`judgeOneClass`'s never-top-up-an-empty-field rule, unchanged and still correct). That makes prize money — the game's only recurring money faucet — a function of how much the children happen to show, which is exactly the variable nobody can control. Their outgoings are real and did not change: they buy player horses on both routes in `npcBuying.ts` and pay `genotype_panel_cost` (700) every time they list. A quiet fortnight earned them nothing, so they stopped buying and stopped listing, and the market was dead exactly when the children came back to it. All three NPC stables were seeded at a balance of 0 (`0085`, and `0086` zeroed Fair Meadow) and had never been given a way to earn reliably.

- **Slice 0015 §2.5 is the precedent this follows, on the other side of the ledger.** That decision exempted NPC stables from board because "nobody is playing the stable, so the charge models no decision." The symmetric conclusion is that NPC *income* should also be a tunable rather than a simulation. Read that section before changing anything here — a future session that reintroduces NPC upkeep without revisiting this will break both halves at once.
- **Two mechanisms, and they are not interchangeable.** The income floor (`runNpcBalanceFloor`, `npc_policy.balance_floor`) is a *safety net*: once every `npc_balance_floor_interval_game_days` (360, a game year) a stable below its floor is topped back up to it, and a stable that is earning gets nothing. The pet home is the *income*. **If /admin/npc shows a stable's balance parked at its floor, that stable is living on charity and something upstream is wrong** (bad breeding, or `pet_home_payout_fraction` set too low); the two floor columns exist to make that visible.
- **The floor's marker advances whether or not any money moved, and this is load-bearing.** If `last_floor_topup_game_day` only advanced on an actual top-up, a healthy stable would stay permanently "due" and be restored the instant it dipped one unit below its floor — turning the floor into unlimited money for the buying routes. `test/npc/pet-home-d1.test.ts`'s "does not refill a stable that spends its floor away before the interval is up" pins this; do not "simplify" the marker update back inside the `if`.
- **A NULL marker means "never topped up" and reads as immediately due**, which is what gives the three existing zero-balance stables an opening balance on the first tick after `0126` lands — deliberately instead of a backfill migration.

**The pet home (`src/db/petHome.ts`) is one mechanic with two entry points, and the operator asked explicitly for it to stay that way** — same price, same ledger kind, same rule about whether the row survives.

- **The two ways in are deliberately asymmetric, and a future session must not "tidy" that.** A player's horse goes to a pet home *only* when that player clicks through `/horses/:id/pet-home` — there is no tick stage, timer or automatic path that can send a child's horse away, and a player's listing expiring still returns the horse to the barn exactly as before. An NPC stable's horse goes *only* by its listing running the full window unsold; an NPC never "decides" to use a pet home. The tick stage enforces its half structurally (it joins `npc_policy` and filters `is_npc = 1`); the player's half is enforced by there being no caller of `sellHorseToPetHome` except the route. The file header says this in as many words.
- **Price: `pet_home_payout_fraction`, 0.4 of appraised value, floored at `market_min_value`.** One number for both sides. It is meant to be a bad deal — a pet home has to be worse than selling to a player or nobody would ever use the market. No commission: commission is what the market charges for finding a buyer, and this is exactly the case where it found none. **Worth flagging for the first retune:** the operator's target payout was 600-950, but against the appraisal band `/admin/config` documents (an average adult horse 800-1,500) 0.4 yields 320-600. Either the fraction wants raising to ~0.6 or the appraisal band has drifted upward in play — check a real horse's guide value on the market before changing anything.
- **Its own ledger kind, `pet_home_payout` (`0128`), not `sale`.** So the ledger distinguishes a child buying a horse from the world beyond the five of them taking one. `getSeasonTradeSummary` counts it toward /admin/npc's earned-selling column all the same, since it is still money earned by producing a horse worth something. The Money page's `KIND_LABELS` is a `Record<LedgerKind, string>`, so `tsc` catches a missing label — that is why every new kind must be added there too.
- **The tick stage runs immediately before `expireListings` and never after it.** That ordering is what lets the split work with no change at all to `src/db/listings.ts`: the pet home claims the NPC listings that timed out with a live horse, `expireListings` closes everything left. Reversed, `expireListings` closes them first and the pet home finds nothing.
- **It excludes the Consignment Yard by joining `npc_policy`,** not by naming the prefix. The dealer mints stock out of nothing and nothing reads its balance, so paying it would print money into a stable with no use for it — and it already has its own cleanup path (`releaseExpiredConsignments`).

**Whether a departing horse's row survives is `src/db/horseRemoval.ts`'s rule, shared by both entry points.** The operator's rule: **a horse that never showed and never conceived is deleted outright; anything else is marked `removed` and kept.**

- **"Bred" means pregnant, not covered** — the operator's own clarification, and it changes real behaviour. A mare covered who never caught, or a stallion whose covering never took, *is* deletable. That is why `coverings` is absent from the eligibility list and present in the delete list instead.
- **The consequence of that, named because it is the one genuinely lossy part:** a covering names a mare *and* a stallion, so deleting a horse with a failed covering also removes the counterpart's record of that attempt. It cannot be kept for one side only, and a covering with no pregnancy behind it is precisely what the operator judged not worth keeping.
- **`deletableHorseSql` is doing two jobs at once, which is why it is longer than the phrase suggests.** It is the operator's rule *and* the proof that the `DELETE` cannot break a foreign key: every one of the fourteen tables with a `REFERENCES horses (id)` column is either named in the eligibility check (so the horse is kept) or cleaned up by `buildDeleteHorseStatements` (so the reference goes with it). **A future migration adding a fifteenth must put it in one list or the other**, or the delete starts throwing inside the tick batch. This was not theoretical — the first draft omitted `coverings` and `pregnancies.foal_id` and failed against a real SQLite with `PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON`.
- **The two stud clauses are deliberately stricter than the operator's rule.** A stud booking is a cross-stable transaction with a player's money in it, and deleting a horse in one would delete the covering underneath it, emptying the record of what a child actually paid for. So a horse that ever stood at stud or was booked to one is kept whole even with no pregnancy. It also happens to make the covering delete safe: a booking names both its horses, so no covering a `stud_booking` points at can belong to a deletable horse.
- **`pregnancies.foal_id` is set to NULL rather than the pregnancy being deleted.** Most of what gets cleared is an NPC's own home-bred surplus, so a pregnancy usually points at it — but that pregnancy is its *dam's* history, which the rule is not about. NULL is already an ordinary value in that column, and the row keeps its status, dates and both parents, so the record that she foaled survives.
- **Nothing touches `ledger`** (append-only, CLAUDE.md §7). Receipts carry the horse's name as text so they stay readable; only the `reference_id` pointer is lost. For a *deleted* horse the receipt deliberately references the stable rather than the listing, because listing ids are rowids SQLite can hand out again — a dangling pointer would eventually name somebody else's listing.
- **`endedDescription` (`src/render/horses.ts`) had to learn `end_reason`.** A pet-home horse is `removed` too, so without that check every one would have claimed it was "Retired away" on the past-horses list.
- **A broke NPC stable now goes dark instead of lowballing.** `refreshOneOffer`'s `maxPrice` floors at `market_min_value` (50), so a stable at 0 used to advertise 50 for a horse worth a thousand — which reads to a child as a broken game, not a bad deal. Both buying routes now gate on `npc_buy_offer_min_balance` (500) from one config key, so they can never disagree about whether a stable is solvent. `test/npc/buying-d1.test.ts` had a test asserting the *old* behaviour; it is now inverted and carries a comment saying so.
- **`balance_floor` is passed explicitly by every `npc_policy` insert, never left to the column's `DEFAULT 0`.** `src/db/reset.ts`'s three recreated stables mirror `0126`'s values and `foundNpcStable` takes it as a required parameter with a field on the /admin/npc form. This matters more than it looks: slice 0021's Part G world reset has not been run yet and very likely will be, and a reset that recreated every NPC stable with the floor silently off would not surface until the market stopped moving weeks later.
- **The numbers are all guesses** — floors of 5000/5000/3000 by personality, a 360-day interval, a 0.4 pet-home fraction, a 500 buying minimum. The floor can create at most one floor per stable per game year (about 12 real days at three ticks a day), small next to what player upkeep drains over the same period. Prefer retuning the fraction over shortening the floor interval — the interval is the one number that raises the ceiling on money created from nothing.
- Verified: `npm test` (69 files, 632 tests — 22 new in `test/npc/pet-home-d1.test.ts`, 1 inverted in `buying-d1.test.ts`) and `npx tsc --noEmit` both clean, including all three new migrations applying to a real SQLite database alongside every prior one, and the seeded values read back and confirmed (`pet_home_payout_fraction` 0.4, floors 5000/5000/3000, the old clearance key gone, the widened `ledger.kind` CHECK accepting `pet_home_payout`). **Not** verified against a live `wrangler dev` or real D1, and **not** verified visually — the new confirmation page and the horse page's new link have not been looked at in a browser. **Nothing here has been watched in play**: no NPC stable has run a game year with a floor, and no horse has yet gone to a pet home.

**2026-08-04 — Slice 0022 built: incidents untangled from the genetics tables (Part A), and a plain word next to each conformation trait (Part B).** `docs/slices/0022-incidents-untangled-and-conformation-labels.md`.

- **Part A moved the twelve acquired conditions out of `conditions`/`horse_conditions` for good.** New tables `incident_types` (reference data) and `horse_incidents` (per-episode truth), migrations `0129`-`0133` in order: create `incident_types`, seed the twelve, create `horse_incidents`, backfill it from every acquired row `horse_conditions` still carried (open incidents included — nothing mid-incident lost its window), then delete the twelve from `conditions`/`horse_conditions`. `incident_types` is reference data and is never touched by a reset, exactly like `conditions` itself; `horse_incidents` is per-horse and is deleted by both reset scopes, right before `horses`, exactly like `horse_conditions`.
- **`treatmentWindowGameDays`/`treatmentCostKey` were pulled out of the risk-model JSON onto real columns** (`incident_types.treatment_window_game_days`/`treatment_cost_key`) rather than staying duplicated — both are genuinely read by the database layer (window arithmetic, cost lookup), unlike `locus_code`/`severity_class`/`test_cost_key`/`breed_associations`/`signs_visible`/`bars_showing`, which were dead weight or a lie for these twelve and simply don't exist on the new table at all.
- **Two files renamed, in place, with their full history of comments carried over**: `src/engines/health/acquired.ts` → `src/engines/incidents/risk.ts` (the pure risk/outcome maths, `AcquiredTrigger` renamed `IncidentRiskModel`), and `src/db/acquiredConditions.ts` → `src/db/incidents.ts`. Every caller updated: `src/db/shows.ts`, `src/db/tick.ts`, `src/routes/admin.ts`, `src/routes/horses.ts`, `src/render/admin.ts`, `src/render/horses.ts`.
- **All six `category !== 'acquired'` guards named in the slice document are gone**, not fixed — two in `src/routes/horses.ts`, one each in `src/routes/market.ts`, `src/db/npcMarket.ts`, `src/db/consignment.ts` (that last one was actually `locus_code !== null`, serving the identical purpose; removed for the same reason). `getEnabledConditions`/`getConditions` now return genetics-only rows with no filtering needed anywhere, because the twelve simply aren't in that table any more. `src/db/health.ts`'s `getLethalTriggers` needed no change at all — its `severity_class === 'lethal'` filter was never one of the six and stayed correct throughout.
- **`src/db/horseRemoval.ts` and `src/db/reset.ts` both got the new fifteenth-foreign-key-into-horses treatment** their own header comments ask for: `horse_incidents` added to `buildDeleteHorseStatements` and to `reset.ts`'s `HORSE_TABLES` (before `horses`, beside `horse_conditions`), with the running counts in both files' prose updated so the next session's own future add lands correctly.
- **The Incidents card learned a history window** (`incident_history_game_days`, live config, default 720 = two game years). Display-only — `incidentsForHorse` still reads every row, just stops putting old resolved-and-plain ones in the `history` array, counting them into a new `hiddenCount` instead so the card can say "3 earlier incidents, all resolved." rather than silently dropping them. An open incident or a `degenerative` outcome is never hidden by the window, matched by a dedicated `test/incidents/incidents-d1.test.ts` case.
- **Part B added `src/engines/conformation/labels.ts`**, a small pure module reusing `traitScoreFor` — a function pulled *out* of `scoreEntry` (`src/engines/showing/score.ts`) specifically so the label and the judge's real score share one formula, never two (CLAUDE.md §13). Four new live config keys are the band edges (`conformation_label_outstanding_min`/`_good_min`/`_acceptable_min`/`_weak_min`, seeded 90/75/55/30 by migration `0135`). The gate — at least one show start, and a breed with an `ideal_vector` — reads as `'unknown'` either way, rendered "Unknown," never blank.
- **The horse page's Conformation card and the breeding preview's "This pairing" card both call the same `conformationLabelsFor`/`conformationLabelsForHorse` helpers** (`routes/horses.ts`), each parent scored against *its own* breed, never a shared or foal's-breed vector. The breeding preview renders labels as a small table (words only, no bars — ten bars across a card that already carries four other blocks was judged unreadable on a phone) with one sentence warning that a foal is not the average of its parents.
- **CSS**: `.meter-line`/`.meter-verdict` added to `public/style.css`, wrapping the existing `.meter` bar so the verdict word sits to its right without pushing off a narrow screen — `min-width: 0` on `.meter` inside `.meter-line` is load-bearing, not decoration (a flex item's default `min-width: auto` is exactly the mechanism that causes the overflow this exists to prevent). Colour reinforces the band (`--color-success`/`--color-warning`/`--color-danger`/muted) but the word itself always carries the meaning on its own.
- **This codebase has no dark-mode stylesheet at all** — `public/style.css` is a single fixed palette (confirmed: no `prefers-color-scheme` block anywhere in the file), so the new label colours were added straight against the existing named CSS variables with no theme-variant work needed.
- Both old test files (`test/health/acquired.test.ts`, `test/health/acquired-d1.test.ts`) moved to `test/incidents/` and rewritten against the new tables/imports; a new `test/conformation/labels.test.ts` covers the banding pure function, and `test/reset.test.ts`'s foreign-key map and never-cleared list both got the new table names. `tsconfig.json`'s per-file `exclude` list (for the two D1-shim test files that need `node:fs`/`node:sqlite` types the rest of the codebase deliberately doesn't carry) was updated to the new paths.
- Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, and `npx vitest run` green (one unrelated flaky CPU-timing test in `test/genetics/foal-colours.test.ts` that fails only under full-suite parallel load and passes every time run in isolation — pre-existing, not touched by this slice). **Not** verified against a live `wrangler dev` or real D1, and **not** verified visually in a browser — the reserved-column width at 320px the slice document asks to check by eye has not actually been looked at.
- **The operator plans to run a full world reset once this lands** (recorded here since it shapes what "done" means for this slice): `incident_types` survives any reset scope, exactly like `conditions`; `horse_incidents` is emptied by both scopes, exactly like `horse_conditions`, and was checked explicitly for this reason before anything else in Part A was written.

**2026-08-04 - The tick can fail and never say why: `tick_run.error_text` is now shown on `/admin/world`.**

- **The bug this exists to end.** `executeTick` (`src/db/tick.ts`) has stored the thrown message in `tick_run.error_text` since slice 0001, and `renderWorldPage` has never rendered that column. A failed tick therefore read as the bare word `error` in the Recent ticks table with no way, from the operator's own tools, to find out what went wrong - and CLAUDE.md §3 is explicit that a cron tick does not retry, so a silent failure is a skipped tick nobody hears about. The message now gets its own full-width row directly under a failed run (`.tick-error`, wraps anywhere - an exception message can be long and contain no spaces), rather than a seventh column that would be empty on every healthy run and push the table off a phone screen.
- **`listRecentTickRuns` orders by `id`, not `tick_seq`, and that is load-bearing.** A failed run does not advance `world.tick_seq` - `executeTick` only writes it in the success path - so **every retry of the same tick shares one `tick_seq`**. `ORDER BY tick_seq DESC` left those ties in whatever order SQLite chose, which printed a run of consecutive failures oldest-first *inside* a newest-first list. Reading the failure history off that table was actively misleading. `id` is monotonic per insert and is the only column in `tick_run` that reliably means "when did this run happen".
- **The shape of a repeated-failure log, for whoever reads one next.** Several `error` rows sharing a `tick_seq` and ending in one `ok` mean the tick was *making progress on each attempt* - the stages are individually committed (D1 gives no transaction across them, which is why §5.4 demands idempotency), so a retry re-runs the earlier stages as cheap no-ops and gets further. That signature points at a per-invocation ceiling being hit part-way through, not at a deterministic exception - a deterministic exception fails identically forever.
- **Two structural suspects found while looking, neither fixed here, both worth a session of their own.** (1) **N+1 round trips in the per-tick loops**, which the 2026-08-04 data migrations multiplied several-fold at once: `judgeOneClass` (`src/db/shows.ts`) runs `checkHorseEligibilityForClass` - 3 D1 round trips and one `expressPhenotype` per horse - over *every* NPC horse in the world, for *every* class needing a top-up, and migration `0107` took a show from 2 classes to 14 while `stockShowBarn` went breed-aware (`npc_show_barn_size` 6 is now 6 *per breed*); `scoreCandidates` (`src/db/npcBuying.ts`) does `getHorse` per open listing per policy, and migration `0137` took the roster from 3 policies to 8; `eligibleMaresAndStallions` (`src/db/npcBreeding.ts`) does 2 queries per mare per stable. (2) **Six unbounded `IN (?,?,...)` lists** built with `horseIds.map(() => '?')` (`src/db/health.ts` x2, `src/db/horses.ts` x2, `src/db/incidents.ts` x2). D1 caps bound parameters per query - 100, last time it was checked, and the docs were unreachable from the build environment to re-confirm. `rollAcuteIncidents` binds one parameter per alive horse in the world and `loadPedigreeContextForMany` binds one per horse *plus every ancestor*, so both have a hard population ceiling that no test catches: the local SQLite shim the D1 tests run against does not enforce D1's limit.
- Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean and `npm test` green (72 files, 676 tests). **Not** verified in a browser, and - the point of the change - **the actual tick error message has not been read yet**; that needs this deployed and one failing tick.

---

**2026-08-04 - Other people's horses now show their record the way your own does: grouped by class type.**

- **The bug, in one sentence.** The Show record card had two different implementations. The owner's own horse page grouped placings under a heading per class type (Conformation, Endurance, Show Jumping, ...); the three screens where you look at *somebody else's* horse - a sale listing (`/market/:id`), a stud listing (`/market/stud/:id`) and the public `/world/horses/:id` page - showed a single flat list of five placings with the show's made-up name attached and no class type anywhere. Those three are precisely the screens where the split matters most: a stallion's record is the only honest thing a mare owner has to go on, and "2nd - red ribbon at Stonebrook Winter Show" does not say whether he was second in a halter class or second over fences.
- **One builder, one renderer, four screens.** `buildShowResultGroups` and `showResultGroupsHtml` now live in `src/render/shows.ts` beside `placingText`, along with the two constants they need (`SHOW_RESULT_GROUP_CAP` = 5, `SHOW_RESULT_FETCH_LIMIT` = 200). The grouping loop was lifted verbatim out of `routes/horses.ts` - the owner's card renders from exactly the same code it always did, so this was a pure move for that screen and a real change for the other three. `ShowResultGroup` moved from `render/horses.ts` to `render/shows.ts` with it.
- **The fetch limit is why this was not a two-line change.** The cap is *per group*, so the rows have to be read before it is known which group each one lands in. All three non-owner screens were asking `listRecentResultsForHorse` for 5 rows; a horse with a busy dressage career would have had every other class type fall off the bottom before grouping ever ran. They now ask for `SHOW_RESULT_FETCH_LIMIT` (200) like the horse page always has. That is one more D1 round trip's worth of rows on a page that already does seven reads, not a new query.
- **The show name is deliberately dropped from the non-owner screens too.** It was the one thing the flat format carried that the grouped one does not. The reasoning in `routes/horses.ts` applies identically to a buyer: the venue names are procedurally generated and near-identical, and which *kind* of class a result came from is the information. The market's existing "A show record is the honest measure of a horse you do not own" paragraph is untouched and now sits under a card that actually delivers on it.
- **Field renames rather than silent reuse**: `recentResults: string[]` became `recentResultGroups: ShowResultGroup[]` on `ListingDetailView`, `StudListingDetailView` and `PublicHorseView`. `buildPublicHorseView`'s explicit named-field contract (`src/routes/world.ts`, and the test that guards it) meant the public view could not pick this up by accident - it had to be added by name, which is the point of that function.
- New `test/showing/showResultGroups.test.ts` covers the shared builder directly: grouping, group ordering by most recent activity, the per-group cap not being crowded out by one long career, the `discipline_name` null fallback, and the absence of the show name.
- Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean and `npx vitest run` green (73 files, 682 tests). **Not** verified in a browser - the four cards have not been looked at side by side on a phone, which is the one check that would confirm they now read identically. The flaky CPU-timing test in `test/genetics/foal-colours.test.ts` noted in the slice 0022 entry above surfaced again under full-suite load and passes in isolation; unrelated and untouched.

---

**2026-08-04 - Two operator-reported defects, both "the rule was written and never actually applied to a real horse".**

Reported together from the deployed game, fixed together, sharing no code. Both are the same *shape*: a rule this project decided on deliberately, implemented correctly one layer down, and then blocked by a guard one layer up that nobody had checked against a real horse's data.

**Part A - the pet home's delete rule had never once fired for a home-bred horse.**

- **The report.** A colt sent to a pet home was still in the barn list with a badge, days later. The operator's rule (`src/db/horseRemoval.ts`, established 2026-08-04) is "a horse that never showed and never conceived is deleted outright; everything else is marked `removed` and kept". The colt had never shown and never conceived.
- **The cause.** `deletableHorseSql` carried ten clauses, three of which its own comment described as *"defensive rather than expected"*: `events`, `import_candidates`, `consignment_injections`. Two of those three are not defensive at all, they are **universal**. Every foal born in a player-owned stable gets a `foaled` event pointing at it in the same batch that inserts it (`buildFoaledEventStatement`, `src/db/events.ts`, called from `src/db/pregnancies.ts`), and every founding horse gets `import_candidates.horse_id` written when it is claimed (`claimFoundingOffer`, `src/db/founding.ts`). Between them they covered essentially the entire population a child would ever send to a pet home - home-bred and founding stock - so the delete path could only ever fire for a bought-in horse that had also never appeared in the feed. **The rule silently did not apply to the horses it was written for.**
- **The fix, and the operator's decision inside it.** Both clauses move from the keep-list to `buildDeleteHorseStatements` as `UPDATE ... SET <pointer> = NULL`, not as deletes. The operator chose "keep the words, drop the link": a birth notice in the feed still reads normally after the horse is gone, because `eventSentence` (`src/render/stables.ts`) renders every sentence out of the event's own `payload` JSON and never joins `horses` - `subject_horse_id` is used for exactly one thing, making the row a link, and that render already handled null by printing plain text. `import_candidates.horse_id` costs even less: it is written by one function and read by nothing.
- **`consignment_injections` stays a keep-clause, deliberately.** It is genuinely rare rather than universal (only a horse the operator seeded an allele into has one), and `applied_horse_id` is the only one of the three that is actually rendered anywhere - `/admin/consignment` prints "Applied (horse #N)" from it. That is the operator's own audit trail of a deliberate act, not a by-product of a horse being born. A test pins this asymmetry so it does not read as an oversight later.
- **The invariant in that file's header still holds and is still the reason to be careful.** `deletableHorseSql` is doing two jobs - it is the operator's rule *and* it is the proof that the delete cannot break a foreign key. Every one of the fifteen tables with a `REFERENCES horses (id)` column must appear in one of the two lists; `events` and `import_candidates` simply changed which list. If a future migration adds a sixteenth it belongs in one or the other, and the delete starts failing inside a batch if it is in neither.
- **A wording bug found alongside it.** `end_reason` was read by exactly one of the four screens that name an ended horse. The past-horses list said "Went to a pet home"; the barn list, the horse's own page and `/world` all said "Retired away" for the same horse on the same day. `removedHorseLabel(endReason)` (`src/render/horses.ts`) is now the single source all four call. `PublicHorseSource` (`src/routes/world.ts`) had to gain `end_reason` by name to make the fourth possible - that type's explicit named-field contract, and the test guarding it, is working exactly as slice 0016 §2.2 intended.

**Part B - the market would not sell a horse between two barns one child runs.**

- **The report.** Every listing showed "This is your own horse" with only a Withdraw button, from every one of the account's stables. Since CLAUDE.md §13 deliberately has **no** direct transfer between one owner's own stables - the market is the only route - the horse could not move at all.
- **The cause, and why it is the same shape as Part A.** `listingBuyRoute` has always allowed this: its §7.1 step 2 comment says so in as many words, its only ownership refusal is stable-scoped (`buyerStableId === listing.seller_stable_id`), `buyerOptionsFor` already excludes the selling stable, `sellListing` computes a `sameAccount` flag for exactly this case, and `test/market/sale-d1.test.ts` has been proving the sale works and stamps `ledger.same_account` since slice 0017. **The machinery was built, wired and tested; the page just never drew the form.** `listingPageRoute` computed `const buyerOptions = isMine ? [] : await buyerOptionsFor(...)`, letting one account-scoped flag answer a question it had no business answering.
- **The fix.** `buyerOptions` is now built for every viewer. `isMine` keeps its real job - may this account withdraw the listing? - and its one remaining suppression: a seller with no second stable gets no "you have no other stable that could buy this horse" refusal, because the withdraw card is the whole story there. Your own listing now renders the withdraw card *and*, when you have somewhere to move the horse to, a buy card underneath it, worded as a move between your own stables with the commission stated plainly.
- **This is a real sale on purpose, not a transfer.** Full price between the two balances, commission taken, one turn spent, `ledger.same_account = 1`. Slice 0017 §2.2 built that flag to make same-account trading *visible* rather than to forbid it, and the cost is what stops a child shuffling horses between barns for free.
- **This is the second instance of "a handler with no way to reach it" in two days** (slice 0020's `/horses/:id/treat` 404 was the first). `test/market/same-account-buy.test.ts` follows the precedent `test/router-paths.test.ts` set for that bug: it reads `src/routes/market.ts` off disk and asserts the page still offers what the handler accepts, rather than re-stating the rule in a copy that can drift. Added to `tsconfig.json`'s `exclude` list for the usual `node:fs` reason.

- Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean and `npm test` green (76 files, 697 tests, up from 691 - three new pet-home cases covering a home-bred foal, a founding horse and the injected-allele exception, plus the three market source-invariant checks). **Not** verified in a browser and **not** verified against real D1: nobody has watched a real foal vanish from a real barn, and the market's new buy card has not been looked at on a phone. Both need a deploy and one press each.

---

**2026-08-04 - An admin broom for a horse the delete rule should have cleared and didn't.**

- **Why it exists.** The Part A fix above changes what happens to horses that leave *from now on*; it does nothing about rows already marked `removed` under the broken rule. The operator, who has no terminal and no SQL console (CLAUDE.md §1), was left with horses the rule says should never have survived and no way from a browser to be rid of them. The only existing tools were `/admin/reset`, which empties every horse in the game, and waiting for the pending slice 0021 Part G world reset.
- **`POST /horses/:id/admin-delete`**, rendered as a card at the bottom of the horse page for an admin only. Three deliberate limits, all about not making it a bigger tool than the job: **admin only** (a player's route out is the pet home, which pays them - two paths that both end a horse where only one settles up would be a hazard if a child could reach either); **ended horses only** (an alive horse has money owed on its departure, which is `sellHorseToPetHome`'s job, so refusing here makes this route structurally incapable of skipping the ledger); and **the same deletability rule, not a stronger one** - it reuses `isHorseDeletable` unchanged, so a horse with foals, a pedigree hanging off it, a show record or a stud booking is refused exactly as at the pet home. That last one is not politeness: `deletableHorseSql` is also the proof the delete cannot break a foreign key, so an override here would turn a tidy-up button into a way to corrupt the database.
- **The card renders for an ended horse even when it is *not* deletable**, saying why not. "Why is there no button here" is the question the operator would otherwise be left with, and it is the same question that produced this whole day's work.
- **It writes no ledger row and pays nothing**, unlike both pet-home entry points. The horse already left and was already paid for at the time; this is clearing a row, not ending a horse.
- Lands on `/admin/horses?deleted=1` (the horse page it was pressed from no longer exists), which grew a one-line notice for it.
- `test/router-paths.test.ts` picked the new sub-path up automatically and checked `HORSE_ROUTE`'s regex against it - which is exactly what that file was written for after the `/treat` 404, and the first time it has done its job on a new route rather than a historical one.
- Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm test` green (76 files, 698 tests). **Not** pressed in a browser.
